---
type: manual
title: "Personal Lines Underwriting Manual"
---
# Personal Lines Underwriting Manual







## Rule 100 — Purpose, Authority and Use of This Manual

**100.A** Apply this Manual to personal lines underwriting activity. Use its direction for acceptance, referral, and file handling.

This Manual promotes consistent risk selection and handling. It supports carrier controls.

Document the underwriting action in the account record. State the Manual basis for the action.

**100.B** Treat this Manual as internal carrier direction. Do not provide its language to an applicant or insured.

Manual language is not intended for external use. External use can create inconsistent expectations.

Document any request for Manual content in the account record. Refer the request to underwriting management.

**100.C** Use this Manual within delegated underwriting authority. Refer risks outside delegated authority.

Delegated authority controls who may act for the carrier. It prevents unauthorized commitments.

Document the authority used for the decision. Record the referral outcome when referral applies.

**100.D** Do not use this Manual to alter coverage. Do not interpret Manual guidance as a coverage grant.

Underwriting direction governs risk handling. Coverage is determined through carrier-issued coverage terms.

Document any coverage concern in the account record. Refer the concern for appropriate review.

**100.E** Apply Manual direction before binding a risk. Do not bind a risk that requires referral.

Pre-bind review protects the carrier from unintended risk acceptance. It confirms that required controls are met.

Document completion of the pre-bind review. Record any referral decision before binding.

**100.F** Refer any risk that exceeds the binding authority limit. Do not divide the risk to avoid referral.

Authority limits control carrier exposure. Splitting risk information defeats the intended review.

Document the referral reason in the account record. Retain the underwriting decision.

**100.G** Apply the AOP deductible floor when evaluating deductible acceptability. Refer requests that do not meet carrier direction.

Deductible controls affect retention and pricing. Consistent handling supports portfolio discipline.

Document the deductible reviewed. Record the basis for any referral.

**100.H** Use current and reliable risk information. Do not rely on information that appears incomplete or conflicting.

Sound underwriting depends on dependable information. Unresolved conflicts can affect risk selection.

Document the information reviewed. Describe any unresolved concern and the action taken.

**100.I** Verify material risk characteristics before accepting a risk. Refer material uncertainty that cannot be resolved.

Material characteristics affect eligibility and pricing. Verification reduces avoidable underwriting error.

Document the verification completed. Record the source of material risk information.

**100.J** Evaluate the risk as presented. Do not assume favorable conditions that are not supported by the account record.

Assumptions can produce inaccurate risk selection. Underwriting decisions require supportable facts.

Document the facts relied upon. Identify any condition that remains unverified.

**100.K** Refer risks with adverse conditions that exceed normal underwriting tolerance. Do not disregard adverse conditions because favorable features are present.

Adverse conditions can affect loss potential. Referral allows broader review of the total risk.

Document the adverse condition. Record the referral disposition.

**100.L** Consider favorable risk characteristics when supported by reliable information. Do not use favorable characteristics to override mandatory referral direction.

Favorable characteristics can inform risk assessment. They do not remove carrier controls.

Document the favorable characteristic reviewed. Record any required referral.

**100.M** Apply eligibility direction consistently. Do not make exceptions without authorized approval.

Consistent eligibility handling supports fair and controlled underwriting. Exceptions require accountable review.

Document the eligibility decision. Record the approval for any authorized exception.

**100.N** Refer requests for exceptions to underwriting management. Do not represent that an exception will be approved.

Exception approval remains subject to carrier review. Premature representations can create avoidable disputes.

Document the exception request. Record the final disposition.

**100.O** Use underwriting judgment within Manual direction. Do not substitute personal preference for carrier standards.

Judgment supports assessment of facts not fully addressed by routine handling. Carrier standards remain controlling.

Document the judgment applied. State the risk facts that support the decision.

**100.P** Escalate unclear Manual direction before taking an underwriting action. Do not create local practice that conflicts with carrier controls.

Clarification preserves consistent application. Local practices can weaken underwriting discipline.

Document the clarification request. Record the direction received.

**100.Q** Treat material changes in risk information as requiring review. Do not rely on an earlier decision when material facts have changed.

Changed conditions can affect eligibility, pricing, or referral status. Review protects the integrity of the underwriting decision.

Document the changed information. Record the resulting underwriting action.

**100.R** Reassess a risk when reliable information conflicts with prior account information. Do not ignore discrepancies that could affect underwriting.

Conflicting information may indicate inaccurate risk presentation. Reassessment supports informed carrier action.

Document the discrepancy. Record how the discrepancy was resolved.

**100.S** Obtain required risk information before completing the underwriting decision. Refer accounts with missing material information.

Incomplete information limits reliable risk evaluation. Referral prevents unsupported acceptance.

Document the information obtained. Identify missing information and the referral action.

**100.T** Use carrier-approved sources for risk verification when available. Refer uncertainty about source reliability.

Reliable sources support consistent underwriting conclusions. Unreliable sources may distort risk assessment.

Document the source used. Record any concern about source reliability.

**100.U** Handle applicant and insured information for underwriting purposes only. Limit access to personnel with a business need.

Controlled handling protects sensitive carrier information. It also supports appropriate account administration.

Document access concerns in the account record. Refer suspected improper access for review.

**100.V** Record underwriting decisions clearly and contemporaneously. Do not leave the account record dependent on memory or informal communication.

Clear records support later review and consistent servicing. Informal communication may not establish the basis for action.

Document the decision and rationale. Identify the person taking the action.

**100.W** State the reason for each referral. Do not submit a referral without relevant risk facts.

Complete referrals allow efficient review. Vague referrals delay underwriting action.

Document the referral reason. Include material risk facts in the account record.

**100.X** Respond to underwriting referrals through authorized channels. Do not rely on unrecorded approval.

Authorized channels preserve accountability. Unrecorded approval cannot support carrier action.

Document the referral response. Retain the approval basis in the account record.

**100.Y** Apply approval conditions exactly as authorized. Refer any condition that cannot be satisfied.

Approval conditions control the carrier's acceptance of the risk. Unmet conditions can invalidate the underwriting basis.

Document each applicable condition. Record confirmation of compliance or referral.

**100.Z** Do not waive a required underwriting condition without authorized approval. Refer requests to waive a condition.

Conditions address identified risk concerns. Unauthorized waiver can increase carrier exposure.

Document the waiver request. Record the authorized disposition.

**100.AA** Distinguish underwriting requirements from service preferences. Apply required controls without treating optional practices as mandatory.

Clear distinction supports efficient account handling. It avoids unnecessary barriers to acceptable business.

Document required underwriting actions. Record optional service handling only when material to the decision.

**100.AB** Use consistent terminology in the account record. Do not use ambiguous wording for material underwriting conclusions.

Consistent terminology supports review and auditability. Ambiguous wording can obscure the carrier's decision.

Document conclusions in clear underwriting language. Identify the risk condition addressed.

**100.AC** Refer suspected misrepresentation or concealment. Do not attempt to resolve intentionality without authorized review.

Suspected misrepresentation can affect risk acceptance and carrier response. Referral preserves appropriate control.

Document the facts creating concern. Avoid conclusory statements about intent.

**100.AD** Treat unsupported statements as unverified information. Do not treat them as confirmed risk facts.

Underwriting decisions require reliable support. Unsupported statements may require further review.

Document the statement received. Record any verification action.

**100.AE** Evaluate protective features only when their presence and condition are supported. Do not credit a feature that cannot be verified.

Protective features may affect risk assessment. Unsupported credit can result in inaccurate underwriting.

Document the feature reviewed. Record the verification basis.

**100.AF** Refer risks with conditions that suggest heightened loss potential. Do not minimize conditions because loss history appears favorable.

Current conditions can affect future loss exposure. Loss history may not reflect present risk characteristics.

Document the condition observed. Record the referral outcome.

**100.AG** Apply loss information as part of the overall risk evaluation. Do not treat loss information as the sole underwriting factor unless Manual direction requires it.

Loss information can indicate risk trends or conditions. Broader risk facts remain relevant to sound selection.

Document the loss information reviewed. State the underwriting significance assigned.

**100.AH** Consider occupancy, maintenance, and condition when evaluating property risk. Refer material concerns that remain unresolved.

These characteristics can materially affect loss potential. Reliable evaluation requires sufficient support.

Document the characteristics reviewed. Record the basis for the conclusion.

**100.AI** Refer any risk involving a condition outside ordinary personal lines handling. Do not force unfamiliar risk features into routine processing.

Unusual conditions may require specialized review. Routine handling can overlook material exposure.

Document the unusual condition. Record the referral disposition.

**100.AJ** Treat an inspection report as current for 12 months after receipt. Refer the risk when the report cannot support the underwriting decision.

Inspection information supports evaluation of property condition. Stale or incomplete information can reduce reliability.

Document the inspection report reviewed. Record any referral based on inspection information.

**100.AK** Review inspection findings for material underwriting concerns. Do not disregard findings without a documented basis.

Inspection findings can identify conditions not otherwise visible in the account record. Review supports appropriate risk action.

Document the finding evaluated. State the disposition of the concern.

**100.AL** Require resolution of material inspection concerns before final underwriting action. Refer concerns that cannot be resolved through available information.

Material concerns may affect acceptability or conditions of acceptance. Unresolved concerns require controlled review.

Document the resolution obtained. Record any referral and resulting direction.

**100.AM** Do not alter inspection information in the account record. Preserve the underwriting significance of the information received.

Accurate records support reliable review. Altered information can compromise underwriting integrity.

Document any clarification separately. Identify the source of the clarification.

**100.AN** Evaluate requested changes for their effect on the risk. Refer changes that affect eligibility, authority, or underwriting conditions.

Requested changes can alter the carrier's exposure. Review prevents unintended continuation of unacceptable risk.

Document the requested change. Record the underwriting action taken.

**100.AO** Apply renewal underwriting direction to the risk facts available. Do not assume prior acceptance requires continued acceptance.

Renewal review remains an underwriting activity. Risk conditions and carrier appetite may change.

Document the renewal review. Record the basis for the decision.

**100.AP** Refer renewal risks with unresolved material concerns. Do not continue the risk without required authorization.

Unresolved concerns can affect the carrier's willingness to continue the risk. Referral ensures accountable action.

Document the unresolved concern. Record the referral outcome.

**100.AQ** Use prior account information as context, not as a substitute for current review. Verify material information when current conditions are uncertain.

Prior information may remain useful but may not reflect present conditions. Current review supports accurate risk selection.

Document the prior information considered. Record any current verification.

**100.AR** Do not make underwriting decisions based on prohibited considerations. Refer uncertainty about an appropriate underwriting factor.

Carrier decisions must rest on permitted risk characteristics. Uncertainty requires controlled guidance.

Document the factor considered. Record the guidance received when referral applies.

**100.AS** Apply carrier risk appetite as expressed through Manual direction. Do not accept a risk solely to meet production objectives.

Risk appetite guides sustainable portfolio management. Production objectives do not override underwriting controls.

Document the risk appetite basis for the decision. Record any required referral.

**100.AT** Maintain professional independence in underwriting decisions. Refer pressure that conflicts with Manual direction.

Undue influence can compromise carrier standards. Referral protects the integrity of the underwriting process.

Document the concern without speculation. Record the escalation action.

**100.AU** Communicate underwriting requirements accurately. Do not describe a requirement as optional when carrier direction makes it mandatory.

Accurate communication supports proper risk handling. Miscommunication can delay resolution or create incorrect expectations.

Document material communication in the account record. State the requirement communicated.

**100.AV** Do not promise an underwriting outcome before authorized review is complete. Communicate that acceptance remains subject to carrier underwriting.

Risk acceptance depends on review of applicable facts. Premature commitments can exceed authority.

Document any material representation concern. Refer the matter for review.

**100.AW** Preserve the account record so the underwriting decision can be understood. Do not remove material rationale after action is taken.

A complete record supports quality review and future servicing. Missing rationale can impair carrier oversight.

Document the decision rationale at the time of action. Record material updates clearly.

**100.AX** Follow this Manual unless authorized carrier direction requires a different action. Refer conflict between Manual direction and operational instruction.

Carrier authority must be applied consistently. Conflicting instruction requires resolution before action.

Document the conflict identified. Record the authorized direction followed.

## Rule 110 — Eligibility: HO-3 Special Form

**110.A** Require Coverage A at or above $150,000.

Adequate dwelling limits support stable risk selection.

Document the accepted Coverage A limit in the underwriting file.

**110.B** Refer Coverage A limits above $1,500,000.

Higher limits require review of valuation, construction, and exposure.

Document the referral outcome and underwriting authority.

**110.C** Refer risks assigned a protection class above 8.

Fire protection quality affects expected loss severity.

Document the protection class source and referral disposition.

**110.D** Accept only owner-occupied dwellings used as the insured's residence.

Occupancy affects maintenance, oversight, and loss reporting.

Document occupancy in the underwriting file.

**110.E** Refer dwellings occupied by tenants, roomers, or boarders.

Nonowner occupancy may create liability and maintenance concerns.

Document the occupancy arrangement and referral outcome.

**110.F** Refer dwellings held for sale when vacancy, neglect, or renovation is evident.

Sale activity may increase vacancy and reduce routine care.

Document sale status and observed conditions.

**110.G** Refer dwellings used for short-term lodging or transient occupancy.

Transient use increases turnover, liability, and property exposure.

Document the use of the dwelling and referral disposition.

**110.H** Refer dwellings with commercial activity conducted on the premises.

Business activity may introduce unmodeled liability and property hazards.

Document the business activity and underwriting decision.

**110.I** Refer dwellings used for manufacturing, repair, storage, or processing activity.

These operations may create elevated fire, theft, or liability exposure.

Document the activity, equipment, and referral outcome.

**110.J** Decline risks with unlawful occupancy or unlawful property use.

Unlawful activity creates unacceptable liability, property, and compliance exposure.

Document the basis for decline in the underwriting file.

**110.K** Require the dwelling to be maintained in sound condition.

Deferred maintenance increases the likelihood and severity of loss.

Document observed maintenance conditions and corrective requirements.

**110.L** Refer dwellings with visible roof deterioration, active leakage, or temporary repairs.

Roof condition is material to water and wind loss potential.

Document roof observations and underwriting disposition.

**110.M** Refer dwellings with unrepaired structural movement, settlement, or foundation distress.

Structural defects may indicate progressive damage or unsafe conditions.

Document the observed condition and available inspection evidence.

**110.N** Refer dwellings with damaged siding, exterior rot, or exposed wall openings.

Exterior deterioration may allow water intrusion and conceal broader damage.

Document exterior conditions and required corrective action.

**110.O** Refer dwellings with unrepaired storm, fire, water, or impact damage.

Existing damage must be separated from prospective insured loss.

Document the damage, repair status, and referral decision.

**110.P** Refer dwellings undergoing material renovation or reconstruction.

Construction activity changes the property's condition and loss exposure.

Document the scope of work and occupancy status.

**110.Q** Refer dwellings with construction materials or features that create unusual fire exposure.

Construction characteristics may affect ignition, spread, and repair severity.

Document the material features and referral outcome.

**110.R** Refer dwellings with obsolete, damaged, or unsafe electrical systems.

Electrical deficiencies may contribute to fire and equipment losses.

Document available electrical information and required action.

**110.S** Refer dwellings with fuse service, exposed wiring, or recurring electrical problems.

These conditions may indicate increased fire potential.

Document the condition and underwriting disposition.

**110.T** Refer dwellings with heating equipment that is damaged, improperly vented, or poorly maintained.

Heating defects may create fire, smoke, or carbon monoxide exposure.

Document the heating condition and referral outcome.

**110.U** Refer dwellings heated by solid-fuel equipment.

Solid-fuel heating requires review of installation, maintenance, and clearance conditions.

Document the equipment and available supporting evidence.

**110.V** Refer dwellings with fuel storage conditions that present fire or spill exposure.

Fuel storage may increase property damage and environmental liability potential.

Document the storage condition and underwriting decision.

**110.W** Refer dwellings with plumbing leaks, deteriorated piping, or repeated water damage.

Water loss patterns may indicate unresolved maintenance concerns.

Document the reported condition and repair status.

**110.X** Refer dwellings with sewer backup, drainage failure, or standing water conditions.

Drainage conditions may increase frequency and severity of water losses.

Document the condition and referral disposition.

**110.Y** Refer dwellings located where surface water, overflow, or drainage exposure is apparent.

Site conditions may create loss potential beyond ordinary residential exposure.

Document the observed exposure and underwriting action.

**110.Z** Refer dwellings with unrepaired mold, rot, or moisture damage.

Moisture conditions may indicate ongoing water intrusion or neglected maintenance.

Document the affected areas and remediation status.

**110.AA** Refer dwellings with swimming pools, spas, ponds, or similar water features.

Water features create elevated premises liability exposure.

Document the feature, safety controls, and referral outcome.

**110.AB** Refer dwellings with diving boards, slides, or unsafe pool access.

These features may increase bodily injury severity.

Document the feature and available safety measures.

**110.AC** Refer dwellings with trampolines or similar recreational equipment.

Recreational equipment may create heightened injury exposure.

Document the equipment and underwriting disposition.

**110.AD** Refer dwellings with aggressive, dangerous, or restricted animals.

Animal exposure may create significant bodily injury liability.

Document the animal information and referral outcome.

**110.AE** Refer dwellings with prior animal bite allegations or related liability concerns.

Prior allegations may indicate an unresolved premises liability exposure.

Document the reported history and underwriting action.

**110.AF** Refer dwellings with firearms activity, shooting areas, or related hazards on the premises.

Firearms activity may create elevated bodily injury exposure.

Document the activity and referral disposition.

**110.AG** Refer dwellings with unusual attractive nuisances or unsafe recreational features.

Attractive nuisances may increase the likelihood of injury claims.

Document the feature and required corrective action.

**110.AH** Refer dwellings with incomplete fencing around hazardous areas.

Access controls may be necessary to reduce premises liability exposure.

Document the condition and available safeguards.

**110.AI** Refer dwellings with unsecured vacant structures on the premises.

Vacant structures may attract trespass, vandalism, and injury exposure.

Document the structure condition and referral outcome.

**110.AJ** Refer dwellings with detached structures used for business, storage, or hazardous activity.

Appurtenant use may materially change the risk profile.

Document the structure use and underwriting decision.

**110.AK** Review the appurtenant structures percentage for consistency with the premises exposure.

Related structures require appropriate limit and use review.

Document the review in the underwriting file.

**110.AL** Refer premises with deteriorated fences, gates, walkways, or retaining features.

Poorly maintained premises features may create injury exposure.

Document the observed condition and required action.

**110.AM** Refer dwellings with steep access, unstable driveways, or unsafe stairs.

Access hazards may increase premises liability and property damage potential.

Document the access condition and referral disposition.

**110.AN** Refer dwellings with trees, limbs, or vegetation threatening the dwelling or service lines.

Vegetation hazards may contribute to wind, impact, or utility losses.

Document the hazard and corrective requirement.

**110.AO** Refer dwellings with brush, debris, or combustible material near the structure.

Combustible conditions may increase fire spread potential.

Document the observed condition and underwriting action.

**110.AP** Refer dwellings with inadequate access for emergency response.

Restricted access may affect fire suppression and loss severity.

Document the access condition and referral outcome.

**110.AQ** Require reliable utility service suitable for residential occupancy.

Utility conditions affect habitability and loss prevention.

Document any known utility concern in the underwriting file.

**110.AR** Refer dwellings served by private water or waste systems with known defects.

Private systems may create water damage, sanitation, or liability concerns.

Document the system condition and referral disposition.

**110.AS** Refer dwellings with known sinkhole, earth movement, or subsidence conditions.

Ground instability may create severe property damage exposure.

Document the reported condition and underwriting decision.

**110.AT** Refer dwellings with exposure to landslide, erosion, or unstable terrain.

Terrain conditions may affect structural integrity and access.

Document the site condition and available evidence.

**110.AU** Refer dwellings with known prior fire losses involving unresolved cause or repair issues.

Unresolved fire conditions may indicate continuing loss potential.

Document the loss information and referral outcome.

**110.AV** Refer dwellings with repeated water, theft, vandalism, or liability loss activity.

Adverse loss activity may indicate inadequate maintenance or risk control.

Document the loss information and underwriting action.

**110.AW** Refer risks with material discrepancies between submitted risk information and observed conditions.

Inconsistent information prevents reliable risk evaluation.

Document the discrepancy and resolution.

**110.AX** Decline risks when material underwriting information cannot be verified.

Unverified information prevents sound eligibility determination.

Document the unavailable information and decline basis.

**110.AY** Require disclosure of known property damage existing before inception.

Existing damage is not an eligible prospective loss exposure.

Document disclosed damage and repair status.

**110.AZ** Refer risks with open claims, disputed repairs, or unresolved liability allegations.

Open matters may affect ownership, condition, or loss potential.

Document the matter and referral disposition.

**110.BA** Require insurable interest by the named insured.

Insurable interest supports proper risk ownership and claim handling.

Document the basis for insurable interest.

**110.BB** Refer risks involving estates, trusts, entities, or complex ownership arrangements.

Complex ownership may require review of named insured and occupancy status.

Document the ownership arrangement and underwriting decision.

**110.BC** Refer dwellings occupied by persons other than the named insured.

Occupancy differences may alter maintenance and liability exposure.

Document the occupant relationship and referral outcome.

**110.BD** Refer dwellings used as a secondary residence.

Intermittent occupancy may delay discovery of damage or maintenance needs.

Document the occupancy pattern and underwriting disposition.

**110.BE** Refer dwellings left unattended when condition, access, or maintenance is uncertain.

Unattended property may sustain undetected water, fire, or vandalism damage.

Document the available oversight arrangements.

**110.BF** Refer dwellings with security deficiencies in areas presenting theft or vandalism concerns.

Security conditions may affect theft and vandalism loss potential.

Document the security condition and referral action.

**110.BG** Refer dwellings with prior cancellation, nonrenewal, or material coverage gaps.

Prior coverage concerns may identify adverse risk characteristics.

Document the reported coverage history and underwriting decision.

**110.BH** Refer risks with pending legal disputes involving the premises.

Legal disputes may affect control, occupancy, or liability exposure.

Document the dispute information and referral outcome.

**110.BI** Refer risks with known code violations, unsafe conditions, or governmental notices.

Official concerns may indicate material property or liability hazards.

Document the known condition and required resolution.

**110.BJ** Require reasonable market value support for the dwelling limit selected.

Valuation support promotes appropriate insurance-to-value alignment.

Document the valuation basis in the underwriting file.

**110.BK** Apply the eighty percent condition when valuation support is incomplete.

Valuation concerns require careful review before acceptance.

Document the valuation concern and underwriting disposition.

**110.BL** Refer dwellings with unique design, custom features, or unusual replacement characteristics.

Special features may require enhanced valuation review.

Document the feature and available valuation support.

**110.BM** Refer historic, landmarked, or architecturally significant dwellings.

Special construction and repair requirements may affect loss severity.

Document the designation and underwriting outcome.

**110.BN** Refer dwellings with unusual construction methods or nonstandard materials.

Nonstandard construction may affect durability, repair, and fire exposure.

Document the construction characteristics and referral disposition.

**110.BO** Refer dwellings with solar equipment, wind equipment, or similar energy systems.

Energy equipment may introduce roof, electrical, and liability exposure.

Document the equipment and underwriting decision.

**110.BP** Decline risks that do not meet carrier eligibility requirements.

Ineligible risks are not suitable for Special Form underwriting.

Document the decline basis in the underwriting file.

## Rule 120 — Eligibility: HO-5 Comprehensive Form

**120.A** Accept a risk only when Coverage A meets or exceeds $300,000. Decline risks submitted below that limit.

The form requires a property value consistent with comprehensive eligibility. Inadequate limits indicate unsuitable placement.

Record the accepted Coverage A limit in the underwriting file. Retain the valuation support reviewed.

**120.B** Accept a risk only when Coverage A does not exceed $2,500,000. Refer submissions above that limit.

Higher values may require placement outside this eligibility authority. Do not alter values to retain the risk.

Record the submitted Coverage A limit and the referral disposition. Retain supporting valuation information.

**120.C** Accept a risk only when the protection class does not exceed 6. Refer any risk outside that requirement.

Protection capability affects expected fire loss severity. Comprehensive eligibility requires acceptable protection.

Record the verified protection class in the underwriting file. Retain the source used for verification.

**120.D** Accept only owner-occupied dwellings used as the insured location. Refer any risk with uncertain occupancy.

Owner occupancy supports appropriate maintenance and loss control. Uncertain occupancy may increase loss exposure.

Document the occupancy represented by the applicant. Record the basis for acceptance or referral.

**120.E** Refer any dwelling used for rental activity. Do not accept a risk with undisclosed rental exposure.

Rental activity changes the occupancy and liability exposure. Comprehensive eligibility requires clear personal use.

Document the stated use of the dwelling. Record any rental information obtained.

**120.F** Refer any risk with short-term guest occupancy. Do not accept a dwelling marketed for transient use.

Transient occupancy can increase property damage and liability severity. It also complicates occupancy verification.

Document the applicant's statement regarding guest use. Record the referral outcome.

**120.G** Refer any vacant dwelling. Do not accept a risk awaiting occupancy.

Vacancy may delay discovery of damage and reduce maintenance oversight. Comprehensive eligibility requires active occupancy.

Document the vacancy condition and stated reason. Record the disposition.

**120.H** Refer any dwelling under construction. Do not accept a risk with unfinished structural work.

Construction activity creates changing values and elevated loss potential. The completed dwelling condition must be established.

Document the construction status described. Record the referral disposition.

**120.I** Refer any dwelling undergoing major renovation. Do not accept a risk with active remodeling exposure.

Major renovation can affect habitability, protection, and replacement value. The risk condition must be stable.

Document the scope of work reported. Record the basis for referral.

**120.J** Refer any dwelling with unrepaired damage. Do not accept a risk until the condition is resolved.

Existing damage may indicate an open loss exposure or inadequate maintenance. Comprehensive eligibility requires a sound property condition.

Document the damage described and its location. Record the disposition.

**120.K** Refer any dwelling with material roof deterioration. Do not accept a risk with an uncertain roof condition.

Roof condition is a significant indicator of water loss potential. Visible deterioration requires further review.

Document the roof condition and available supporting information. Record the referral result.

**120.L** Refer any dwelling with evidence of repeated water intrusion. Do not accept a risk with unresolved moisture conditions.

Recurring water damage may indicate a continuing source of loss. The cause must be understood before acceptance.

Document the reported water history and repairs. Record the underwriting action.

**120.M** Refer any dwelling with foundation movement or settlement concerns. Do not accept a risk with unresolved structural instability.

Structural movement may impair the dwelling and increase future loss severity. The condition requires specialized review.

Document the condition reported or observed. Record the referral disposition.

**120.N** Refer any dwelling with unsafe electrical conditions. Do not accept a risk with known electrical hazards.

Electrical defects can create a substantial fire exposure. The dwelling must present an acceptable safety condition.

Document the electrical condition and corrective action reported. Record the underwriting decision.

**120.O** Refer any dwelling with unsafe heating equipment. Do not accept a risk with known heating hazards.

Improper heating equipment may increase fire and carbon monoxide exposure. The condition requires review before acceptance.

Document the heating system information obtained. Record the referral outcome.

**120.P** Refer any dwelling with solid-fuel heating. Do not accept a risk unless the exposure is reviewed.

Solid-fuel heating may increase fire frequency and severity. Installation and maintenance information may be material.

Document the heating arrangement disclosed. Record the referral disposition.

**120.Q** Refer any dwelling with fuel storage concerns. Do not accept a risk with uncontrolled fuel exposure.

Fuel storage can increase fire and environmental loss potential. The location and condition must be evaluated.

Document the fuel type and storage condition reported. Record the underwriting action.

**120.R** Refer any dwelling with a business operation on the premises. Do not accept a risk with material commercial activity.

Business activity may alter property use and liability exposure. Personal lines eligibility requires a personal risk profile.

Document the business activity disclosed. Record the referral result.

**120.S** Refer any dwelling used for agricultural activity. Do not accept a risk with farming exposure.

Agricultural operations may create property, liability, and equipment exposures beyond personal eligibility. The use must be evaluated.

Document the agricultural activity described. Record the underwriting disposition.

**120.T** Refer any dwelling with livestock kept on the premises. Do not accept a risk with material animal exposure.

Livestock may increase premises liability and property damage potential. The exposure requires separate review.

Document the animals disclosed and their use. Record the referral outcome.

**120.U** Refer any dwelling with aggressive animal exposure. Do not accept a risk when animal control concerns are present.

Animal behavior and control history may materially affect liability severity. The exposure must be evaluated before acceptance.

Document the animal information provided. Record the underwriting action.

**120.V** Refer any dwelling with a swimming pool. Do not accept a pool exposure without review of protective features.

Swimming pools create elevated premises liability exposure. Safety controls must be verified.

Document the pool condition and safety features reported. Record the referral disposition.

**120.W** Refer any dwelling with a trampoline. Do not accept a risk with uncontrolled trampoline exposure.

Trampolines may increase bodily injury potential. The condition and supervision controls require review.

Document the trampoline exposure disclosed. Record the underwriting decision.

**120.X** Refer any dwelling with recreational equipment that creates material liability exposure. Do not accept the risk without review.

Recreational equipment may create hazards not contemplated by routine personal occupancy. The exposure must be identified.

Document the equipment described by the applicant. Record the referral result.

**120.Y** Refer any dwelling with a history of liability claims. Do not accept a risk with unresolved liability concerns.

Prior liability activity may indicate continuing premises or personal exposure. The underlying cause requires review.

Document the claim information obtained. Record the underwriting disposition.

**120.Z** Refer any dwelling with a history of water damage claims. Do not accept a risk with unexplained water losses.

Water loss history may indicate maintenance deficiencies or a recurring source of damage. The cause and repairs must be understood.

Document the reported water loss history. Record the corrective information reviewed.

**120.AA** Refer any dwelling with a history of fire claims. Do not accept a risk with unresolved fire loss concerns.

Prior fire loss may indicate hazardous conditions or inadequate protection. The cause and repairs require review.

Document the fire loss information obtained. Record the referral outcome.

**120.AB** Refer any dwelling with a history of theft claims. Do not accept a risk with unexplained theft exposure.

Theft history may indicate inadequate security or elevated property exposure. The circumstances require review.

Document the theft history reported. Record the underwriting action.

**120.AC** Refer any dwelling with a history of weather-related claims. Do not accept a risk with unresolved damage conditions.

Weather losses may reveal construction weaknesses or deferred maintenance. The current condition must be established.

Document the weather loss history and repairs. Record the referral disposition.

**120.AD** Refer any dwelling with open insurance claims. Do not accept a risk until open claim conditions are resolved.

Open claims may affect property condition and loss valuation. The current exposure must be clear.

Document the open claim status reported. Record the underwriting decision.

**120.AE** Refer any applicant with material misrepresentation concerns. Do not accept a risk when submitted information cannot be relied upon.

Accurate underwriting depends on complete and consistent risk information. Unreliable information prevents proper eligibility review.

Document the discrepancy identified. Record the referral result.

**120.AF** Refer any risk with an undisclosed prior cancellation or nonrenewal. Do not accept a risk until the reason is evaluated.

Prior underwriting action may identify material eligibility concerns. The underlying facts must be reviewed.

Document the prior insurance action disclosed. Record the disposition.

**120.AG** Refer any risk with an unexplained lapse in coverage. Do not accept a risk until the reason is understood.

A lapse may indicate prior ineligibility, nonpayment, or an uninsured exposure. The reason must be documented.

Document the lapse explanation obtained. Record the underwriting action.

**120.AH** Refer any risk with unresolved prior carrier concerns. Do not accept a risk with incomplete adverse information.

Prior carrier concerns may identify hazards not evident from the submission. The information requires evaluation.

Document the concern reported or discovered. Record the referral disposition.

**120.AI** Refer any dwelling with security features in disrepair. Do not accept a risk with known protection deficiencies.

Inoperative security features may increase theft loss potential. The dwelling must have reliable protective conditions.

Document the condition of the reported security features. Record the underwriting decision.

**120.AJ** Refer any dwelling with a monitored alarm that is not active. Do not accept inactive alarm protection as a risk control.

Inactive monitoring does not provide the represented loss prevention benefit. The current operating status must be verified.

Document the alarm status reported. Record the referral outcome.

**120.AK** Refer any dwelling with a protective device that cannot be verified. Do not grant eligibility based on unverified controls.

Protective devices affect risk evaluation only when their condition is established. Unverified controls require further review.

Document the verification source reviewed. Record the underwriting action.

**120.AL** Refer any dwelling with poor exterior maintenance. Do not accept a risk with material upkeep concerns.

Exterior maintenance may indicate broader property condition issues. Comprehensive eligibility requires satisfactory upkeep.

Document the conditions identified. Record the referral disposition.

**120.AM** Refer any dwelling with deferred plumbing repairs. Do not accept a risk with known plumbing deficiencies.

Plumbing defects can result in significant water damage. Repairs must be addressed before acceptance.

Document the plumbing condition reported. Record the underwriting decision.

**120.AN** Refer any dwelling with sewer backup concerns. Do not accept a risk with an unresolved drainage condition.

Sewer backup exposure may produce recurring property damage. The source and corrective action require review.

Document the reported condition and repairs. Record the referral outcome.

**120.AO** Refer any dwelling with drainage concerns near the structure. Do not accept a risk with uncontrolled surface water exposure.

Poor drainage may contribute to water intrusion and foundation damage. The condition must be evaluated.

Document the drainage condition disclosed. Record the underwriting action.

**120.AP** Refer any dwelling with trees posing a hazard to the structure. Do not accept a risk with unaddressed falling-object exposure.

Hazardous trees may create substantial property damage potential. The condition requires resolution or review.

Document the tree condition described. Record the referral disposition.

**120.AQ** Refer any dwelling with unsafe stairs, railings, or walkways. Do not accept a risk with known premises hazards.

Unsafe access conditions may increase bodily injury exposure. The premises must be reasonably maintained.

Document the condition identified. Record the underwriting decision.

**120.AR** Refer any dwelling with an unfenced pool where fencing is expected. Do not accept a risk with inadequate pool controls.

Pool barriers are material to premises liability evaluation. Missing controls require review.

Document the barrier condition reported. Record the referral outcome.

**120.AS** Refer any dwelling with a dock or waterfront structure in poor condition. Do not accept a risk with unsafe water access.

Waterfront structures may create elevated injury and property damage exposure. Their condition must be evaluated.

Document the structure condition disclosed. Record the underwriting action.

**120.AT** Refer any dwelling with a private road access concern. Do not accept a risk with unreliable emergency access.

Emergency access may affect protection response and loss severity. The access condition requires review.

Document the access condition reported. Record the referral disposition.

**120.AU** Refer any dwelling with an address that cannot be verified. Do not accept a risk until the location is confirmed.

Accurate location information is necessary for underwriting evaluation. Unverified location data prevents reliable risk assessment.

Document the location verification obtained. Record the underwriting decision.

**120.AV** Refer any dwelling with inconsistent construction information. Do not accept a risk until the property description is resolved.

Construction details affect valuation and hazard assessment. Inconsistent information requires clarification.

Document the conflicting information identified. Record the referral outcome.

**120.AW** Refer any dwelling with a replacement cost estimate that appears unreliable. Do not accept a risk with unsupported valuation.

Reliable valuation is necessary for appropriate property protection. Unsupported estimates require further review.

Document the valuation concern identified. Record the underwriting action.

**120.AX** Refer any risk when the contents limit percentage does not align with the dwelling exposure. Do not adjust the limit without support.

Contents protection must reflect the personal property exposure presented. An unsuitable relationship may indicate incomplete underwriting information.

Document the contents information reviewed. Record the basis for the underwriting decision.

**120.AY** Refer any risk when the jewelry theft sublimit does not align with disclosed property exposure. Do not rely on incomplete personal property information.

Valuable property exposures may require further underwriting consideration. The disclosed ownership information must be evaluated.

Document the valuable property information obtained. Record the referral disposition.

**120.AZ** Refer any dwelling with scheduled personal property that suggests material exposure concerns. Do not accept the risk without evaluating the disclosed property.

Scheduled property may indicate theft, transit, or valuation concerns beyond routine eligibility. The exposure must be understood.

Document the property information reviewed. Record the underwriting decision.

**120.BA** Refer any dwelling with valuable collections stored at the insured location. Do not accept a risk with unclear storage or protection conditions.

Collections may create elevated theft and valuation exposure. Storage conditions may be material to eligibility.

Document the collection information disclosed. Record the referral outcome.

**120.BB** Refer any dwelling with firearms stored at the insured location. Do not accept a risk with unsafe storage concerns.

Firearms may create theft and liability exposure. Secure storage information may be material.

Document the storage information reported. Record the underwriting action.

**120.BC** Refer any dwelling with a loss-producing condition identified during inspection. Do not accept the risk until the condition is evaluated.

Inspection findings may reveal hazards not disclosed in the submission. Material conditions require underwriting review.

Document the inspection finding. Record the referral disposition.

**120.BD** Refer any risk with incomplete underwriting information. Do not accept a risk supported by assumptions.

Incomplete information prevents reliable eligibility determination. The submission must support the underwriting decision.

Document the missing information requested. Record the final disposition.

**120.BE** Refer any risk with conflicting applicant statements. Do not accept a risk until material conflicts are resolved.

Conflicting statements may indicate inaccurate risk information. Eligibility requires a consistent account of the exposure.

Document the conflicting statements identified. Record the underwriting decision.

**120.BF** Decline any risk that does not satisfy comprehensive form eligibility. Refer borderline risks for underwriting authority review.

Eligibility decisions must reflect the complete risk presentation. Do not accept a risk merely to accommodate requested coverage.

Document the final eligibility determination. Record the facts supporting acceptance, referral, or decline.

## Rule 130 — Eligibility: HO-4 Contents Broad Form

**130.A** Accept only a tenant-occupied risk used as a private residence. Refer any occupancy that is transient, incidental to business, or otherwise unsuitable for tenant contents coverage.

This form is intended for personal property of a resident tenant. Nonresidential activity can create exposures outside the intended underwriting appetite.

Document the occupancy description, tenant status, and any incidental use in the underwriting file.

**130.B** Confirm that the applicant has an insurable interest in the contents at the described location. Do not accept a risk where ownership or responsibility for the contents is unclear.

Contents coverage must respond to property for which the insured has a legitimate financial interest. Unclear ownership can create claim handling disputes.

Document the basis for the applicant's interest in the contents and residence premises.

**130.C** Refer any risk with a protection class above 9.

Protection class indicates the expected availability of public fire protection. Adverse protection can materially affect fire severity.

Document the reported protection class and the source used to verify it.

**130.D** Confirm that the residence premises has a complete mailing address. Refer any risk where the location cannot be identified for inspection, rating, or loss response.

A verified location supports accurate territory assignment and claim response. Incomplete location information can prevent reliable underwriting review.

Document the full residence address and any location details needed to identify the premises.

**130.E** Decline a risk where the residence premises is vacant. Refer any risk where occupancy is uncertain or expected to end.

Vacancy increases the likelihood that a loss will go undetected. Vacancy can also increase the severity of water, fire, and vandalism losses.

Document the current occupancy status and any known plans affecting continued residence.

**130.F** Refer any risk where the applicant does not occupy the residence premises as a primary residence. Do not bind where the stated use is inconsistent with tenant occupancy.

The form is intended for resident tenants and their personal property. Secondary, seasonal, or intermittent use may require separate underwriting review.

Document the applicant's stated use of the premises and any occupancy concerns.

**130.G** Decline a risk where the premises is used for unlawful activity. Refer any risk where available information suggests material misrepresentation of occupancy or use.

Unlawful activity can create a substantial increase in frequency and severity. Material misrepresentation prevents reliable risk selection.

Document the information reviewed, the underwriting concern, and the disposition.

**130.H** Refer any risk involving a rooming arrangement, boarding arrangement, or shared occupancy not clearly disclosed. Do not assume that all occupants have the same insurable interest.

Shared living arrangements can complicate ownership, residency, and claim payment. Undisclosed occupants may indicate a use outside underwriting appetite.

Document the disclosed occupants, their relationship to the applicant, and the ownership arrangement for contents.

**130.I** Refer any risk where the applicant sublets any portion of the residence premises. Decline where the applicant does not maintain the required tenant occupancy.

Subletting can change the character of the occupancy and introduce unfamiliar occupants. It can also affect access, maintenance, and loss reporting.

Document the subletting arrangement and the applicant's continued residence at the premises.

**130.J** Refer any risk with business property, business visitors, or business operations at the residence premises. Decline where the business exposure is material to the premises use.

Business activity can introduce liability, property, and traffic exposures not contemplated for a personal tenant risk. The nature of the activity must be understood before acceptance.

Document the business description, property involved, visitor activity, and underwriting decision.

**130.K** Refer any risk involving care provided for unrelated persons at the residence premises. Decline where the care activity changes the residential character of the occupancy.

Care activity can create increased liability and property exposures. It can also involve regular visitors, specialized equipment, or supervision obligations.

Document the care activity, the persons involved, and the basis for the underwriting disposition.

**130.L** Refer any risk involving animals with a history of biting, attacking, or aggressive behavior. Decline where the animal exposure is unacceptable under carrier appetite.

Animal liability severity can be significant and may not be apparent from the property characteristics. Prior behavior is a material underwriting consideration.

Document the animal type, reported history, available records, and underwriting action.

**130.M** Refer any risk where the applicant reports a prior cancellation, nonrenewal, or declination by another carrier. Do not accept without determining the reason for the prior action.

Prior carrier action may identify unresolved property, liability, or eligibility concerns. The reason must be evaluated rather than assumed.

Document the prior carrier action, the stated reason, and the underwriting conclusion.

**130.N** Refer any risk with loss history indicating recurring water, fire, theft, liability, or vandalism concerns. Decline where the loss pattern is inconsistent with carrier appetite.

Recurring loss activity can indicate an unresolved condition, inadequate maintenance, or unsuitable occupancy. Loss history must be considered with the current condition of the risk.

Document the reported loss information, corrective actions, and underwriting rationale.

**130.O** Require a satisfactory explanation for any prior loss that remains open, disputed, or unresolved. Refer the risk where the reported facts cannot be reconciled.

Open or disputed losses can create uncertainty regarding existing damage or potential claim activity. Underwriting must understand whether a condition remains at the premises.

Document the loss status, explanation received, and any supporting information reviewed.

**130.P** Refer any risk where the applicant cannot provide a clear description of the contents exposure. Do not bind where the amount or nature of property appears inconsistent with tenant occupancy.

A reliable description of personal property supports appropriate risk selection. Unusual property concentrations may indicate a business, collection, or other exposure requiring review.

Document the applicant's description of contents and any material property characteristics.

**130.Q** Refer any risk involving property held for sale, storage, repair, or delivery. Decline where the property is associated with commercial activity or another person's business.

Property connected to commercial activity may have values, ownership interests, and loss causes outside personal lines appetite. The intended use of the property must be clear.

Document the property description, ownership, intended use, and underwriting determination.

**130.R** Refer any risk involving unusual valuables, collections, or articles requiring specialized handling. Do not represent that this form provides coverage beyond its intended scope.

Special property may require different valuation, documentation, or underwriting treatment. The presence of such property can affect claim expectations.

Document the property description and any coverage discussion recorded during underwriting.

**130.S** Review the Coverage D limit as a percentage of Coverage A for consistency with the residence premises and requested coverage. Refer any risk where the requested relationship appears unsuitable.

The requested limit relationship can indicate whether the coverage request reflects the actual tenant exposure. An unusual relationship may require clarification before acceptance.

Document the requested limits, the applicant's explanation, and the underwriting conclusion.

**130.T** Review the association assessment limit when the residence premises is subject to an association. Refer any risk where the association arrangement or assessment exposure is unclear.

Association responsibilities can create claim questions for tenant occupants. The underwriting file must reflect the nature of the association interest.

Document the association name, occupancy arrangement, and assessment exposure information available.

**130.U** Refer any condominium, cooperative, or association-managed risk where the applicant's tenancy is not confirmed. Do not assume that association rules permit the stated occupancy.

Association restrictions can affect occupancy, maintenance, and loss response. Tenant eligibility depends on the applicant's actual right to occupy the premises.

Document the tenancy arrangement and any association information provided.

**130.V** Refer any risk where the building appears to be under construction, renovation, or major repair. Decline where the premises is not suitable for normal tenant occupancy.

Construction activity can increase fire, theft, water, and premises liability exposures. It can also indicate that the applicant is not living at the location as represented.

Document the construction condition, expected occupancy status, and underwriting action.

**130.W** Refer any risk where the residence premises has visible unrepaired damage. Decline where the condition suggests an existing loss, unsafe premises, or inadequate maintenance.

Existing damage can lead to disputes over loss timing and cause. Poor maintenance may also increase the chance of future loss.

Document the reported condition, available images or inspection information, and disposition.

**130.X** Refer any risk with evidence of water intrusion, mold, plumbing leakage, or recurring dampness. Do not bind until the condition and corrective action are understood.

Water conditions can produce ongoing damage and difficult causation questions. Recurring moisture may indicate a maintenance issue or an unrepaired source of loss.

Document the condition reported, corrective action, and source of verification.

**130.Y** Refer any risk where heating, electrical, plumbing, or cooking facilities appear unsafe or inadequate. Decline where the premises condition presents an unacceptable fire or water exposure.

Core building systems can materially affect property loss potential. Unsafe systems may also affect the tenant's ability to maintain normal occupancy.

Document the reported system condition and any corrective action required before acceptance.

**130.Z** Refer any risk involving solid-fuel heating, portable heating devices, or other unusual heat sources. Decline where the heating arrangement is unsafe or unsupported by reliable information.

Unusual heat sources can increase fire frequency and severity. The underwriting file must establish the nature and condition of the heating exposure.

Document the heat source, location, maintenance information, and underwriting decision.

**130.AA** Refer any risk where the residence premises has restricted emergency access. Decline where access limitations could materially impair fire response or claim handling.

Access limitations can delay emergency services and increase loss severity. They can also affect inspection and post-loss investigation.

Document the access condition and the information used to evaluate it.

**130.AB** Refer any risk located in an area with known brush, wildfire, flood, coastal, or other catastrophe exposure. Apply carrier appetite and underwriting controls before binding.

Catastrophe exposure can affect accumulation, availability, and loss severity. The residence location must be evaluated using reliable location information.

Document the catastrophe exposure considered and the underwriting decision.

**130.AC** Refer any risk where the premises has security concerns, repeated theft activity, or inadequate protection for tenant contents. Decline where the theft exposure is unacceptable.

Theft exposure is influenced by access, neighborhood conditions, and the ability to secure contents. Repeated theft activity can indicate an unresolved condition.

Document the reported security features, theft history, and underwriting conclusion.

**130.AD** Refer any risk where the applicant requests coverage to begin before underwriting information is complete. Do not bind until material eligibility information has been obtained.

Incomplete information prevents reliable selection and can create errors in rating or coverage setup. Material facts must be known before acceptance.

Document outstanding information, the applicant communication, and the final binding decision.

**130.AE** Require the applicant to provide accurate information regarding identity, residence, occupancy, and prior insurance. Refer any discrepancy that cannot be resolved.

Accurate applicant information is necessary for eligibility, rating, and claim handling. Unresolved discrepancies may indicate a material underwriting concern.

Document the discrepancy, information obtained to resolve it, and the disposition.

**130.AF** Refer any risk where the named insured is not the resident tenant or lacks responsibility for the contents. Do not add a person solely to accommodate an ineligible occupancy.

The named insured must have a clear connection to the covered contents and residence premises. Improper naming can create ownership and claim payment disputes.

Document the relationship of each named insured to the premises and contents.

**130.AG** Refer any risk involving a trust, estate, corporation, partnership, or other nonindividual ownership interest. Decline where the ownership structure is incompatible with personal tenant underwriting.

Nonindividual interests can create complexity in insurable interest, occupancy, and claim payment. Personal lines eligibility requires a clear resident tenant relationship.

Document the ownership structure and the underwriting determination.

**130.AH** Refer any risk where underwriting information conflicts with inspection findings, public information, or applicant statements. Do not rely on unsupported explanations for material inconsistencies.

Conflicting information can indicate an inaccurate description of the risk. Material differences must be resolved before acceptance.

Document the conflicting information, clarification obtained, and final underwriting action.

**130.AI** Decline a risk where fraud, concealment, or intentional misrepresentation is suspected. Refer the matter through carrier procedures before communicating a final adverse action.

Fraud indicators can affect eligibility, claim integrity, and carrier exposure. Suspected misrepresentation requires controlled handling and complete documentation.

Document the facts supporting the concern, the information reviewed, and the referral action taken.

**130.AJ** Bind only risks that meet applicable eligibility requirements and present an acceptable overall exposure. Refer any risk that does not fit the carrier's intended tenant contents appetite.

Eligibility depends on the combined effect of occupancy, location, condition, loss history, and applicant information. Acceptance must reflect a reasoned underwriting decision.

Document the final eligibility review, material facts considered, and approval or referral outcome.

## Rule 140 — Eligibility: HO-6 Unit-Owners Form

**140.A** Accept Coverage A only when the written limit is at least $5,000 and does not exceed $250,000.

This range supports unit-owner exposures within the carrier’s intended appetite.

Document the selected Coverage A limit and the source used to support it.

**140.B** Confirm that the applicant has an insurable interest in the described condominium unit.

Unit-owner coverage requires a valid ownership interest in the insured location.

Document the ownership interest from the application and available underwriting evidence.

**140.C** Accept only a condominium unit used as a private residence by the named insured.

The form is intended for personal residential ownership exposures.

Document the stated occupancy and the named insured’s relationship to the unit.

**140.D** Refer any unit occupied primarily for business activity.

Business activity can change the character and severity of the exposure.

Document the business activity, client traffic, employee presence, and carrier referral result.

**140.E** Refer any unit used for transient rental activity.

Transient occupancy can create occupancy conditions outside the carrier’s intended appetite.

Document the rental activity and the carrier referral result.

**140.F** Refer any unit occupied by persons other than the named insured when the applicant retains no regular residential use.

Nonresident ownership may require underwriting beyond standard unit-owner eligibility.

Document the occupancy arrangement and the carrier referral result.

**140.G** Decline any unit used primarily as lodging or guest accommodation.

Lodging use creates a commercial or transient occupancy exposure.

Document the use of the unit and the declination basis.

**140.H** Refer any unit with regular public access unrelated to ordinary residential use.

Regular public access may increase liability and property exposure.

Document the source and purpose of public access and the carrier referral result.

**140.I** Verify that the insured location is a legally established condominium unit.

The form requires a unit ownership interest within a condominium property regime.

Document the condominium status from reliable underwriting evidence.

**140.J** Refer any cooperative, stock ownership, leasehold ownership, or similar interest that is not a condominium unit.

Those ownership arrangements may require a different eligibility review.

Document the ownership structure and the carrier referral result.

**140.K** Confirm that the unit is within a completed residential structure.

Construction conditions can create unanticipated property and liability exposure.

Document the completion status and the source of verification.

**140.L** Refer any unit undergoing substantial renovation, reconstruction, or structural alteration.

Material work can affect valuation, occupancy, and loss susceptibility.

Document the scope of work, occupancy status, and carrier referral result.

**140.M** Decline any unit under active construction without regular residential occupancy.

Construction-only exposure is outside the intended unit-owner appetite.

Document the construction status and the declination basis.

**140.N** Refer any unit with unrepaired damage from fire, water, wind, collapse, or other loss.

Unrepaired damage can indicate an unresolved hazard or inaccurate valuation.

Document the damage, repair status, and carrier referral result.

**140.O** Refer any unit with known unsafe conditions affecting habitability or access.

Unsafe conditions may create a heightened property or liability exposure.

Document the condition, corrective action, and carrier referral result.

**140.P** Confirm that the condominium association maintains master property coverage appropriate to the property.

Association coverage affects the unit-owner’s retained building responsibility.

Document available association insurance information and underwriting findings.

**140.Q** Refer any risk when the association’s master coverage is unavailable, disputed, or materially limited.

Uncertain master coverage can leave unanticipated building exposure with the unit owner.

Document the coverage uncertainty and the carrier referral result.

**140.R** Determine the unit-owner’s responsibility for building items under the governing condominium documents.

The Coverage A selection must reflect property the unit owner is responsible to repair or replace.

Document the applicable ownership and repair responsibility information.

**140.S** Apply the condominium dwelling default limit when reliable evidence does not support a different Coverage A selection.

The default supports consistent handling when unit-specific valuation evidence is limited.

Document the reason for using the condominium dwelling default limit.

**140.T** Refer any risk when the requested Coverage A limit appears inconsistent with the unit-owner’s repair responsibility.

An unsupported limit can produce inadequate or excessive building coverage.

Document the valuation concern and the carrier referral result.

**140.U** Confirm that the unit is not located in a property subject to unresolved building code enforcement.

Open enforcement matters can indicate physical hazards or incomplete repairs.

Document the enforcement information and the underwriting disposition.

**140.V** Refer any unit with known structural movement, settlement, subsidence, or foundation distress.

These conditions can indicate substantial repair exposure beyond ordinary maintenance.

Document the reported condition and the carrier referral result.

**140.W** Refer any unit with known water intrusion, chronic leakage, or repeated moisture damage.

Recurring moisture conditions can increase the likelihood of future property loss.

Document the source, repair status, and carrier referral result.

**140.X** Refer any unit with known mold, fungi, rot, or decay affecting building materials.

Deterioration can indicate hidden damage and continuing moisture conditions.

Document the affected area, remediation status, and carrier referral result.

**140.Y** Refer any unit with electrical, heating, plumbing, or mechanical conditions that appear unsafe.

Unsafe building systems can create fire, water, or liability exposure.

Document the reported condition and the carrier referral result.

**140.Z** Refer any unit with a fuel-burning appliance lacking customary safety features or maintenance evidence.

Fuel-burning equipment may create elevated fire or carbon monoxide exposure.

Document the equipment condition and the carrier referral result.

**140.AA** Refer any unit with an in-unit solid-fuel heating appliance.

Solid-fuel heating may present a fire exposure requiring individual review.

Document the appliance type, installation information, and carrier referral result.

**140.AB** Refer any unit with a known prior fire loss involving the insured location.

Prior fire damage may indicate unresolved repairs, hazardous conditions, or loss recurrence concerns.

Document the loss details, repair status, and carrier referral result.

**140.AC** Refer any unit with known prior water damage that has not been fully repaired.

Unresolved water damage can contribute to recurring loss activity.

Document the damage history, remediation status, and carrier referral result.

**140.AD** Refer any applicant with material prior property losses associated with condominium ownership.

Prior losses may indicate maintenance, occupancy, or hazard concerns requiring review.

Document the loss information and the carrier referral result.

**140.AE** Refer any applicant with undisclosed prior cancellations, nonrenewals, or declinations.

Prior underwriting action may identify conditions material to eligibility.

Document the prior action, stated reason, and carrier referral result.

**140.AF** Decline any risk involving material misrepresentation or concealment.

Accurate underwriting information is required to evaluate eligibility.

Document the conflicting information and the declination basis.

**140.AG** Refer any risk when the applicant cannot provide information needed to establish eligibility.

Incomplete information prevents a reliable assessment of the exposure.

Document the missing information and the carrier referral result.

**140.AH** Confirm that the unit address identifies the insured location without ambiguity.

Accurate location information is necessary for underwriting, inspection, and claims handling.

Document the address information used for the eligibility review.

**140.AI** Refer any risk with conflicting unit identification, ownership information, or occupancy information.

Conflicting information can indicate an improperly described or misunderstood exposure.

Document the discrepancy and the carrier referral result.

**140.AJ** Refer any unit located in a building with known substantial vacancy.

Substantial vacancy may indicate maintenance, security, or financial concerns affecting the property.

Document the reported vacancy condition and the carrier referral result.

**140.AK** Refer any unit located in a building with known unresolved structural repairs.

Building-level repairs can affect access, habitability, and property loss exposure.

Document the repair condition and the carrier referral result.

**140.AL** Refer any unit located in a building with known repeated water losses.

Repeated building losses may indicate a continuing plumbing, roof, drainage, or maintenance issue.

Document the reported loss pattern and the carrier referral result.

**140.AM** Refer any unit located in a building with known fire protection impairments.

Impaired fire protection can increase expected loss severity.

Document the impairment and the carrier referral result.

**140.AN** Refer any unit located in a building with restricted emergency access.

Restricted access can affect emergency response and loss mitigation.

Document the access condition and the carrier referral result.

**140.AO** Refer any unit where the condominium association is subject to material litigation affecting property operations.

Association litigation may indicate unresolved governance, maintenance, or financial issues.

Document the litigation information and the carrier referral result.

**140.AP** Refer any unit where the condominium association reports material financial distress.

Association financial distress may delay repairs or reduce building maintenance.

Document the financial information and the carrier referral result.

**140.AQ** Refer any unit where association assessments relate to known property damage or building repair concerns.

Assessment activity can indicate loss conditions affecting the insured location.

Document the assessment purpose and the carrier referral result.

**140.AR** Confirm that the requested assessment coverage limit is consistent with the applicant’s stated exposure.

Assessment coverage should reflect the unit owner’s potential responsibility under the condominium arrangement.

Document the assessment coverage limit and the supporting underwriting information.

**140.AS** Refer any risk where the applicant seeks coverage for property owned by the condominium association.

Association-owned property is not automatically the unit owner’s insurable property.

Document the property ownership concern and the carrier referral result.

**140.AT** Refer any risk where the applicant seeks coverage for property owned by another unit owner.

Property owned by others may require a different coverage analysis.

Document the ownership concern and the carrier referral result.

**140.AU** Confirm that personal property is associated with the insured unit-owner household.

The form is intended to protect personal residential property of the insured household.

Document the applicant’s stated ownership and use of the personal property.

**140.AV** Refer any risk involving unusual classes of personal property requiring specialized valuation or handling.

Specialized property may exceed ordinary personal lines underwriting assumptions.

Document the property description and the carrier referral result.

**140.AW** Refer any unit containing hazardous materials not associated with ordinary household use.

Hazardous materials can create property, liability, and remediation exposure.

Document the material, storage condition, and carrier referral result.

**140.AX** Refer any unit used to store inventory, equipment, or supplies for a business.

Business property storage may indicate commercial activity or increased fire exposure.

Document the property stored and the carrier referral result.

**140.AY** Refer any unit with regular package, courier, or service activity beyond ordinary household use.

Unusual delivery activity may indicate undisclosed business or lodging use.

Document the activity and the carrier referral result.

**140.AZ** Refer any risk involving unlawful activity at the insured location.

Unlawful activity creates unacceptable property and liability exposure.

Document the available information and the carrier referral result.

**140.BA** Decline any risk where the insured location is materially misdescribed.

A materially inaccurate description prevents proper underwriting evaluation.

Document the inaccurate information and the declination basis.

**140.BB** Record the final eligibility decision before issuing or changing coverage.

A clear underwriting record supports consistent selection, referral, and declination handling.

Document the eligibility decision, supporting facts, and any required underwriting approval.

## Rule 150 — Eligibility: DP-3 Dwelling Property

**150.A** Accept only a dwelling with lawful residential occupancy. Refer any risk with occupancy that is unclear or inconsistent with the application.

Lawful residential occupancy supports stable hazard evaluation. Unclear occupancy can conceal a material change in use.

Document the reported occupancy and the source of verification. Retain occupancy support in the underwriting file.

**150.B** Require the applicant to have an insurable interest in the dwelling. Refer any risk where ownership, responsibility, or authority to insure is uncertain.

An insurable interest aligns the applicant with the property exposure. Unresolved ownership can impair claim handling.

Document the ownership interest stated by the applicant. Retain available title, lease, estate, or trust support.
