**150.C** Refer any dwelling located where lawful placement, access, or residential use is in doubt. Do not bind where governmental action may prevent normal occupancy.

Legal or access restrictions can materially affect property condition and repairability. Such conditions may also delay claim resolution.

Document the reported location conditions and any referral findings. Retain available correspondence or public-record support.

**150.D** Accept only a dwelling of sound construction and reasonably serviceable condition. Refer any risk with structural distress, visible deterioration, or incomplete exterior protection.

Construction condition affects the likelihood and severity of property damage. Material defects may indicate deferred maintenance or hidden damage.

Document observed construction features and condition concerns. Retain inspection notes, photographs, and repair information.

**150.E** Require Coverage A to meet the applicable floor of $75,000. Refer any request that does not support an adequate dwelling limit.

An adequate dwelling limit supports proper valuation and claim settlement. Limits below the floor can create unacceptable underinsurance exposure.

Document the requested Coverage A limit and valuation basis. Retain the calculation or valuation support used for the file.

**150.F** Do not bind Coverage A above $750,000 without referral approval. Refer any request that exceeds the applicable underwriting authority.

Higher dwelling limits can create aggregation and valuation concerns. Referral review confirms that the exposure remains within carrier appetite.

Document the requested limit and the reason for the higher amount. Retain referral approval in the underwriting file.

**150.G** Require a supportable replacement cost estimate for the dwelling. Refer any risk where the estimate conflicts with visible features, condition, or reported updates.

Replacement cost accuracy supports appropriate pricing and claim handling. Unsupported estimates can produce material valuation error.

Document the valuation source and relevant dwelling characteristics. Retain the estimate and any supporting evidence.

**150.H** Refer any dwelling with deferred maintenance that may impair weather resistance or safe occupancy. Do not bind where repairs are necessary to correct an active hazard.

Deferred maintenance can increase the frequency of water, wind, and fire loss. Active hazards may worsen before corrective work is completed.

Document the condition concern and the reported repair plan. Retain photographs, inspection findings, and completion evidence when obtained.

**150.I** Refer any dwelling with roof damage, roof leakage, missing covering material, or visible roof deterioration. Do not bind where the roof does not provide reliable weather protection.

Roof condition is material to water and wind loss exposure. Visible deterioration may indicate concealed damage to the dwelling.

Document roof type, condition, and reported maintenance. Retain photographs, inspection records, and repair support.

**150.J** Refer any dwelling with foundation movement, settlement, cracking, or drainage conditions that may affect structural integrity. Do not bind where the dwelling appears unstable.

Foundation concerns can lead to severe property damage and difficult causation review. Drainage problems may also contribute to recurring water damage.

Document the observed condition and any expert findings. Retain inspection reports, photographs, and repair records.

**150.K** Refer any dwelling with unsafe electrical equipment, visible wiring defects, or reported electrical malfunction. Do not bind where the electrical system presents a fire or shock hazard.

Electrical deficiencies can create substantial fire severity. Improper alterations may also prevent reliable inspection of the system.

Document the electrical concern and the reported corrective action. Retain inspection findings, photographs, and repair verification.

**150.L** Require a permanent heating system suitable for the dwelling and reported occupancy. Refer any risk relying on temporary, improvised, or unreliable heat.

Reliable heat reduces freeze loss and supports safe occupancy. Temporary heat sources can increase fire exposure.

Document the heating source, fuel, and reported condition. Retain service records or repair verification when available.

**150.M** Refer any dwelling with active plumbing leakage, deteriorated supply lines, drainage defects, or unresolved water damage. Do not bind where plumbing condition indicates an ongoing loss source.

Plumbing defects can produce repeated water losses and concealed deterioration. Active leakage can worsen rapidly after binding.

Document the reported plumbing condition and prior repairs. Retain photographs, invoices, and inspection findings.

**150.N** Apply the vandalism exclusion after 30 days of consecutive vacancy. Refer any risk where vacancy status cannot be established.

Vacancy changes the frequency and detectability of vandalism loss. Accurate vacancy reporting is necessary for proper coverage administration.

Document the last known occupancy and the reported vacancy status. Retain communications supporting the occupancy determination.

**150.O** Refer any dwelling that is unoccupied and lacks regular care, supervision, or maintenance. Do not bind where the reported condition suggests the dwelling may be vacant.

Unoccupied property can sustain damage that remains undiscovered. Reduced oversight also increases theft, vandalism, and water loss potential.

Document the reason for unoccupancy and the care arrangements. Retain inspection findings and applicant statements.

**150.P** Refer seasonal or intermittent occupancy where the dwelling may remain unattended. Confirm that maintenance and loss reporting arrangements are adequate.

Intermittent occupancy can delay discovery of property damage. Seasonal conditions may also increase freeze and weather exposure.

Document the occupancy pattern and caretaker arrangements. Retain any available inspection or maintenance support.

**150.Q** Refer any dwelling undergoing renovation, restoration, or substantial repair. Do not bind where construction activity materially changes the hazard.

Construction activity can create open walls, unsecured premises, and impaired protective systems. The exposure may change as work progresses.

Document the nature of the work and the reported completion status. Retain contracts, permits, photographs, and inspection support.

**150.R** Refer any dwelling with unrepaired fire, smoke, or heat damage. Do not bind until the cause and condition are adequately understood.

Prior fire damage may indicate unresolved structural, electrical, or maintenance concerns. Incomplete repair can increase the chance of further loss.

Document the reported loss history and repair status. Retain repair invoices, inspection findings, and cause-of-loss information.

**150.S** Refer any dwelling with prior water intrusion, seepage, leakage, or moisture damage that remains unresolved. Do not bind where the source of water has not been corrected.

Prior water damage may indicate recurring maintenance or drainage problems. Unresolved moisture can lead to further deterioration.

Document the reported source, repair status, and affected areas. Retain invoices, photographs, and inspection support.

**150.T** Refer any dwelling with visible mold, persistent moisture, or reported remediation activity. Do not bind where the underlying moisture source remains uncertain.

Mold conditions can indicate concealed water damage or poor ventilation. Unresolved moisture conditions can produce continuing property deterioration.

Document the observed condition and the reported remediation. Retain inspection findings, contractor records, and photographs.

**150.U** Review reported loss history for frequency, severity, cause, and repair completion. Refer any pattern that indicates an unresolved property condition or elevated hazard.

Loss history can reveal conditions not apparent from the application. Repeated losses may indicate ineffective repairs or misrepresented occupancy.

Document each reported loss and the disposition of repairs. Retain available loss details and supporting correspondence.

**150.V** Refer any risk with open claims, disputed claims, or incomplete repairs from a prior loss. Do not bind until the continuing exposure is understood.

Open claims can involve damaged property, uncertain causation, or unresolved valuation. Incomplete repairs may create an active hazard.

Document the claim status and reported repair condition. Retain available claim correspondence and repair support.

**150.W** Refer any dwelling with impaired fire protection, inaccessible emergency response, or unreliable water supply. Do not bind where protection conditions are materially adverse.

Fire protection affects expected severity and salvage potential. Restricted response capability can increase damage from an otherwise manageable event.

Document the reported protection conditions and access concerns. Retain inspection notes, mapping support, or referral findings.

**150.X** Refer any dwelling with restricted access, private road concerns, unstable approach conditions, or access that may prevent repair activity. Do not bind where normal access is unreliable.

Reliable access supports emergency response, inspection, and loss repair. Poor access can materially increase claim complexity.

Document the route, access condition, and reported maintenance responsibility. Retain photographs and available access agreements.

**150.Y** Refer any dwelling exposed to unmanaged vegetation, combustible debris, or nearby wildfire hazard. Do not bind where defensible space appears inadequate.

Vegetation and debris can increase wildfire ignition and spread potential. Exterior conditions may also affect firefighter access and property protection.

Document the observed vegetation and exterior maintenance condition. Retain photographs and any mitigation information.

**150.Z** Refer any dwelling with material coastal, tidal, or salt-air exposure. Confirm that construction condition and maintenance are consistent with the environment.

Coastal conditions can accelerate corrosion and weather-related deterioration. Water and wind exposure may also complicate claim assessment.

Document the reported location characteristics and visible exterior condition. Retain photographs and inspection findings.

**150.AA** Refer any dwelling with reported flood history, drainage overflow, or recurring surface-water concerns. Do not bind where the property condition suggests unresolved water exposure.

Flood-related conditions can cause repeated property damage and hidden deterioration. Drainage concerns may affect habitability and repair cost.

Document the reported history and current drainage condition. Retain photographs, inspection notes, and mitigation support.

**150.AB** Refer any dwelling with earth movement, slope instability, erosion, or retaining-wall concerns. Do not bind where site conditions appear unsafe.

Ground instability can cause severe structural damage and limit repair options. Erosion can worsen quickly after weather events.

Document the site condition and any reported stabilization work. Retain engineering reports, photographs, and repair records.

**150.AC** Refer any dwelling with sinkhole activity, subsidence concerns, or unexplained ground depressions. Do not bind where structural effects are reported or visible.

Subsidence conditions can produce sudden and extensive property damage. The cause may require specialized review before eligibility can be determined.

Document the reported condition and any investigation results. Retain expert reports, photographs, and applicant statements.

**150.AD** Refer any dwelling affected by mining activity, excavation, blasting, or nearby extraction operations. Confirm that the reported exposure does not impair the dwelling.

Mining and excavation can affect ground stability and structural condition. Blasting activity may create recurring damage potential.

Document the reported activity and the dwelling's observed condition. Retain maps, inspection findings, and available correspondence.

**150.AE** Refer any dwelling with business operations conducted on the premises. Do not bind where business activity materially changes the residential hazard.

Business use can increase visitor traffic, liability exposure, and property use intensity. Commercial equipment or storage may also increase fire severity.

Document the nature of the business activity and the areas used. Retain applicant statements and available inspection support.

**150.AF** Refer any dwelling used for professional services, client meetings, product storage, or business production. Do not bind where the use is not incidental to residence.

Business-related activity may create exposures not contemplated by personal lines underwriting. The operation can also alter property maintenance and occupancy patterns.

Document the reported use and any business equipment present. Retain inspection notes and applicant explanations.

**150.AG** Refer any dwelling associated with farming, animal keeping, crop activity, or agricultural equipment. Do not bind where the premises function as an agricultural operation.

Agricultural activity can increase fire, liability, and property damage exposure. Equipment and animal-related hazards may exceed residential appetite.

Document the reported agricultural use and property features. Retain photographs, inspection findings, and applicant statements.

**150.AH** Refer any dwelling used for transient occupancy or lodging activity. Do not bind where the occupancy changes frequently or is managed as a rental enterprise.

Transient occupancy can increase property wear, theft potential, and liability exposure. Frequent occupant turnover may also impede condition control.

Document the reported rental activity and management arrangements. Retain advertising, agreements, or applicant statements when available.

**150.AI** Refer any dwelling with boarders, roomers, or other nonhousehold occupants. Confirm that the reported arrangement remains consistent with residential eligibility.

Nonhousehold occupancy can change the frequency of property use and the likelihood of unnoticed damage. It may also indicate commercial lodging activity.

Document the reported occupancy arrangement and applicant relationship. Retain applicant statements and available lease support.

**150.AJ** Refer any dwelling with detached structures that are damaged, deteriorated, or used for nonresidential purposes. Do not bind where an outbuilding creates a material hazard.

Detached structures can increase fire spread and premises exposure. Nonresidential use may create hazards outside carrier appetite.

Document the type, condition, and use of each reported structure. Retain photographs and inspection findings.

**150.AK** Refer any dwelling with accessory structures used for storage of hazardous materials, machinery, or business property. Do not bind where storage creates an elevated fire or contamination hazard.

Hazardous storage can increase severity and complicate loss adjustment. Machinery and stored materials may also alter the intended residential use.

Document the reported contents and the condition of the structure. Retain photographs, inspection notes, and applicant explanations.

**150.AL** Refer any risk where personal property use or storage suggests commercial, agricultural, or industrial activity. Do not bind where contents materially alter the property hazard.

Unusual contents can increase fire load, theft potential, and loss complexity. Stored property may also obstruct safe access and maintenance.

Document the reported contents and storage areas. Retain photographs and inspection findings.

**150.AM** Refer any dwelling with code issues, required upgrades, or known ordinance concerns. Do not bind where compliance obligations may materially affect restoration.

Code compliance can increase repair scope after a covered loss. Known deficiencies may also reflect unsafe property condition.

Document the reported code condition and any required corrective work. Retain notices, permits, and repair documentation.

**150.AN** Refer any dwelling with historic features, specialized materials, or preservation restrictions. Confirm that valuation and repair expectations are supportable.

Specialized construction can increase repair cost and limit contractor availability. Preservation requirements may delay restoration after a loss.

Document the reported historic features and material characteristics. Retain valuation support, photographs, and available preservation records.

**150.AO** Refer any dwelling where association control, shared ownership, or common-area responsibility affects the insured premises. Do not bind where responsibility for repair is unclear.

Shared responsibility can complicate maintenance, valuation, and claim settlement. Unclear boundaries may create disputes after a loss.

Document the ownership arrangement and reported maintenance responsibility. Retain governing records or applicant statements when available.

**150.AP** Refer any manufactured, modular, or nontraditional dwelling construction. Do not bind until construction type and condition are verified.

Nontraditional construction can require specialized valuation and repair methods. Construction details may also affect weather and fire performance.

Document the reported construction type and identifying features. Retain photographs, inspection findings, and valuation support.

**150.AQ** Refer any dwelling constructed with log, heavy timber, earthen, or other specialized materials. Confirm that the construction is acceptable for the reported location and condition.

Specialized materials may require unique maintenance and repair expertise. Weathering and fire performance can differ from conventional construction.

Document the construction materials and observed condition. Retain photographs, inspection findings, and applicant statements.

**150.AR** Decline property consisting solely of land or a site without an eligible dwelling exposure. Refer any application where the insured property cannot be identified as a dwelling risk.

Land without a dwelling does not present the intended personal property exposure. Ambiguous property descriptions can impair underwriting and claims handling.

Document the reported property features and reason for disposition. Retain available site information in the underwriting file.

**150.AS** Refer any dwelling under construction or awaiting completion. Do not bind where the structure cannot support normal residential occupancy.

Incomplete construction can expose the property to weather, theft, and vandalism. The risk characteristics may change materially during construction.

Document the construction status and anticipated occupancy. Retain permits, photographs, contracts, and inspection findings.

**150.AT** Refer any dwelling held by an estate, trust, or other legal arrangement where insurable interest is not readily apparent. Confirm authority to insure before binding.

Legal ownership arrangements can affect who may report losses and receive claim payment. Clear authority supports accurate policy administration.

Document the reported legal arrangement and authorized party. Retain available trust, estate, or authorization records.

**150.AU** Refer any risk with title disputes, boundary disputes, pending transfer, or uncertain ownership. Do not bind until the applicant's interest is adequately supported.

Ownership disputes can delay repairs and complicate claim settlement. Boundary issues may also affect the insured property description.

Document the reported dispute or transfer status. Retain available correspondence and ownership support.

**150.AV** Refer any dwelling in foreclosure, repossession, or similar transfer condition. Do not bind where property control, occupancy, or maintenance responsibility is uncertain.

Distressed ownership conditions can result in vacancy and deferred maintenance. Responsibility for repairs may shift during the transfer process.

Document the reported status and current property control. Retain available notices, inspection findings, and applicant statements.

**150.AW** Refer any risk where financial distress may impair maintenance, repairs, or continued occupancy. Do not use financial information unless relevant to a documented property condition.

Financial stress can contribute to deferred maintenance and incomplete repairs. Underwriting action must remain tied to the property hazard.

Document the property condition supporting the referral. Retain only information necessary for the underwriting decision.

**150.AX** Require an inspection where available information does not adequately establish condition, occupancy, or use. Refer any risk where inspection findings conflict with application information.

Inspection supports reliable assessment of the property hazard. Conflicting information may indicate material misrepresentation or an unrecognized exposure.

Document the inspection request and findings. Retain photographs, reports, and follow-up correspondence.

**150.AY** Require current photographs where exterior condition, construction, or occupancy is material to eligibility. Refer any risk where photographs show an undisclosed concern.

Photographs provide objective support for underwriting conclusions. They can also identify hazards not fully described by the applicant.

Document the photograph source and date received. Retain relevant images in the underwriting file.

**150.AZ** Resolve material discrepancies between the application, inspection, valuation, and loss information before binding. Refer any risk where the discrepancy cannot be satisfactorily explained.

Consistent information supports accurate hazard selection. Unresolved discrepancies may indicate incorrect occupancy, condition, or valuation.

Document the discrepancy and the explanation obtained. Retain supporting communications and revised information.

**150.BA** Refer any risk involving suspected concealment, false statements, altered records, or material omission. Do not discuss suspected fraud with the applicant beyond necessary underwriting inquiry.

Misrepresentation can prevent accurate evaluation of the property hazard. Careful handling preserves the integrity of the underwriting review.

Document the facts supporting the concern. Retain records and communications in accordance with carrier handling practices.

**150.BB** Refer any risk outside stated eligibility or underwriting authority. Do not bind until the referral has been reviewed and dispositioned.

Referral review ensures consistent application of carrier appetite. It also provides oversight for unusual or complex exposures.

Document the reason for referral and the final disposition. Retain approval, decline, or withdrawal support in the underwriting file.

**150.BC** Confirm that requested coverage is consistent with the dwelling exposure and reported use. Refer any request that does not align with the property characteristics.

Coverage selection must reflect the actual insured premises. Misalignment can create claim handling uncertainty and inadequate protection.

Document the requested coverage and the basis for acceptance. Retain application details and underwriting notes.

**150.BD** Review the eighty percent condition when establishing dwelling valuation. Refer any risk where available information does not support compliance.

Proper valuation supports consistent underwriting and claim settlement. A supportable valuation reduces the likelihood of avoidable coverage disputes.

Document the valuation method and supporting property information. Retain the completed valuation support in the underwriting file.

**150.BE** Review the additional living expense limit for consistency with the reported occupancy and dwelling characteristics. Refer any request that appears inconsistent with the risk.

Loss-of-use needs can vary with occupancy and property characteristics. Appropriate review supports fair and consistent underwriting.

Document the requested limit and the underwriting basis. Retain relevant application information and referral approval.

## Rule 200 — Construction and Protection Class

**200.A** Refer any risk with incomplete construction information. Do not assign an underwriting position until the file supports the reported construction.

Construction features affect expected fire, wind, water, and liability loss behavior. Unsupported descriptions create avoidable classification error.

Document the source of the construction information. Record any unresolved discrepancy and the referral disposition.

**200.B** Classify the dwelling by the predominant exterior wall construction. Do not rely on listing language where inspection material indicates different construction.

Exterior wall construction affects combustibility, repair cost, and catastrophe response. Mixed or altered construction may require closer review.

Document the observed or reported wall materials. Retain supporting images, inspection notes, or reliable property data in the file.

**200.C** Refer any risk with combustible exterior wall construction near heavy vegetation or unmanaged growth. Request sufficient information to evaluate exposure conditions.

Vegetation can increase wildfire spread and radiant heat exposure. Construction materials may worsen resulting damage.

Document the vegetation condition described in the file. Record the referral outcome and any underwriting requirements.

**200.D** Evaluate roof covering condition before accepting a risk with visible deterioration, patching, or missing materials. Refer conditions that suggest impaired weather resistance.

Roof failure can lead to extensive interior water damage. Deteriorated covering may also increase wind loss severity.

Document the roof condition and the source of the information. Retain relevant images or inspection observations.

**200.E** Refer any risk with a roof that appears improperly installed or materially altered. Do not assume a recent roof replacement is acceptable without reliable support.

Installation defects can affect wind resistance and water shedding. Alterations may also conceal preexisting damage.

Document the reported roof work and available evidence of completion. Record the reason for any referral.

**200.F** Require a wind mitigation inspection when Coverage A exceeds $750,000. Obtain the inspection before final underwriting action.

Wind-resistance features can materially affect catastrophe exposure. Reliable inspection information supports consistent risk evaluation.

Document receipt and review of the wind mitigation inspection. Record any feature that conflicts with other file information.

**200.G** Review roof shape, attachment, and opening protection when wind exposure is material. Refer any risk with conflicting or incomplete wind-resistance information.

Wind losses can increase when roof and opening features do not perform as expected. Incomplete data can lead to unsupported eligibility decisions.

Document the wind-related construction features reviewed. Retain the source material used for the underwriting decision.

**200.H** Apply the hurricane mitigation discount only when the file supports the qualifying construction features. Do not apply the discount based solely on applicant representation.

Mitigation credits require reliable evidence to support rating integrity. Unsupported credits can create premium and audit issues.

Document the evidence supporting the hurricane mitigation discount. Record any discrepancy between reported and verified features.

**200.I** Refer any risk with unprotected exterior openings in an area subject to significant wind exposure. Obtain information regarding shutters, impact resistance, and attachment methods.

Opening failure can permit wind-driven rain and internal pressurization. Resulting damage may extend beyond the affected opening.

Document the opening protection information reviewed. Retain any inspection material, images, or certification information available.

**200.J** Refer any risk with prior wind damage that remains unrepaired or inadequately repaired. Do not bind until the condition has been evaluated.

Prior damage may indicate continuing vulnerability or hidden deterioration. Temporary repairs may not restore weather resistance.

Document the prior damage and repair status. Record the basis for accepting, declining, or conditioning the risk.

**200.K** Evaluate foundation type and visible settlement indicators for every dwelling risk. Refer cracks, displacement, moisture intrusion, or other signs of structural movement.

Foundation distress can affect structural stability and increase future repair severity. Moisture conditions may worsen movement or conceal related damage.

Document the foundation information available. Retain inspection notes and images supporting the underwriting action.

**200.L** Refer any risk with a raised foundation showing decay, damaged supports, or inadequate enclosure condition. Obtain clarification regarding crawlspace access and moisture control.

Raised structures may be vulnerable to water, wind, pest, and structural damage. Deteriorated support components can create serious loss potential.

Document the observed or reported condition. Record any required repairs and evidence of completion.

**200.M** Evaluate basement construction for signs of seepage, flooding, mold, or structural deterioration. Refer any risk with recurring water entry or unresolved moisture conditions.

Below-grade areas are susceptible to water damage and hidden structural issues. Recurrent moisture can affect habitability and repair cost.

Document the water history and observed condition. Retain any inspection findings or applicant explanations.

**200.N** Refer any risk with an unfinished renovation, active reconstruction, or exposed structural components. Do not treat active work as ordinary maintenance.

Unfinished work can increase fire, theft, weather, and liability exposure. Construction conditions may materially differ from the completed dwelling.

Document the scope and status of the work. Record the underwriting disposition and any conditions imposed.

**200.O** Review additions, enclosed porches, converted garages, and similar alterations for construction consistency. Refer alterations that appear unsupported, incomplete, or unsuitable for occupancy.

Altered areas may have different wiring, heating, foundation, or weather-resistance characteristics. Unverified conversions can increase property and liability exposure.

Document the alteration and available evidence of its condition. Record any concern requiring referral or corrective action.

**200.P** Refer any risk with signs of unpermitted or nonstandard construction. Do not rely on informal descriptions where the condition affects eligibility or valuation.

Nonstandard work may not perform as expected during fire, wind, or water events. It may also complicate repair and claim settlement.

Document the information indicating nonstandard construction. Record the final underwriting decision and supporting rationale.

**200.Q** Evaluate masonry veneer, stucco, and similar exterior finishes for cracking, separation, or moisture damage. Refer material conditions that may permit water intrusion.

Exterior finish failure can allow concealed damage to framing and insulation. Repairs may be extensive when moisture has migrated behind the finish.

Document the finish condition observed or reported. Retain supporting images or inspection commentary.

**200.R** Refer any risk with log, timber, adobe, rammed earth, or other atypical wall construction. Obtain sufficient information to assess durability, fire response, and repair availability.

Atypical construction can present specialized repair and valuation considerations. Local fire behavior and weather resistance may differ from conventional dwellings.

Document the wall construction and the source of the classification. Record the referral decision and any underwriting restrictions.

**200.S** Evaluate attached structures for construction, use, and separation from the dwelling. Refer any attachment that creates increased fire, wind, or liability exposure.

Attached structures can transmit fire or weather damage to the main dwelling. Their use may also affect occupancy and loss potential.

Document the attached structure and its reported use. Retain information supporting the classification and underwriting action.

**200.T** Refer any risk with a detached structure used for business, storage of hazardous materials, or nonresidential activity. Obtain a clear description of the structure and its use.

Detached structures can create property, fire, and liability exposures outside normal residential expectations. Hazardous occupancy can increase severity and frequency potential.

Document the reported structure use and contents. Record the referral outcome and any required risk controls.

**200.U** Review chimney, fireplace, wood stove, and solid-fuel heating features for condition and installation information. Refer any feature with visible damage, improper venting, or uncertain maintenance.

Solid-fuel heating can create significant fire and smoke loss potential. Improper venting can also increase carbon monoxide exposure.

Document the heating feature and available condition information. Retain inspection details, images, or maintenance evidence in the file.

**200.V** Refer any risk with an exterior cooking installation, fire feature, or similar heat source located near combustible construction. Evaluate the installation and surrounding conditions.

Open flame and high-heat equipment can ignite adjacent materials. Proximity to the dwelling may increase the chance of severe property damage.

Document the type and location of the heat source. Record the underwriting decision and any required corrective action.

**200.W** Evaluate electrical service condition when the file indicates outdated, damaged, altered, or overloaded components. Refer any risk with exposed wiring, repeated electrical issues, or unsafe panel conditions.

Electrical defects can cause fire and equipment damage. Hidden wiring conditions may worsen loss severity.

Document the electrical information reviewed. Retain inspection observations and record any required repair evidence.

**200.X** Refer any risk with plumbing leaks, corroded piping, prior water loss, or visible moisture damage. Obtain information sufficient to determine whether the condition is active or corrected.

Plumbing deterioration can produce sudden or repeated water damage. Concealed leaks may cause mold, rot, and structural impairment.

Document the plumbing condition and reported loss history. Record repair status and the basis for the underwriting action.

**200.Y** Evaluate heating and cooling equipment for visible deterioration, unsafe installation, or apparent lack of maintenance. Refer any equipment condition that may create fire, water, or freeze exposure.

Mechanical equipment failure can lead to property damage and loss of protective services. Poor maintenance may indicate broader property condition concerns.

Document the equipment condition and source of information. Retain any inspection material relevant to the decision.

**200.Z** Refer any risk with evidence of long-term vacancy, neglect, or deferred maintenance. Do not treat cosmetic deterioration as isolated when broader condition concerns are present.

Neglected property can have concealed roof, plumbing, electrical, and structural defects. Reduced oversight can delay discovery of a loss event.

Document the condition indicators and occupancy information. Record the underwriting disposition and supporting rationale.

**200.AA** Verify the reported protection class using reliable location and protection information. Refer any risk where the reported protection class cannot be supported.

Protection class affects fire response expectations and loss severity. Incorrect classification can affect pricing and eligibility.

Document the protection information reviewed. Record the source used to support the assigned classification.

**200.AB** Refer any risk with limited fire department access, obstructed approach, or uncertain emergency response route. Obtain information concerning access conditions before final action.

Delayed emergency access can increase fire loss severity. Access limitations may also affect rescue and property protection efforts.

Document the access condition and supporting source. Record the referral result and any underwriting requirement.

**200.AC** Evaluate fire hydrant availability and condition when relevant to the risk location. Refer any uncertainty regarding dependable water supply for fire suppression.

Water supply can materially affect fire control efforts. Limited supply may increase the severity of a structure fire.

Document the water-supply information obtained. Record any unresolved concern and the underwriting disposition.

**200.AD** Refer any risk served by a fire department with uncertain staffing, equipment, or response capability. Do not infer protection quality from a department name alone.

Fire response capability can vary by location and operating conditions. Unverified protection information can create classification error.

Document the protection information available in the file. Record the basis for the final underwriting determination.

**200.AE** Review any fire department call fee associated with the risk location. Refer the risk when the fee may affect claim handling or customer expectations.

Emergency response charges can create claim administration and customer service concerns. Clear file information supports consistent handling.

Document the reported fire department call fee information. Record the source and any referral disposition.

**200.AF** Refer any risk located on a private road, narrow lane, steep approach, or poorly maintained access route. Evaluate whether emergency vehicles can reasonably reach the dwelling.

Restricted access can delay fire suppression and emergency response. Road conditions may also affect inspection and claim activity.

Document the reported access characteristics. Retain any image, map, or inspection information supporting the decision.

**200.AG** Evaluate distance-related fire exposure from adjacent structures, commercial activity, and combustible storage without relying solely on mapping data. Refer conditions that suggest elevated spread potential.

Nearby hazards can increase the likelihood of fire transfer and severe loss. Mapping data may not reflect current site conditions.

Document the surrounding exposure reviewed. Record the source and any required underwriting action.

**200.AH** Refer any risk with nearby industrial activity, agricultural processing, or other operations that create smoke, chemical, dust, or fire exposure. Obtain a clear description of the adjoining activity.

External operations can affect property condition, fire response, and habitability. Their exposure may change without notice.

Document the adjoining activity and its apparent relationship to the dwelling. Record the referral decision and rationale.

**200.AI** Evaluate wildfire exposure based on vegetation, topography, access, defensible space, and nearby fuel sources. Refer risks with conditions that may impede suppression or increase spread.

Wildfire severity is influenced by both site condition and surrounding fuels. Construction characteristics may compound wildfire loss potential.

Document the wildfire exposure information reviewed. Retain any inspection observations, images, or mapping support.

**200.AJ** Refer any risk with brush, trees, debris, or combustible materials contacting or overhanging the dwelling. Require satisfactory correction where the condition materially increases fire or property damage exposure.

Combustible material near the structure can support fire spread and physical damage. Overhanging vegetation can also damage roof coverings and gutters.

Document the condition and any corrective action requested. Retain evidence of completion when provided.

**200.AK** Evaluate flood-related construction features when the property shows signs of prior inundation, elevated design, or water exposure. Refer any risk with unresolved flood damage or compromised enclosure components.

Water exposure can weaken materials and conceal deterioration. Improper enclosure features can increase future property damage.

Document the observed features and reported water history. Record the basis for any underwriting decision.

**200.AL** Refer any risk with retaining walls, steep slopes, erosion, or drainage conditions that may affect structural stability. Obtain sufficient information to evaluate the condition.

Ground movement and drainage failure can damage foundations, utilities, and access routes. These conditions may worsen during severe weather.

Document the site condition and available supporting material. Record the referral outcome and any required mitigation.

**200.AM** Evaluate drainage around the dwelling for ponding, poor grading, blocked gutters, or discharge toward the foundation. Refer any condition suggesting recurring water accumulation.

Poor drainage can contribute to foundation damage, water intrusion, and mold. Surface water conditions may also indicate deferred maintenance.

Document the drainage condition described or observed. Retain supporting images or inspection notes.

**200.AN** Refer any risk with security bars, blocked exits, damaged stairs, missing guards, or other apparent life-safety concerns. Do not accept the risk without appropriate review.

Life-safety conditions can increase liability severity and complicate emergency response. Visible hazards may also indicate broader maintenance concerns.

Document the condition and its location. Record the referral disposition and any required correction.

**200.AO** Maintain a clear underwriting record for all construction and protection class decisions. Refer unresolved material discrepancies before binding or renewing the risk.

Consistent documentation supports reliable classification, pricing, and claim handling. A complete record also supports later review of the underwriting decision.

Document the final construction and protection class determination. Record the evidence relied upon and any exception approval.

## Rule 210 — Roof Condition, Age and Material

**210.A** Require roof inspection findings before binding any risk when roof age is at or above 15 years. Hold binding authority until the findings support acceptable condition.

Use current condition to confirm that apparent remaining serviceability aligns with the risk presented. Treat missing or conflicting age evidence as requiring further review.

Record the roof age source and inspection findings in the underwriting file. Record any binding hold and its resolution.

**210.B** Decline any risk when roof age is at or above 25 years. Do not override this appetite limit through discretionary authority.

Apply the age limit consistently when roof replacement evidence is unavailable or unreliable. Treat unsupported statements of recent replacement as unverified.

Record the roof age source and declination basis in the underwriting file. Retain available evidence supporting the decision.

**210.C** Verify roof age from reliable evidence before relying on an applicant statement. Refer any risk with uncertain roof age for underwriting review.

Use verifiable age information to support roof condition evaluation. Treat estimates as provisional until supported.

Record the source used to establish roof age. Describe any unresolved age discrepancy.

**210.D** Require clear evidence of replacement when the applicant states that the roof has been replaced. Do not assume replacement from cosmetic appearance alone.

Use replacement evidence to distinguish full roof replacement from repair activity. Treat incomplete replacement work as existing roof condition.

Record the evidence reviewed and the roof components replaced. Record any limitation in the available evidence.

**210.E** Refer any risk with visible roof deterioration. Require condition findings sufficient to determine whether repairs or replacement are needed.

Use visible deterioration as an indicator of elevated water intrusion potential. Treat deteriorated materials as unsuitable for routine acceptance.

Record the observed deterioration and the source of the observation. Record the underwriting action taken.

**210.F** Refer any risk with missing, lifted, curled, cracked, or damaged roof covering. Do not bind until the condition is evaluated.

Use roof covering integrity to assess vulnerability to wind and water damage. Treat active defects as requiring correction or referral.

Record the roof defects identified and the review outcome. Record any required corrective action.

**210.G** Refer any risk with evidence of active roof leakage. Do not rely on planned repairs as evidence of acceptable present condition.

Use active leakage as an indicator of existing damage exposure. Treat interior staining associated with leakage as relevant roof condition evidence.

Record the leakage evidence and any related interior condition. Record the disposition of the risk.

**210.H** Require review of roof repairs that appear temporary or incomplete. Refer any risk with patching that does not establish durable condition.

Use repair quality to determine whether the roof remains susceptible to further damage. Treat improvised repairs as unresolved maintenance concerns.

Record the repair description and supporting evidence. Record whether further action is required.

**210.I** Refer any risk with roof repairs following weather damage. Confirm that repairs restored the roof to acceptable condition.

Use weather-related repair history to identify potential recurring damage. Treat undocumented repairs as requiring additional verification.

Record the stated cause of damage and repair information. Record the condition determination.

**210.J** Refer any risk with roof damage attributed to tree contact or vegetation. Require confirmation that the roof covering and supporting components remain sound.

Use tree-related damage to identify possible concealed roof impairment. Treat overhanging vegetation as an ongoing maintenance concern.

Record the vegetation condition and any roof damage evidence. Record required mitigation.

**210.K** Require review when roof drainage appears impaired. Refer any risk with standing water, blocked drainage paths, or drainage damage.

Use drainage condition to identify water accumulation exposure. Treat persistent water retention as adverse roof maintenance.

Record the drainage condition and available supporting evidence. Record the underwriting disposition.

**210.L** Refer any risk with damaged, loose, or absent flashing. Do not treat flashing defects as cosmetic conditions.

Use flashing condition to assess likely water entry points. Treat failed flashing as a material roof defect.

Record the flashing condition and affected roof areas. Record any required correction.

**210.M** Refer any risk with damaged roof penetrations, vents, or seals. Require sufficient information to determine whether water intrusion is present.

Use penetration condition to identify common leakage sources. Treat deteriorated seals as needing repair or further review.

Record the condition of roof penetrations and seals. Record the action required before binding.

**210.N** Refer any risk with visible sagging, uneven roof planes, or structural distortion. Do not accept structural roof concerns without review.

Use roof shape and alignment to identify possible framing or deck impairment. Treat distortion as potentially greater than surface damage.

Record the observed structural concern and its location. Record the referral outcome.

**210.O** Require review of any roof with soft, spongy, or deteriorated decking indicators. Refer risks where deck condition cannot be confirmed.

Use deck condition to evaluate roof support and fastening reliability. Treat suspected deck deterioration as a material condition issue.

Record the deck indicators and available evidence. Record the underwriting decision.

**210.P** Refer any risk with signs of mold, rot, or moisture damage associated with the roof. Require clarification of the source and extent of the condition.

Use moisture-related damage to identify unresolved leakage or ventilation concerns. Treat visible rot as evidence requiring further evaluation.

Record the condition observed and its apparent relationship to the roof. Record the required remediation or disposition.

**210.Q** Require review when attic observations suggest inadequate ventilation. Refer any risk with heat, moisture, or staining conditions linked to roof performance.

Use attic conditions to identify possible deterioration beneath the roof covering. Treat persistent moisture indicators as adverse maintenance evidence.

Record the attic findings and supporting observations. Record any required corrective action.

**210.R** Refer any risk with roof materials that are brittle, weathered, or materially worn. Do not accept material wear without confirming remaining condition.

Use material condition to assess susceptibility to future damage. Treat advanced wear as inconsistent with preferred risk quality.

Record the material condition and source of the finding. Record the underwriting outcome.

**210.S** Identify the roof material before completing eligibility review. Refer any risk where the roof material cannot be determined.

Use roof material identification to apply material-specific underwriting judgment. Treat unknown materials as requiring further information.

Record the roof material and the source of identification. Record any uncertainty requiring follow-up.

**210.T** Refer any risk with a roof material known to have elevated maintenance needs. Require condition evidence that supports acceptable durability.

Use material characteristics to identify potential loss frequency concerns. Treat poor maintenance history as a reason for additional review.

Record the material type and maintenance information considered. Record the final disposition.

**210.U** Refer any risk with wood roof material. Require clear condition evidence and confirmation that the material is maintained.

Use wood roof condition to evaluate susceptibility to weathering, decay, and ignition exposure. Treat untreated deterioration as unacceptable.

Record the wood roof condition and available maintenance evidence. Record the underwriting action.

**210.V** Refer any risk with slate roof material. Require confirmation that the roof covering, fasteners, and flashing are serviceable.

Use slate roof condition to identify breakage, displacement, and repair complexity. Treat missing or damaged slate as requiring evaluation.

Record the slate condition and supporting observations. Record any required referral action.

**210.W** Refer any risk with tile roof material. Require confirmation that tiles, underlayment, and edge components remain sound.

Use tile roof condition to identify breakage and concealed water entry exposure. Treat displaced or damaged tile as material roof impairment.

Record the tile condition and evidence reviewed. Record the underwriting disposition.

**210.X** Refer any risk with metal roof material showing corrosion, loose panels, or failed fasteners. Require condition findings before binding.

Use metal roof condition to identify wind resistance and water entry concerns. Treat corrosion affecting roof performance as adverse.

Record the metal roof condition and affected areas. Record required corrective action.

**210.Y** Refer any risk with roof material combinations. Confirm that each material area is identified and evaluated.

Use mixed materials to avoid relying on condition evidence from only part of the roof. Treat unidentified material areas as unresolved.

Record each observed roof material and its condition. Record any limitation in the available information.

**210.Z** Require review when roofing material differs from the material stated in the submission. Resolve the discrepancy before binding.

Use consistent material information to support accurate risk selection. Treat conflicting descriptions as requiring verification.

Record the conflicting material descriptions and the verified result. Record the source supporting the final classification.

**210.AA** Refer any risk with evidence of prior roof claims or reported roof damage. Require condition findings that address any residual impairment.

Use prior damage history to identify repairs that may not have restored acceptable condition. Treat unresolved damage history as a referral concern.

Record the prior damage information and current roof findings. Record the disposition of the risk.

**210.AB** Require review when roof claims history conflicts with stated roof condition. Do not accept unsupported assurances that prior damage was fully corrected.

Use claim information to identify possible repair or maintenance gaps. Treat conflicting information as requiring clarification.

Record the conflicting information and the resolution. Record the evidence relied upon.

**210.AC** Refer any risk where roof maintenance appears deferred. Require evidence that material defects have been corrected before binding.

Use maintenance condition to identify potential future water damage exposure. Treat deferred maintenance as adverse risk quality.

Record the maintenance concerns and available evidence. Record the required underwriting action.

**210.AD** Require review of roof surfaces with excessive debris accumulation. Refer any risk where debris may conceal damage or impair drainage.

Use debris condition to identify maintenance and moisture retention concerns. Treat concealed roof areas as requiring further inspection.

Record the debris condition and any concealed areas. Record any required cleanup or review.

**210.AE** Refer any risk with moss, algae, or vegetation growth affecting the roof. Require confirmation that the growth has not impaired roof materials.

Use growth patterns to identify retained moisture and maintenance concerns. Treat material deterioration associated with growth as adverse.

Record the observed growth and roof condition. Record the action required.

**210.AF** Refer any risk with roof-mounted equipment that may affect roof integrity. Require confirmation that mounting points and seals are acceptable.

Use roof-mounted equipment to identify additional penetration and drainage concerns. Treat unverified mounting conditions as requiring evaluation.

Record the equipment observed and the roof areas affected. Record the underwriting disposition.

**210.AG** Require review of solar equipment installed on or near the roof. Refer any risk where installation condition cannot be confirmed.

Use solar equipment condition to identify potential roof penetration and access concerns. Treat damaged or poorly maintained equipment as a referral issue.

Record the equipment condition and observed roof impacts. Record the final action.

**210.AH** Refer any risk with rooftop access equipment that appears damaged or unsecured. Require confirmation that the equipment has not impaired roof materials.

Use rooftop access conditions to identify puncture, fastening, and maintenance concerns. Treat unsecured equipment as adverse until resolved.

Record the equipment condition and roof impact. Record required mitigation.

**210.AI** Require review when roof access is limited or unsafe. Do not rely on incomplete observations as confirmation of acceptable condition.

Use access limitations to identify gaps in condition evidence. Treat inaccessible roof areas as requiring alternate verification.

Record the access limitation and alternate evidence obtained. Record any remaining uncertainty.

**210.AJ** Refer any risk with roof areas obscured by snow, debris, vegetation, or equipment. Require condition evidence for obscured areas before binding.

Use unobstructed observations to support reliable roof condition assessment. Treat unseen areas as unverified.

Record the obscured areas and the evidence used for review. Record the underwriting decision.

**210.AK** Require review when exterior observations conflict with interior observations. Resolve the source of the discrepancy before accepting the risk.

Use consistent findings to support reliable condition evaluation. Treat unexplained staining or moisture evidence as potentially roof-related.

Record the conflicting observations and the resolution. Record the evidence supporting acceptance or referral.

**210.AL** Refer any risk with interior ceiling stains, wall stains, or moisture marks near roof lines. Require confirmation whether the condition relates to roof leakage.

Use interior evidence to identify possible concealed roof damage. Treat unexplained moisture marks as requiring further evaluation.

Record the interior condition and stated cause. Record the roof review outcome.

**210.AM** Require review of roof replacement invoices or contractor statements when offered as age evidence. Verify that the evidence identifies the work performed.

Use detailed replacement evidence to distinguish complete replacement from limited repairs. Treat vague contractor statements as insufficient.

Record the evidence reviewed and the work described. Record the verified roof age basis.

**210.AN** Refer any risk where roof age evidence identifies only repairs, overlays, or partial work. Do not classify partial work as full roof replacement.

Use the scope of work to establish accurate roof age treatment. Treat material additions without replacement evidence as existing roof age.

Record the work scope and roof age determination. Record the evidence supporting the decision.

**210.AO** Require review of roof overlays. Refer any risk where the underlying roof condition cannot be established.

Use overlay information to identify concealed materials and potential deterioration. Treat unknown underlying condition as adverse.

Record the overlay information and available underlying condition evidence. Record the underwriting action.

**210.AP** Refer any risk with roof valleys showing wear, damage, or debris accumulation. Require confirmation that water flow remains unobstructed.

Use valley condition to identify concentrated water exposure. Treat damaged valley areas as material roof defects.

Record the valley condition and affected roof areas. Record required correction.

**210.AQ** Refer any risk with damaged roof edges, eaves, or rake components. Require review of the roof covering and related drainage condition.

Use edge condition to identify wind damage and water entry potential. Treat loose or deteriorated edge components as adverse.

Record the edge condition and supporting observations. Record the disposition.

**210.AR** Require review of gutters and downspouts when their condition suggests roof drainage impairment. Refer any risk with damage affecting water movement from the roof.

Use drainage component condition to identify water backup and overflow potential. Treat failed drainage components as maintenance concerns.

Record the drainage component condition and any roof impact. Record required mitigation.

**210.AS** Refer any risk with ice-related roof damage indicators. Require confirmation that damaged materials and drainage components have been corrected.

Use ice-related indicators to identify stress on roof coverings and edges. Treat recurring ice effects as requiring further review.

Record the observed indicators and condition findings. Record the underwriting action.

**210.AT** Require review when roof condition suggests wind damage. Refer any risk with displaced materials, exposed areas, or unsecured components.

Use wind damage indicators to identify impaired weather protection. Treat exposed roof areas as unacceptable until corrected.

Record the wind damage evidence and current condition. Record the risk disposition.

**210.AU** Refer any risk with hail damage indicators. Require confirmation that the roof remains serviceable and free of active impairment.

Use hail indicators to identify reduced material integrity and future leakage potential. Treat widespread impact evidence as requiring evaluation.

Record the hail damage indicators and available condition evidence. Record the final underwriting action.

**210.AV** Require review of roofs with uneven weathering across material areas. Refer any risk where uneven wear may indicate repair, damage, or drainage concerns.

Use weathering patterns to identify localized roof performance issues. Treat unexplained material variation as requiring clarification.

Record the weathering pattern and affected areas. Record the resolution.

**210.AW** Refer any risk with exposed underlayment, roof deck, or substrate. Do not bind until the exposure has been corrected or otherwise resolved.

Use exposed roof components to identify immediate water intrusion exposure. Treat exposed materials as unacceptable present condition.

Record the exposed area and corrective action status. Record the underwriting disposition.

**210.AX** Require review when roof fasteners appear loose, missing, corroded, or improperly installed. Refer any risk where fastening integrity cannot be confirmed.

Use fastening condition to assess wind resistance and material attachment. Treat failed fasteners as material roof impairment.

Record the fastening condition and affected roof areas. Record required correction.

**210.AY** Refer any risk with chimney-related roof damage or deteriorated chimney flashing. Require confirmation that the roof-to-chimney connection is watertight.

Use chimney interface condition to identify common leakage exposure. Treat damaged flashing or surrounding materials as adverse.

Record the chimney-related roof condition and evidence reviewed. Record the action taken.

**210.AZ** Require review when skylights are present. Refer any risk with damaged skylight glazing, flashing, seals, or surrounding roof materials.

Use skylight condition to identify roof penetration and water entry concerns. Treat unverified skylight integrity as requiring further evaluation.

Record the skylight condition and related roof findings. Record the underwriting decision.

**210.BA** Refer any risk with roof additions, extensions, or altered roof lines. Require confirmation that altered areas match the condition of the primary roof.

Use altered roof areas to identify transition points and drainage concerns. Treat unverified additions as requiring condition review.

Record the altered roof areas and condition findings. Record the risk disposition.

**210.BB** Require review when roof geometry creates concealed valleys, low-slope areas, or complex drainage paths. Refer any risk where condition evidence is incomplete.

Use roof geometry to identify areas vulnerable to water accumulation. Treat concealed drainage areas as requiring verification.

Record the roof geometry concerns and evidence obtained. Record any required action.

**210.BC** Refer any risk with low-slope roof areas showing wear, ponding, cracking, or seam separation. Require condition findings before binding.

Use low-slope condition to assess water retention and membrane integrity. Treat drainage or seam defects as adverse.

Record the low-slope condition and affected areas. Record the underwriting outcome.

**210.BD** Require review of flat roof areas. Refer any risk where drainage condition, membrane condition, or maintenance history is unclear.

Use flat roof information to identify elevated water retention exposure. Treat incomplete flat roof information as requiring referral.

Record the flat roof findings and information source. Record the disposition.

**210.BE** Refer any risk where roof condition cannot be determined from available information. Do not infer acceptable condition from the absence of reported defects.

Use sufficient condition evidence to support underwriting judgment. Treat missing information as an unresolved eligibility concern.

Record the missing information and efforts to obtain it. Record the final action.

**210.BF** Require current roof condition evidence when prior evidence does not address present condition. Refer any risk where available findings are no longer reliable.

Use current findings to support accurate assessment of maintenance and damage. Treat stale or incomplete evidence as insufficient.

Record the evidence reviewed and any reliability concern. Record the condition verification obtained.

**210.BG** Refer any risk with roof condition concerns identified during inspection review. Do not disregard adverse observations because the roof appears recently replaced.

Use condition findings to evaluate actual exposure rather than stated age alone. Treat visible defects as requiring action regardless of replacement history.

Record the adverse observations and underwriting response. Record any corrective requirement.

**210.BH** Apply the roof schedule trigger age when determining whether enhanced roof review is appropriate. Refer any risk where roof age treatment cannot be established from reliable evidence.

Use the roof schedule trigger age to support consistent review handling. Treat uncertain schedule treatment as requiring underwriting review.

Record the roof age basis and schedule handling decision. Record any referral outcome.

**210.BI** Apply the minimum roof payment percentage only when the risk otherwise meets applicable underwriting requirements. Do not use roof payment treatment to cure unacceptable roof condition.

Use the minimum roof payment percentage to align handling with approved roof condition treatment. Treat material defects as separate eligibility concerns.

Record the roof condition determination and payment treatment basis. Record any exception request.

**210.BJ** Refer any request for exception involving roof age, condition, or material. Do not grant an exception without documented underwriting rationale.

Use exception review to preserve consistent application of roof eligibility standards. Treat unsupported exception requests as ineligible.

Record the exception request, rationale, and final decision. Record any conditions imposed.

**210.BK** Require correction of material roof defects before binding when correction is necessary for acceptable condition. Do not accept verbal assurances that work will occur.

Use completed corrective action to confirm that known defects no longer affect the risk. Treat pending work as unresolved.

Record the required correction and evidence of completion. Record the binding decision.

**210.BL** Refer any risk where roof condition changes after initial review. Reassess eligibility when new information indicates deterioration, damage, or replacement activity.

Use updated information to maintain accurate risk evaluation. Treat changed roof condition as superseding prior assumptions.

Record the changed information and reassessment outcome. Record any revised underwriting action.

**210.BM** Document all roof age, material, condition, and inspection determinations supporting the underwriting decision. Maintain a clear record of referral, correction, acceptance, or declination activity.

Use complete file documentation to support consistent roof underwriting. Treat undocumented roof determinations as incomplete.

Record the final roof eligibility decision and supporting evidence. Record any outstanding follow-up requirement.

## Rule 220 — Water Exposure and Plumbing

**220.A** Identify all sources of water entering or leaving the insured location. Refer any risk with unresolved water entry conditions.

Water exposure may arise from site drainage, plumbing failure, appliance discharge, or backup conditions. Clear identification supports consistent eligibility decisions.

Document the reported water sources, visible conditions, and underwriting disposition in the file.

**220.B** Obtain a complete description of prior water damage disclosed by the applicant. Refer any loss pattern that is not fully explained.

Prior water damage may indicate an unresolved condition or recurring exposure. The cause and corrective action affect acceptability.

Document the reported cause, damaged areas, repairs, and supporting information reviewed.

**220.C** Refer any risk with active leakage, moisture staining, mold evidence, or wet building materials. Do not bind while the condition remains unresolved.

Active moisture conditions may produce additional damage after binding. The condition may also indicate concealed plumbing or building envelope failure.

Document the observed condition, source of information, referral decision, and corrective action required.

**220.D** Verify whether the dwelling has a basement, crawlspace, slab, or other below-grade area. Refer any below-grade area with a history of water entry.

Below-grade construction may be vulnerable to seepage, drainage failure, and plumbing discharge. Water entry history may indicate elevated future loss potential.

Document the foundation type, below-grade use, reported water history, and underwriting action.

**220.E** Refer any risk with a sump pump that lacks reliable discharge arrangements. Require correction when discharge may return water toward the dwelling.

Improper sump discharge may cause water to collect near the foundation. Recirculated water can increase seepage and backup exposure.

Document the sump pump description, discharge location, and corrective action required.

**220.F** Evaluate grading and drainage conditions when water exposure is reported or visible. Refer any risk where surface water drains toward the dwelling.

Adverse grading can direct water against foundation walls and openings. Persistent drainage conditions may contribute to repeated water damage.

Document the reported or observed drainage condition and the underwriting disposition.

**220.G** Refer any risk with roof runoff directed near the foundation. Require correction where downspouts or extensions create water accumulation.

Roof runoff can overload drainage areas and increase foundation moisture. Proper discharge reduces water concentration near the dwelling.

Document the runoff condition, reported correction, and evidence reviewed.

**220.H** Obtain details of plumbing material when the applicant reports prior plumbing repairs or water losses. Refer any risk with deteriorated, recalled, or incompatible plumbing components.

Plumbing material affects the likelihood of leakage, rupture, and connection failure. Repair history may reveal a broader system condition.

Document the plumbing material, repair history, and referral outcome.

**220.I** Refer any risk with visible corrosion, oxidation, or deterioration at plumbing connections. Do not accept unsupported assurances that the condition has been corrected.

Connection deterioration may precede leakage or sudden discharge. Reliable evidence supports an informed underwriting decision.

Document the affected component, source of condition information, and repair evidence reviewed.

**220.J** Review water heater condition when the dwelling has prior water damage or reported plumbing concerns. Refer any water heater with leakage, corrosion, or unsafe installation.

Water heater failure can cause substantial interior water damage. Installation condition may also affect the severity of a discharge.

Document the water heater condition, installation details, and underwriting action.

**220.K** Refer any risk with a water heater located above finished living space unless adequate protection is established. Evaluate containment and drainage arrangements.

A water heater discharge above finished areas may spread rapidly through concealed spaces. Protective features may reduce damage severity.

Document the water heater location, protective features reported, and referral decision.

**220.L** Obtain information regarding washing machines, dishwashers, refrigerators, and similar water-using appliances when prior appliance leakage is reported. Refer repeated appliance leakage or unresolved supply line concerns.

Appliance hoses, fittings, and supply lines are common sources of sudden water discharge. Repeated leakage may indicate inadequate maintenance or unsuitable installation.

Document the appliance involved, cause of leakage, repairs completed, and underwriting outcome.

**220.M** Refer any risk with flexible water supply lines showing wear, bulging, corrosion, or leakage. Require replacement where condition information indicates imminent failure.

Supply line failure can release water rapidly and may remain undiscovered. Condition-based action helps prevent avoidable loss.

Document the supply line condition, replacement information, and file decision.

**220.N** Evaluate plumbing shutoff accessibility when the applicant reports prior leakage or a vacant exposure. Refer any risk where water cannot be shut off promptly.

Accessible shutoff controls can limit the duration of a plumbing discharge. Delayed shutoff may materially increase loss severity.

Document the shutoff location, accessibility, and any required correction.

**220.O** Refer any risk with a history of frozen pipes, burst pipes, or winter-related water damage. Obtain the cause and corrective action before determining acceptability.

Freeze losses may result from inadequate heat, insulation, maintenance, or occupancy practices. Corrective action must address the underlying cause.

Document the reported freeze event, source of damage, repairs, and prevention measures.

**220.P** Evaluate seasonal, vacant, and intermittently occupied dwellings for freeze and plumbing exposure. Refer any risk without reliable temperature control and water management practices.

Reduced occupancy can delay detection of a leak or heating failure. Water systems may require protective measures when regular oversight is limited.

Document the occupancy pattern, heating arrangement, water management practices, and underwriting decision.

**220.Q** Refer any risk with plumbing repairs performed by an unidentified or unqualified party when the repairs relate to prior water damage. Obtain credible repair information before binding.

Improper repairs can conceal a continuing defect or create a new source of leakage. Reliable repair information supports eligibility evaluation.

Document the repair description, party performing the work, supporting evidence, and referral result.

**220.R** Review renovation activity involving kitchens, bathrooms, laundry areas, or plumbing systems. Refer ongoing work or incomplete restoration following water damage.

Renovation activity can expose unfinished plumbing, open wall cavities, and temporary water connections. Incomplete restoration may leave hidden damage unresolved.

Document the renovation status, affected areas, and conditions required before acceptance.

**220.S** Refer any risk with water staining, warped finishes, damaged flooring, or ceiling discoloration when the cause is unknown. Do not rely solely on cosmetic repair information.

Visible damage may reflect an active or concealed water source. Cosmetic work does not establish that the cause has been corrected.

Document the affected areas, reported cause, repair evidence, and underwriting action.

**220.T** Evaluate prior water claims for cause, location, severity, and corrective action. Refer any claim history showing recurring water damage from a similar source.

Recurring claims may indicate an exposure that remains uncorrected. Loss history must be assessed with the condition of the property.

Document the loss cause, affected area, corrective action, and file disposition.

**220.U** Refer any risk with a reported sewer backup, drain backup, or sump overflow loss. Obtain details of the source and all mitigation measures.

Backup events may arise from public systems, private lines, drainage conditions, or equipment failure. The source determines the appropriate underwriting response.

Document the reported backup source, affected area, mitigation measures, and referral outcome.

**220.V** Apply the water backup and sump overflow sublimit when the risk is otherwise eligible for that coverage. Refer any request that requires underwriting authority beyond the available limit.

Water backup exposure requires consistent application of carrier appetite and available coverage terms. Coverage decisions must reflect the condition and history of the risk.

Document the requested coverage, applicable underwriting authority, and final coverage disposition.

**220.W** Refer any request for water backup limits above $25,000. Do not issue the requested limit without authorized approval.

Higher requested limits may increase exposure where drainage, sewer, or sump conditions are unfavorable. Referral permits review of loss history and risk controls.

Document the requested limit, referral rationale, and authorized decision.

**220.X** Apply the backup endorsement deductible that is available for the risk and approved by underwriting. Do not alter the deductible without authorized approval.

The deductible supports appropriate participation in water backup loss exposure. Consistent application protects rating and underwriting discipline.

Document the deductible applied, approval source, and any special handling instructions.

**220.Y** Evaluate the check valve requirement when the risk has a reported backup condition or applicable local drainage exposure. Refer any risk where required protection is absent or condition information is incomplete.

Check valves may reduce the likelihood of water entering through drainage connections. Their effectiveness depends on proper installation and maintenance.

Document the reported valve condition, supporting information, and underwriting disposition.

**220.Z** Refer any risk with a damaged, blocked, disconnected, or improperly maintained check valve. Require repair or replacement when the condition affects backup exposure.

A malfunctioning valve may fail when drainage systems are under pressure. Maintenance condition is material to the effectiveness of the protection.

Document the valve condition, corrective action, and evidence reviewed.

**220.AA** Obtain information about private sewer lines, septic systems, and drainage connections when the applicant reports water backup or sewage discharge. Refer unresolved system defects.

Private systems may create exposures not controlled by a public utility. Defects can result in repeated backup, discharge, or property damage.

Document the system type, reported condition, prior events, and underwriting action.

**220.AB** Refer any risk with a septic system that has backed up into the dwelling or discharged near the structure. Obtain repair information before considering acceptance.

Septic backup may indicate system failure, improper maintenance, or unsuitable site conditions. The cause must be addressed before exposure can be evaluated.

Document the backup history, repair description, and referral decision.

**220.AC** Evaluate sewer lateral condition when water backup loss history is reported. Refer any risk with a damaged, collapsed, obstructed, or root-intruded lateral.
