Document the access control arrangement. Record any corrective action required.

**250.BA** Refer any vacant dwelling located in an area with repeated vandalism or theft activity. Confirm available protective measures.

Local loss activity can compound the hazard of vacancy. Property protection may require underwriting review.

Document the reported area conditions. Record available security information.

**250.BB** Refer any dwelling where a neighbor, tenant, or other party reports a change in occupancy. Verify the information with the insured or authorized representative.

Third-party reports can reveal material facts not otherwise disclosed. Verification protects against inaccurate classification.

Document the report and its source. Record the verification effort and outcome.

**250.BC** Require clear file support for any exception involving occupancy, vacancy, or rental exposure. Refer exceptions lacking adequate justification.

Exceptions require a defensible underwriting basis. Complete records support consistent handling.

Document the exception rationale. Retain approval authority in the file.

**250.BD** Refer any risk where occupancy facts remain unresolved after reasonable inquiry. Do not bind or continue the risk without underwriting direction.

Unresolved facts prevent reliable assessment of the exposure. Uncertainty can result in improper eligibility or rating.

Document the unresolved issues. Record all information requested and received.

**250.BE** Record the final occupancy, vacancy, and rental classification before completing underwriting action. Confirm that the file supports the classification.

Clear classification supports consistent servicing and claim handling. Complete documentation preserves the underwriting basis.

Document the final classification and decision. Retain all material supporting records.

## Rule 300 — Binding Authority

**300.A** Bind Coverage A only when the requested limit does not exceed $800,000. Refer any request above that authority.

This control keeps line underwriting within delegated capacity. It prevents unsupported binding decisions.

Record the requested Coverage A limit in the account record. Record the binding authority used.

**300.B** Bind Coverage A only when the requested limit does not exceed $1,500,000 when acting as a senior underwriter. Refer any request above that authority.

This control preserves senior underwriting delegation. It directs elevated exposures to the proper authority.

Record the requested Coverage A limit in the account record. Record the senior underwriting authority used.

**300.C** Bind only risks that fall within the underwriter’s active delegation. Refer any risk outside the assigned delegation.

This control aligns binding action with assigned authority. It prevents unauthorized commitments.

Record the assigned authority level in the account record. Record the referral disposition.

**300.D** Do not approve a risk that requires authority beyond the underwriter’s delegation. Refer the risk for independent review.

This control separates underwriting evaluation from elevated approval. It supports disciplined authority use.

Record the reason for referral in the account record. Record the approving authority.

**300.E** Bind coverage only after confirming the requested effective date. Refer any request with an unclear effective date.

This control prevents unintended coverage commitments. It supports accurate policy processing.

Record the requested effective date in the account record. Record the source of confirmation.

**300.F** Bind only after obtaining sufficient risk information for an underwriting decision. Refer any risk with unresolved material information.

This control supports informed underwriting. It limits decisions based on incomplete facts.

Record the material information reviewed in the account record. Record unresolved items and disposition.

**300.G** Confirm the named insured’s identity before binding. Refer any risk with conflicting identity information.

This control prevents binding to an uncertain party. It supports accurate account setup.

Record the identity information reviewed in the account record. Record the resolution of any conflict.

**300.H** Confirm the insured’s interest in the property before binding. Refer any risk with unclear ownership or occupancy rights.

This control verifies the insurable interest. It prevents coverage from attaching to an unsupported exposure.

Record the ownership information reviewed in the account record. Record the basis for the underwriting decision.

**300.I** Confirm the occupancy presented for underwriting before binding. Refer any risk with unclear, unusual, or changing occupancy.

This control aligns coverage with the actual exposure. It identifies occupancy conditions requiring review.

Record the stated occupancy in the account record. Record the source of occupancy verification.

**300.J** Confirm the property location before binding. Refer any risk with an incomplete or conflicting location.

This control ensures the risk is evaluated at the correct premises. It prevents territory and exposure errors.

Record the property location in the account record. Record the source used to verify the location.

**300.K** Bind only risks located within the carrier’s accepted territory. Refer any location with uncertain territorial eligibility.

This control prevents commitments outside approved geographic appetite. It supports proper risk assignment.

Record the location eligibility determination in the account record. Record the referral outcome when applicable.

**300.L** Refer any risk with a material hazard not addressed by available underwriting information. Do not bind until the hazard is resolved or approved.

This control prevents acceptance of unknown exposure conditions. It supports consistent hazard evaluation.

Record the hazard identified in the account record. Record the approval or corrective action.

**300.M** Review prior loss information before binding when available. Refer any loss pattern that raises material underwriting concern.

This control identifies adverse loss experience. It supports appropriate pricing and eligibility decisions.

Record the loss information reviewed in the account record. Record the underwriting disposition.

**300.N** Refer any risk with prior cancellation, nonrenewal, or coverage restriction for an underwriting reason. Do not bind until the cause is evaluated.

This control identifies potential adverse selection. It supports informed acceptance decisions.

Record the prior coverage action in the account record. Record the cause and underwriting response.

**300.O** Refer any risk with open, disputed, or unresolved claim activity. Do not bind until the exposure is evaluated.

This control prevents binding while material claim facts remain uncertain. It supports proper exposure assessment.

Record the claim activity reviewed in the account record. Record the referral disposition.

**300.P** Confirm the property condition is acceptable before binding. Refer any risk with deferred maintenance, damage, or unsafe condition.

This control prevents acceptance of impaired property. It supports accurate evaluation of physical hazards.

Record the condition information reviewed in the account record. Record required corrective action.

**300.Q** Use supportable valuation information when binding property coverage. Refer any risk with valuation information that appears inadequate or inconsistent.

This control limits underinsurance and excessive limit exposure. It supports reliable coverage decisions.

Record the valuation basis in the account record. Record the source of valuation information.

**300.R** Confirm that requested coverage aligns with the reported property and occupancy. Refer any mismatch between requested coverage and risk characteristics.

This control prevents unsupported coverage combinations. It supports accurate policy construction.

Record the coverage review in the account record. Record the resolution of any mismatch.

**300.S** Bind only available coverage provisions that match the accepted risk. Refer any request requiring unavailable or uncertain coverage treatment.

This control prevents unauthorized coverage grants. It supports consistent underwriting practice.

Record the requested coverage treatment in the account record. Record the approval authority when required.

**300.T** Confirm that the selected deductible is acceptable for the risk. Refer any deductible selection that does not match underwriting direction.

This control supports appropriate risk sharing. It prevents unsuitable deductible arrangements.

Record the selected deductible in the account record. Record the basis for acceptance.

**300.U** Refer any request for an endorsement that changes the risk materially. Do not bind the endorsement without required authority.

This control prevents unreviewed changes to coverage scope. It supports controlled use of underwriting accommodations.

Record the endorsement request in the account record. Record the approval or declination.

**300.V** Do not alter exclusions or limitations without delegated authority. Refer any request that would broaden coverage beyond accepted terms.

This control preserves approved coverage boundaries. It prevents unintended carrier exposure.

Record the requested alteration in the account record. Record the authority supporting the decision.

**300.W** Refer any risk that presents an underwriting concern not addressed by standard guidance. Do not bind based solely on judgment when referral is warranted.

This control captures unusual exposures. It supports consistent treatment of comparable risks.

Record the underwriting concern in the account record. Record the referral response.

**300.X** Obtain required approval before binding a referred risk. Do not treat a pending referral as approval.

This control prevents premature commitments. It preserves the integrity of the referral process.

Record the approval in the account record. Record the approving authority.

**300.Y** Bind a referred risk only within the terms approved. Refer any change from the approved terms.

This control prevents expansion of an approved exception. It ensures the approval remains applicable.

Record the approved terms in the account record. Record any change requiring further review.

**300.Z** Do not rely on verbal approval without a clear account record. Confirm the approval terms before binding.

This control reduces misunderstanding of authority decisions. It supports reliable audit review.

Record the approval details in the account record. Record the source of confirmation.

**300.AA** Confirm that delegated authority is active before binding. Refer any risk when authority status is uncertain.

This control prevents use of expired, suspended, or unavailable authority. It supports proper control of binding access.

Record the authority status reviewed in the account record. Record the referral disposition.

**300.AB** Bind only through approved carrier systems and access channels. Refer any request that cannot be processed through authorized access.

This control protects binding controls and account integrity. It prevents untracked commitments.

Record the system action in the account record. Record any access issue and resolution.

**300.AC** Confirm that all material rating information supports the proposed transaction before binding. Refer any inconsistency that affects underwriting acceptability.

This control reduces rating and eligibility error. It supports accurate policy issuance.

Record the rating information reviewed in the account record. Record the resolution of any inconsistency.

**300.AD** Do not backdate coverage without required authority. Refer any request for retroactive coverage treatment.

This control prevents unreviewed assumption of prior exposure. It supports controlled effective-date decisions.

Record the requested coverage treatment in the account record. Record the approval authority.

**300.AE** Confirm that the requested expiration aligns with the carrier’s available transaction handling. Refer any request with an unclear coverage term.

This control prevents unintended coverage duration. It supports accurate account processing.

Record the requested expiration in the account record. Record the basis for acceptance.

**300.AF** Confirm that the premium indication is available before binding. Refer any risk with unavailable or materially uncertain premium treatment.

This control prevents binding without an identified financial obligation. It supports accurate transaction processing.

Record the premium indication in the account record. Record the source of the indication.

**300.AG** Do not bind when required payment handling is unresolved. Refer any risk with payment information that prevents normal processing.

This control prevents incomplete account setup. It supports orderly policy administration.

Record the payment status in the account record. Record the referral disposition.

**300.AH** Evaluate producer-provided risk information for completeness and consistency. Refer any material statement that cannot be reasonably supported.

This control prevents reliance on uncertain risk representations. It supports sound underwriting decisions.

Record the information reviewed in the account record. Record the basis for accepting or questioning it.

**300.AI** Refer any risk with suspected misrepresentation, concealment, or fraud. Do not bind while the concern remains unresolved.

This control protects the carrier from intentional adverse selection. It supports appropriate investigation and escalation.

Record the concern in the account record. Record the referral disposition.

**300.AJ** Refer any risk with a legal, regulatory, or compliance concern. Do not bind until the concern is resolved by appropriate authority.

This control prevents commitments that may create compliance exposure. It supports controlled risk acceptance.

Record the compliance concern in the account record. Record the resolution and approving authority.

**300.AK** Refer any vacant, unoccupied, or intermittently occupied property. Do not bind until the occupancy exposure is evaluated.

This control identifies property conditions with elevated loss potential. It supports appropriate eligibility review.

Record the reported occupancy condition in the account record. Record the underwriting disposition.

**300.AL** Confirm the construction characteristics presented for underwriting. Refer any property with unusual, mixed, or uncertain construction.

This control ensures that physical characteristics are evaluated accurately. It supports appropriate hazard assessment.

Record the construction information in the account record. Record the source of verification.

**300.AM** Refer any property undergoing renovation, repair, or construction activity. Do not bind until the activity and occupancy exposure are evaluated.

This control identifies changing physical hazards. It supports proper treatment of transitional property conditions.

Record the activity described in the account record. Record the approval or required action.

**300.AN** Confirm that reported protective features are present and operational when material to underwriting. Refer any unavailable or conflicting protective information.

This control prevents reliance on unsupported risk mitigation. It supports accurate evaluation of property protection.

Record the protective information reviewed in the account record. Record the source of confirmation.

**300.AO** Refer any risk involving animals, liability hazards, or other conditions that may materially increase personal liability exposure. Do not bind until the exposure is evaluated.

This control identifies liability conditions outside routine acceptance. It supports consistent treatment of heightened exposures.

Record the liability condition in the account record. Record the underwriting disposition.

**300.AP** Refer any property with business, commercial, or income-producing use. Do not bind until the use is evaluated.

This control prevents acceptance of exposure outside personal lines intent. It supports proper classification of the risk.

Record the reported use in the account record. Record the referral outcome.

**300.AQ** Refer any risk involving recreational equipment or activity that may alter liability exposure. Do not bind until the exposure is evaluated.

This control identifies hazards that may require specialized treatment. It supports controlled acceptance decisions.

Record the reported equipment or activity in the account record. Record the underwriting disposition.

**300.AR** Refer any risk with water intrusion, drainage, sewer, or flood-related concern. Do not bind until the condition is evaluated.

This control identifies property exposures with elevated loss potential. It supports proper assessment of water-related hazards.

Record the reported condition in the account record. Record the referral response.

**300.AS** Confirm roof condition when roof information is material to the risk. Refer any property with uncertain, damaged, or deteriorated roof condition.

This control identifies a material source of property loss. It supports sound physical condition review.

Record the roof information in the account record. Record the basis for acceptance.

**300.AT** Refer any property with electrical conditions that may create a fire or shock hazard. Do not bind until the condition is evaluated.

This control identifies hazards that may not be reflected in basic property information. It supports appropriate safety review.

Record the electrical condition in the account record. Record the underwriting disposition.

**300.AU** Refer any property with plumbing conditions that may create water damage exposure. Do not bind until the condition is evaluated.

This control identifies potential severity from hidden or active water loss. It supports proper physical risk assessment.

Record the plumbing condition in the account record. Record the required action or approval.

**300.AV** Refer any property with heating conditions that may create fire, freeze, or carbon monoxide exposure. Do not bind until the condition is evaluated.

This control identifies material habitability and property hazards. It supports appropriate acceptance decisions.

Record the heating information in the account record. Record the underwriting disposition.

**300.AW** Refer any risk involving solid-fuel heating, open flame, or comparable fire hazard. Do not bind until the exposure is evaluated.

This control identifies elevated fire potential. It supports consistent handling of nonroutine heating exposures.

Record the reported hazard in the account record. Record the approval or required action.

**300.AX** Refer any risk involving a pool, spa, or similar water feature. Do not bind until safety and liability exposure are evaluated.

This control identifies potentially severe premises liability exposure. It supports proper acceptance review.

Record the water feature information in the account record. Record the referral disposition.

**300.AY** Refer any risk involving a trampoline, climbing feature, or similar recreational hazard. Do not bind until the liability exposure is evaluated.

This control identifies premises conditions with heightened injury potential. It supports consistent liability review.

Record the reported hazard in the account record. Record the underwriting disposition.

**300.AZ** Confirm that location characteristics support the proposed risk classification. Refer any risk with conflicting property or territory characteristics.

This control prevents classification based on inaccurate premises information. It supports reliable underwriting treatment.

Record the location characteristics in the account record. Record the source of verification.

**300.BA** Refer any risk that may contribute to concentrated catastrophe exposure. Do not bind until accumulation concern is evaluated.

This control limits unintended aggregation of severe loss potential. It supports portfolio management.

Record the catastrophe concern in the account record. Record the referral outcome.

**300.BB** Refer related risks when their combined exposure may affect underwriting acceptability. Do not bind until the relationship is evaluated.

This control identifies exposure that may not appear in an isolated account review. It supports proper aggregation assessment.

Record the related risk information in the account record. Record the underwriting disposition.

**300.BC** Do not split, sequence, or structure a transaction to avoid authority limits. Refer any transaction that appears designed to bypass review.

This control preserves the purpose of delegated authority. It prevents circumvention of approval requirements.

Record the transaction concern in the account record. Record the referral response.

**300.BD** Refer any risk when available information indicates an adverse condition not reflected in the proposed terms. Do not bind until the inconsistency is resolved.

This control prevents acceptance based on incomplete favorable information. It supports balanced risk evaluation.

Record the adverse information in the account record. Record the resolution or approval.

**300.BE** Resolve conflicting risk information before binding. Refer any conflict that materially affects eligibility, coverage, or pricing.

This control prevents decisions based on uncertain facts. It supports accurate policy issuance.

Record the conflicting information in the account record. Record the resolution source.

**300.BF** Seek reasonable verification when material information appears unreliable. Refer any risk when verification cannot support the proposed decision.

This control improves the reliability of underwriting facts. It limits acceptance of unsupported exposure information.

Record the verification performed in the account record. Record the resulting underwriting decision.

**300.BG** Reassess binding authority when material risk information changes before issuance. Refer any change that moves the risk outside authority.

This control ensures that approval remains appropriate as facts develop. It prevents reliance on outdated evaluation.

Record the changed information in the account record. Record the revised authority determination.

**300.BH** Review material changes reported after binding for underwriting action. Refer any change that may require coverage revision or account action.

This control supports continued accuracy of the bound account. It identifies risks that may no longer meet acceptance standards.

Record the reported change in the account record. Record the resulting action.

**300.BI** Escalate any bound risk that requires cancellation, restriction, or other corrective action. Do not make an unsupported coverage change.

This control ensures that adverse action receives proper review. It supports consistent handling of post-bind issues.

Record the reason for corrective action in the account record. Record the approving authority.

**300.BJ** Record the basis for declining a risk when a binding request is not accepted. Communicate the disposition through authorized channels.

This control supports consistent underwriting outcomes. It creates a reliable record of adverse decisions.

Record the declination basis in the account record. Record the communication action.

**300.BK** Communicate binding terms accurately to authorized parties. Refer any request to change terms after communication when authority is uncertain.

This control prevents misunderstanding of the carrier’s commitment. It supports controlled transaction handling.

Record the communicated terms in the account record. Record any subsequent change.

**300.BL** Maintain the account record so the binding decision can be understood without unsupported assumptions. Include material facts, authority, and disposition.

This control supports auditability and consistent account servicing. It preserves the basis for the underwriting decision.

Record material underwriting facts in the account record. Record the final binding disposition.

**300.BM** Review the account record for completeness before completing the binding action. Refer any account with material missing information.

This control prevents incomplete transactions from moving forward. It supports reliable downstream processing.

Record the completeness review in the account record. Record any item requiring follow-up.

**300.BN** Do not treat an exception as authority for unrelated risks. Refer every new exception request for separate evaluation.

This control prevents informal expansion of underwriting appetite. It preserves the limits of risk-specific approvals.

Record the exception request in the account record. Record the approval terms.

**300.BO** Refer any disagreement regarding authority, eligibility, or binding terms. Do not bind while the disagreement remains unresolved.

This control prevents commitments made without aligned underwriting direction. It supports timely escalation of material disputes.

Record the disagreement in the account record. Record the resolution and approving authority.

**300.BP** Transfer a binding request only to an authorized underwriter when the assigned underwriter cannot act. Do not leave authority status unclear during transfer.

This control preserves accountability for the underwriting decision. It supports orderly handling of pending risks.

Record the transfer in the account record. Record the receiving authority.

**300.BQ** Protect carrier system access used for binding activity. Report any suspected unauthorized access or transaction activity.

This control protects the integrity of underwriting commitments. It supports reliable control of binding functions.

Record the access concern in the account record. Record the reported disposition.

**300.BR** Complete a final authority and risk review before confirming a binding action. Refer any material issue identified during final review.

This control captures errors before the carrier assumes exposure. It supports accurate and authorized binding.

Record the final review in the account record. Record the binding confirmation or referral disposition.

## Rule 310 — Mandatory Referral Conditions

**310.A** Refer any account with a reported loss at or above $100,000. Hold binding authority pending referral disposition.

Large reported losses may indicate unresolved severity, liability, or property condition concerns. Central review confirms the account remains within carrier appetite.

Document the reported loss facts and referral disposition in the underwriting file. Record the source of the loss information.

**310.B** Refer any risk with an open claim. Do not bind, renew, or broaden coverage pending referral disposition.

An open claim may affect eligibility, valuation, occupancy, or loss-control requirements. Central review determines whether the claim creates an unacceptable exposure.

Document the claim status and known allegations in the underwriting file. Record the referral disposition and any imposed conditions.

**310.C** Refer any risk with a disputed claim. Suspend underwriting action until the dispute is resolved or referral authority directs otherwise.

Claim disputes may indicate coverage uncertainty, valuation concerns, or adverse litigation potential. Central review evaluates the effect on account acceptability.

Document the disputed issues and available claim information. Record the referral decision in the underwriting file.

**310.D** Refer any risk with a liability allegation involving bodily injury. Do not rely solely on applicant characterization of the event.

Bodily injury allegations may create material severity exposure. Central review evaluates available facts and claim handling status.

Document the alleged circumstances and known injuries. Record the information source and referral disposition.

**310.E** Refer any risk with a liability allegation involving property damage. Hold issuance activity pending referral disposition.

Property damage allegations may indicate an ongoing liability exposure or hazardous condition. Central review determines whether corrective action is required.

Document the reported damage and event circumstances. Record the referral decision and any required action.

**310.F** Refer any risk with an allegation of negligent supervision. Do not accept explanations without supporting account facts.

Negligent supervision allegations may indicate elevated personal liability exposure. Central review assesses the nature of the allegation and account controls.

Document the allegation and available supporting information. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.G** Refer any risk with an allegation of intentional conduct. Escalate immediately when facts suggest criminal conduct.

Intentional conduct allegations may create severe liability, coverage, and reputational concerns. Central review evaluates the account under carrier standards.

Document the reported conduct and source of information. Record all referral instructions in the underwriting file.

**310.H** Refer any risk involving pending litigation. Do not bind or alter coverage without referral authority approval.

Pending litigation may affect loss potential, defense obligations, and account eligibility. Central review reviews the litigation posture and available allegations.

Document the parties, allegations, and known case status. Record the referral disposition and underwriting action taken.

**310.I** Refer any risk involving a demand for damages. Treat informal demands as referral conditions when facts indicate a potential claim.

A demand for damages may precede formal claim activity. Central review assesses severity potential and coverage implications.

Document the demand details and the source of information. Record the referral decision in the underwriting file.

**310.J** Refer any risk with prior cancellation for underwriting reasons. Do not treat applicant explanation as sufficient without review.

Prior cancellation may indicate unacceptable conditions, undisclosed hazards, or adverse loss experience. Central review determines whether the underlying concern has been resolved.

Document the stated reason for prior cancellation. Record available verification and referral disposition.

**310.K** Refer any risk with prior nonrenewal for underwriting reasons. Hold renewal activity pending referral disposition.

Prior nonrenewal may reflect exposure characteristics outside carrier appetite. Central review evaluates the reason for nonrenewal and current conditions.

Document the prior nonrenewal reason and information source. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.L** Refer any risk declined by another carrier for hazard-related reasons. Do not infer acceptability from incomplete decline information.

A hazard-related decline may identify conditions requiring specialized review. Central review confirms whether the condition is within carrier appetite.

Document the known decline reason and applicant explanation. Record the referral disposition and any conditions imposed.

**310.M** Refer any risk with materially inconsistent application information. Suspend processing until the inconsistency is resolved.

Inconsistent information may indicate undisclosed exposure or unreliable eligibility data. Central review determines whether the discrepancy affects acceptability.

Document the conflicting information and verification efforts. Record the referral decision in the underwriting file.

**310.N** Refer any risk with suspected material misrepresentation. Do not issue coverage while the concern remains unresolved.

Material misrepresentation may affect eligibility, pricing, and carrier reliance on the submission. Central review determines the appropriate underwriting response.

Document the suspected misrepresentation and supporting facts. Record the referral disposition and final account action.

**310.O** Refer any risk where ownership cannot be verified. Do not bind coverage based on unverified insurable interest.

Unverified ownership may create coverage, fraud, and valuation concerns. Central review evaluates the available evidence of insurable interest.

Document the ownership information received and outstanding concerns. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.P** Refer any risk with a trust, estate, or similar ownership arrangement. Obtain referral direction before completing underwriting action.

Nonstandard ownership arrangements may affect named insured, insurable interest, and authority requirements. Central review confirms acceptable account structure.

Document the ownership arrangement and available supporting information. Record the referral disposition and required account setup.

**310.Q** Refer any risk with business activity conducted from the insured location. Do not classify incidental activity without referral authority approval.

Business activity may create premises, product, professional, or public liability exposure. Central review determines whether the activity is compatible with personal lines appetite.

Document the described activity and location use. Record the referral outcome and any restrictions.

**310.R** Refer any risk with commercial storage at the insured location. Hold coverage action pending review of the stored property and operations.

Commercial storage may increase fire, theft, premises liability, or traffic exposure. Central review evaluates the nature of the stored property.

Document the storage use and known materials present. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.S** Refer any risk with manufacturing, repair, or processing activity at the insured location. Do not proceed without referral authority direction.

Manufacturing, repair, or processing may create hazards outside personal lines appetite. Central review assesses equipment, materials, and public access.

Document the activity and any known equipment or materials. Record the referral outcome and underwriting restrictions.

**310.T** Refer any risk with regular public access to the insured location. Suspend binding authority pending referral disposition.

Public access may increase premises liability and security exposure. Central review evaluates the reason for access and controls in place.

Document the nature of public access and applicant explanation. Record the referral decision in the underwriting file.

**310.U** Refer any risk with a vacant or unoccupied location. Do not issue or continue coverage without referral authority approval.

Vacancy or lack of occupancy may increase undetected damage, vandalism, and maintenance concerns. Central review determines whether protective measures are adequate.

Document the occupancy status and known protective measures. Record the referral disposition and any requirements.

**310.V** Refer any risk with ongoing renovation or major repair activity. Hold coverage action until the exposure is reviewed.

Renovation and repair activity may alter fire, theft, liability, and valuation exposure. Central review evaluates work scope and site controls.

Document the described work and occupancy status. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.W** Refer any risk with structural damage that has not been repaired. Do not rely on verbal assurances regarding condition.

Unrepaired structural damage may indicate continuing property and liability exposure. Central review determines whether the location remains acceptable.

Document the damage description and known repair status. Record the referral disposition and required corrective action.

**310.X** Refer any risk with foundation movement, settlement, or cracking concerns. Suspend underwriting action pending referral review.

Foundation concerns may indicate material structural instability and recurring damage potential. Central review assesses condition, repair history, and current stability.

Document the reported condition and available repair information. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.Y** Refer any risk with roof damage, roof leakage, or unresolved roof repairs. Do not bind coverage pending referral disposition.

Roof concerns may increase water damage and property deterioration exposure. Central review evaluates repair status and condition information.

Document the known roof condition and repair history. Record the referral decision and any imposed requirements.

**310.Z** Refer any risk with recurring water intrusion. Do not treat temporary remediation as a final correction without review.

Recurring water intrusion may indicate unresolved construction, drainage, or maintenance concerns. Central review evaluates cause, repairs, and resulting damage.

Document the reported water source and corrective measures. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.AA** Refer any risk with plumbing leakage that remains unresolved. Hold binding authority pending referral direction.

Unresolved plumbing leakage may cause continuing water damage and mold concerns. Central review determines whether repairs adequately address the source.

Document the leakage history and known repairs. Record the referral outcome and required action.

**310.AB** Refer any risk with sewer backup, drain backup, or sanitation concerns. Do not complete underwriting action without referral review.

Backup conditions may indicate property damage, health, and maintenance exposure. Central review evaluates cause, remediation, and recurrence potential.

Document the reported condition and corrective measures. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.AC** Refer any risk with suspected mold, fungi, or rot. Suspend processing until referral authority provides direction.

Mold, fungi, and rot may indicate hidden moisture damage or inadequate maintenance. Central review evaluates severity, remediation, and remaining exposure.

Document the reported condition and source of information. Record the referral outcome and required follow-up.

**310.AD** Refer any risk with electrical hazards or outdated electrical components. Do not accept unsupported statements that conditions are safe.

Electrical concerns may increase fire and shock exposure. Central review evaluates the nature of the condition and corrective work.

Document the reported electrical condition and available repair information. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.AE** Refer any risk with heating equipment concerns. Hold underwriting action pending review of the reported condition.

Heating equipment concerns may create fire, smoke, or carbon monoxide exposure. Central review assesses condition, maintenance, and suitability.

Document the equipment concern and known corrective action. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.AF** Refer any risk with fuel storage or fuel leakage concerns. Do not bind coverage without referral authority approval.

Fuel concerns may create fire, environmental, and property damage exposure. Central review evaluates storage conditions and remediation status.

Document the fuel condition and available corrective information. Record the referral decision and any account restrictions.

**310.AG** Refer any risk with a wood-burning appliance or solid-fuel heating source. Suspend processing pending referral disposition.

Solid-fuel heating may increase fire and smoke loss potential. Central review evaluates installation, maintenance, and protective features.

Document the heating source and available condition information. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.AH** Refer any risk with an in-ground pool, above-ground pool, spa, or similar water feature. Do not issue coverage without referral review.

Water features may create drowning, premises liability, and maintenance exposure. Central review evaluates access controls and condition information.

Document the water feature and known protective measures. Record the referral disposition and any required action.

**310.AI** Refer any risk with a trampoline, climbing structure, or similar recreational apparatus. Hold binding authority pending referral direction.

Recreational apparatus may increase bodily injury liability exposure. Central review assesses ownership, condition, and access controls.

Document the apparatus and known safety measures. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.AJ** Refer any risk with an animal having aggressive behavior or a biting history. Do not bind coverage pending referral disposition.

Animal behavior concerns may create material liability exposure. Central review evaluates the reported conduct and household controls.

Document the animal information and reported behavior. Record the referral decision and any imposed restrictions.

**310.AK** Refer any risk with an exotic, wild, or restricted animal. Suspend underwriting action until referral authority responds.

Nontraditional animals may create specialized liability and property damage exposure. Central review determines whether the exposure is within carrier appetite.

Document the animal type and containment information. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.AL** Refer any risk with firearms used for business, instruction, sale, or organized activity. Do not classify the exposure as ordinary personal use.

Nonpersonal firearm activity may create elevated liability exposure. Central review evaluates the activity, storage, and public contact.

Document the described firearm activity and controls. Record the referral disposition and underwriting action.

**310.AM** Refer any risk with a recreational vehicle used for commercial activity. Hold issuance activity pending referral review.

Commercial use may create liability exposure outside personal lines appetite. Central review evaluates the activity and vehicle use.

Document the stated use and applicant explanation. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.AN** Refer any risk with vehicle use involving delivery, transportation, or passenger carriage. Do not bind coverage without referral authority approval.

Delivery and transportation activity may increase frequency and liability severity. Central review determines whether the use is eligible.

Document the vehicle use and known business connection. Record the referral disposition and any restrictions.

**310.AO** Refer any risk with an operator lacking a valid driving privilege. Suspend underwriting action pending referral direction.

Invalid driving privilege may indicate elevated accident, compliance, and eligibility concerns. Central review evaluates available operator information.

Document the known driving status and information source. Record the referral outcome in the underwriting file.

**310.AP** Refer any risk with an operator subject to a license restriction. Do not assume compliance with the restriction.

License restrictions may affect operator eligibility and loss potential. Central review determines whether the restriction is acceptable.

Document the restriction and available operator information. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.AQ** Refer any risk with a serious moving violation history. Hold binding authority pending central review.

Serious moving violations may indicate elevated driving behavior concerns. Central review evaluates the violation details and current account facts.

Document the known violation information and source. Record the referral outcome and underwriting action.

**310.AR** Refer any risk with alcohol-related driving conduct. Do not bind, renew, or broaden coverage without referral disposition.

Alcohol-related driving conduct may create significant liability and frequency concerns. Central review assesses the conduct and available rehabilitation information.

Document the reported conduct and information source. Record the referral decision in the underwriting file.

**310.AS** Refer any risk with vehicle modification affecting performance, safety, or value. Suspend processing pending referral review.

Vehicle modifications may alter valuation, repairability, and accident exposure. Central review evaluates the modification and account eligibility.

Document the modification details and stated purpose. Record the referral outcome and any restrictions.

**310.AT** Refer any risk with a collector, antique, custom, or unusual vehicle. Do not assign standard treatment without referral authority approval.

Unusual vehicles may require specialized valuation and usage review. Central review confirms whether the vehicle fits carrier appetite.

Document the vehicle description and stated use. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.AU** Refer any risk with property held primarily for rental use. Hold binding authority pending referral disposition.

Rental use may create premises liability, maintenance, and occupancy exposure. Central review evaluates the rental arrangement and property condition.

Document the stated rental use and occupancy information. Record the referral outcome and underwriting action.

**310.AV** Refer any risk with short-term lodging activity. Do not treat lodging activity as ordinary residential occupancy.

Short-term lodging may increase turnover, public access, and liability exposure. Central review evaluates the activity and location controls.

Document the lodging activity and occupancy details. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.AW** Refer any risk with shared occupancy not disclosed at application. Suspend processing until occupancy information is clarified.

Undisclosed shared occupancy may affect eligibility, liability, and property exposure. Central review determines whether the occupancy arrangement is acceptable.

Document the occupancy information and source. Record the referral outcome and any account changes.

**310.AX** Refer any risk with a location used for organized events or gatherings. Do not bind coverage without referral authority direction.

Organized events and gatherings may increase premises liability and crowd-related exposure. Central review evaluates the activity, frequency, and controls.

Document the described activity and anticipated attendance. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.AY** Refer any risk with prior fraud indicators or suspected fraudulent activity. Escalate immediately when available facts support the concern.

Fraud indicators may affect carrier reliance, claim integrity, and account eligibility. Central review evaluates the information under carrier fraud procedures.

Document the observed indicator and information source. Record the referral outcome without unsupported conclusions.

**310.AZ** Refer any risk with identity verification concerns. Do not issue coverage until referral authority confirms acceptable verification.

Identity concerns may indicate fraud, ownership, or insurable interest exposure. Central review determines whether available information supports account acceptance.

Document the verification concern and information received. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.BA** Refer any risk with materially adverse credit-related information used in underwriting. Hold processing pending referral direction.

Adverse credit-related information may require review for accuracy, applicability, or carrier treatment. Central review confirms appropriate underwriting handling.

Document the information source and account relevance. Record the referral outcome and any required correction.

**310.BB** Refer any risk with a prior carrier investigation involving the applicant or insured location. Do not rely on incomplete investigation information.

Prior investigation activity may indicate unresolved fraud, hazard, or eligibility concerns. Central review evaluates available facts before underwriting action.

Document the known investigation information and source. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.BC** Refer any risk with adverse inspection findings. Suspend binding authority until referral authority determines acceptability.

Adverse inspection findings may identify conditions not apparent from application information. Central review evaluates the condition and corrective action.

Document the inspection finding and available remediation information. Record the referral outcome and requirements.

**310.BD** Refer any risk where protective features are represented but cannot be verified. Do not apply underwriting credit or rely on the representation.

Unverified protective features may affect property loss potential and account classification. Central review determines whether verification is necessary for acceptance.

Document the represented feature and verification status. Record the referral disposition in the underwriting file.

**310.BE** Refer any risk with unusual construction, nonstandard materials, or unusual design features. Hold issuance activity pending referral review.

Unusual construction may affect fire resistance, repairability, valuation, and loss severity. Central review evaluates the construction characteristics and available support.

Document the construction information and source. Record the referral outcome and any special handling.

**310.BF** Refer any risk that presents a material hazard not otherwise addressed by routine underwriting. Escalate when available facts indicate uncertainty about carrier appetite.

Material hazards require consistent review and documented underwriting judgment. Central review determines whether the exposure is acceptable, restricted, or declined.

Document the hazard, available facts, and reason for referral. Record the final referral disposition in the underwriting file.

## Rule 320 — Conditions That Cannot Be Cleared

**320.1** Decline any risk with a known condition that materially increases expected loss and cannot be corrected before binding.

Such conditions defeat acceptable risk selection and cannot be cured through underwriting notes.

Document the condition, the source of the information, and the decline action in the underwriting file.

**320.2** Decline any risk with unrepaired structural damage affecting the dwelling, attached structure, or insured premises.

Structural damage may indicate ongoing instability, water intrusion, or loss potential beyond acceptable tolerance.

Document the observed damage, inspection findings, and supporting images or reports in the underwriting file.

**320.3** Decline any risk with a compromised foundation, visible settlement, active movement, or material cracking.

Foundation defects may impair the dwelling and create progressive damage exposure.

Document the defect, the source of the finding, and any available professional evaluation.

**320.4** Decline any risk with roof damage, roof deterioration, or active roof leakage that remains unresolved.

Roof defects create immediate weather loss exposure and may conceal related interior damage.

Document the roof condition, inspection evidence, and reason the condition cannot be cleared.

**320.5** Decline any risk with missing roof covering, unsecured roof materials, or visible roof openings.

Unsecured roof materials increase wind, water, and liability exposure.

Document the observed condition and retain available images or inspection notes.

**320.6** Decline any risk with active water intrusion into the dwelling or another insured structure.

Active water intrusion may cause concealed damage, mold conditions, and escalating property loss.

Document the location of the intrusion, the reported cause, and the disposition.

**320.7** Decline any risk with an unrepaired plumbing leak or evidence of ongoing plumbing failure.

Ongoing plumbing failure presents immediate water damage potential and may indicate deferred maintenance.

Document the leak source, visible damage, and information relied upon for the decision.

**320.8** Decline any risk with defective wiring, exposed conductors, overloaded electrical equipment, or unsafe electrical alterations.

Electrical defects create unacceptable fire and shock hazards.

Document the electrical condition, inspection findings, and any supporting contractor information.

**320.9** Decline any risk with a recalled electrical panel or another known electrical hazard that remains in service.

Known electrical hazards cannot be accepted while the hazardous component remains installed.

Document the equipment identified, the hazard source, and the decline action.

**320.10** Decline any risk with an unsafe heating appliance, venting defect, or active fuel leak.

Heating and fuel defects create fire, explosion, and carbon monoxide exposure.

Document the hazardous condition and retain available inspection or contractor records.

**320.11** Decline any risk with an inoperable or unsafe solid-fuel heating appliance.

Solid-fuel heating equipment requires safe installation, operation, and venting before the risk can be considered.

Document the appliance condition, the source of the finding, and the underwriting action.

**320.12** Decline any risk with an open fire hazard, unsafe combustibles storage, or visible ignition source near flammable materials.

Uncontrolled ignition hazards create unacceptable fire severity potential.

Document the hazard, its location, and the evidence supporting the decision.

**320.13** Decline any risk with an unsafe fireplace, chimney, flue, or venting system.

Defective combustion venting may cause fire damage or hazardous indoor conditions.

Document the condition and retain available inspection findings or images.

**320.14** Decline any risk with prior fire damage that has not been fully repaired.

Unrepaired fire damage may involve structural impairment, electrical defects, or concealed smoke damage.

Document the fire damage, repair status, and source of the information.

**320.15** Decline any risk with active mold, fungal growth, rot, or moisture damage that remains unresolved.

Moisture-related damage may indicate ongoing water intrusion or inadequate maintenance.

Document the affected area, apparent cause, and available inspection evidence.

**320.16** Decline any risk with deteriorated exterior siding, wall openings, or unsecured building envelope components.

Building envelope defects increase weather damage potential and may permit ongoing interior deterioration.

Document the exterior condition and retain available images or inspection notes.

**320.17** Decline any risk with an unsafe deck, porch, balcony, stairway, railing, or elevated walking surface.

Unsafe elevated surfaces create bodily injury and property damage exposure.

Document the unsafe feature, the location, and the basis for the decision.

**320.18** Decline any risk with a swimming pool, spa, pond, or water feature that lacks required safety controls.

Unsafe water features present severe premises liability exposure.

Document the water feature condition and retain available inspection evidence.

**320.19** Decline any risk with an unsecured trampoline, climbing structure, recreational device, or similar hazard.

Uncontrolled recreational hazards increase the likelihood of bodily injury claims.

Document the hazard, its location, and the underwriting disposition.

**320.20** Decline any risk with an aggressive animal, a history of animal injury, or an animal that presents unacceptable liability exposure.

Animal behavior and prior injury history may indicate recurring liability potential.

Document the animal exposure, the source of the information, and the decline action.

**320.21** Decline any risk with an unsafe premises condition that creates a foreseeable bodily injury hazard.

Premises hazards that cannot be corrected before binding are not acceptable.

Document the hazard, the affected area, and the reason the condition cannot be cleared.

**320.22** Decline any risk with abandoned vehicles, derelict equipment, or hazardous debris on the insured premises.

Abandoned property may create fire, injury, environmental, or municipal enforcement exposure.

Document the property observed and retain available images or inspection notes.

**320.23** Decline any risk with ongoing construction, demolition, major renovation, or material alteration.

Active work changes the property exposure and may involve incomplete systems or unprotected areas.

Document the work activity, the affected property area, and the underwriting action.

**320.24** Decline any risk with vacant, neglected, or materially unoccupied premises.

Vacancy and neglect increase the potential for undetected loss, vandalism, and deterioration.

Document the occupancy condition and the information supporting the determination.

**320.25** Decline any risk with unlawful occupancy, disputed possession, or an unresolved ownership conflict.

Unresolved occupancy or ownership disputes create coverage, maintenance, and liability uncertainty.

Document the reported dispute and the source of the information.

**320.26** Decline any risk with prior loss damage that remains unrepaired.

Unrepaired loss damage may indicate continuing exposure and insufficient restoration of the property.

Document the prior damage, repair status, and any available claim or inspection information.

**320.27** Decline any risk with repeated water damage, repeated fire damage, or another recurring loss cause that remains unresolved.

Recurring loss activity indicates that the underlying cause has not been effectively addressed.

Document the loss pattern, the unresolved cause, and the underwriting decision.

**320.28** Decline any risk with evidence of misrepresentation, altered records, or material inconsistencies in underwriting information.

Reliable information is required to evaluate eligibility and exposure.

Document the inconsistency, the source record, and the disposition without speculation.

**320.29** Decline any risk when the applicant refuses access needed to verify material property conditions.

Unverified material conditions prevent reliable assessment of the risk.

Document the requested access, the refusal, and the underwriting action.

**320.30** Decline any risk when material underwriting information cannot be obtained or verified.

Incomplete or unverifiable information prevents acceptable risk selection.

Document the missing information, efforts to obtain it, and the final disposition.

**320.31** Decline any risk with known criminal activity conducted from the insured premises.

Criminal activity creates heightened property, liability, and moral hazard exposure.

Document the reported activity, the information source, and the underwriting action.

**320.32** Decline any risk with hazardous materials, unlawful substances, or contamination that remains present at the insured premises.

Hazardous conditions may create severe property damage, bodily injury, and environmental exposure.

Document the material or condition reported and retain available supporting evidence.

**320.33** Decline any risk with underground storage concerns, fuel contamination, or visible petroleum release.

Petroleum exposure may create costly remediation and property damage potential.

Document the reported condition, location, and source of the information.

**320.34** Decline any risk with sinkhole activity, earth movement damage, or unstable ground conditions that remain unresolved.

Ground instability may cause progressive structural damage and sudden loss.

Document the condition, inspection findings, and available expert information.

**320.35** Decline any risk with flood damage, drainage failure, or standing water conditions that remain unresolved.

Unresolved water conditions may produce recurring property damage and unsafe premises conditions.

Document the water source, affected area, and reason the risk cannot be cleared.

**320.36** Decline any risk with damaged retaining walls, failing slopes, or unsafe grading.

Retaining and grading defects may contribute to ground movement, water intrusion, and bodily injury exposure.

Document the observed condition and retain available inspection evidence.

**320.37** Decline any risk with trees, limbs, vegetation, or exterior hazards that present an immediate threat to insured property.

Immediate exterior hazards may cause property damage or bodily injury before corrective action occurs.

Document the hazard location, the property at risk, and the underwriting disposition.

**320.38** Decline any risk with unrepaired damage caused by wind, hail, storm, or another weather event.

Weather damage that remains open may worsen and may affect the integrity of the dwelling.

Document the damage, repair status, and source of the information.

**320.39** Suspend binding for storm-exposed risks when forecast landfall is within 48 hours.

Forecast landfall conditions may change rapidly and prevent reliable evaluation of the property condition.

Document the forecast information relied upon and the binding suspension action.

**320.40** Decline any risk located at a property with restricted emergency access or unsafe access conditions.

Restricted access may delay emergency response and increase loss severity.

Document the access condition, the source of the finding, and the underwriting action.

**320.41** Decline any risk with blocked driveways, obstructed fire access, or inaccessible insured structures.

Obstructed access can delay fire suppression, rescue, and loss mitigation.

Document the obstruction and retain available images or inspection notes.

**320.42** Decline any risk with an inoperable protective device represented as present for underwriting purposes.

Protection credits and eligibility determinations require reliable protective features.

Document the represented device, the verification result, and the disposition.

**320.43** Decline any risk with an alarm, sprinkler, or protective system that is impaired and cannot be restored before binding.

Impaired protective systems may materially increase property loss severity.

Document the impairment, the affected system, and the underwriting action.

**320.44** Decline any risk with a material protection gap that prevents acceptable property evaluation.

A material protection gap may leave the carrier exposed to avoidable loss conditions.

Document the gap, the requested correction, and the reason clearance was not obtained.

**320.45** Decline any risk with a replacement cost concern that cannot be resolved using reliable property information.

Unresolved valuation concerns may produce inadequate insurance and claim handling uncertainty.

Document the valuation concern, the information reviewed, and the disposition.

**320.46** Decline any risk when the requested insurance does not satisfy the applicable coinsurance threshold.

Inadequate insurance may create unacceptable valuation and loss settlement concerns.

Document the valuation basis, the coinsurance threshold determination, and the underwriting action.

**320.47** Decline any risk with material commercial activity conducted from the insured premises.

Commercial activity may alter occupancy, liability, property, and traffic exposure.

Document the activity, the premises area involved, and the source of the information.

**320.48** Decline any risk with manufacturing, repair, storage, or service activity that creates commercial exposure.

Business operations may introduce hazards outside acceptable personal lines underwriting parameters.

Document the activity observed or reported and the underwriting disposition.

**320.49** Decline any risk with short-term occupancy activity that creates unacceptable turnover or liability exposure.

Frequent occupancy changes may increase property damage, theft, and premises liability potential.

Document the occupancy activity and the information relied upon for the decision.

**320.50** Decline any risk with an unapproved tenant arrangement or occupancy use that cannot be verified.

Unverified occupancy arrangements may alter the nature of the insured risk.

Document the reported occupancy use, verification efforts, and final disposition.

**320.51** Decline any risk with property maintenance conditions showing material neglect.

Material neglect may indicate a broader inability or unwillingness to maintain the insured property.

Document the maintenance conditions and retain available images or inspection findings.

**320.52** Decline any risk with a known condition that requires repair by a qualified professional but lacks credible evidence of completed repair.

Professional repair requirements must be supported by reliable evidence before the risk can be accepted.

Document the required repair, the missing evidence, and the underwriting action.

**320.53** Decline any risk when a material condition remains disputed and cannot be resolved through reliable evidence.

Unresolved disputes prevent a dependable assessment of eligibility and exposure.

Document the disputed condition, the evidence reviewed, and the final disposition.

**320.54** Refer any risk with a condition not addressed in this rule when the condition cannot be cleared before binding.

Unlisted conditions may present material exposure requiring underwriting judgment outside routine handling.

Document the condition, the basis for referral, and the underwriting decision.

## Rule 400 — Endorsement Attachment Rules

**400.A** Attach endorsements only when the risk facts support the requested coverage. Confirm that the attachment does not conflict with the underwriting decision.

Accurate attachment preserves the intended risk transfer. Unsupported attachments create avoidable claim handling issues.

Record the risk facts that support the attachment. Record the endorsement disposition in the underwriting file.

**400.B** Review every requested endorsement before binding or renewing the risk. Resolve unclear requests before completing the transaction.

Clear review prevents unintended coverage changes. Early resolution reduces later correction activity.

Record the request source and the review outcome. Record any clarification obtained from the producer or applicant.

**400.C** Attach endorsements only to risks eligible for the applicable coverage. Refer risks with uncertain eligibility.

Eligibility controls maintain consistent underwriting treatment. Referral prevents unauthorized exceptions.

Record the eligibility basis for the attachment. Record the referral response when referral is required.

**400.D** Match each endorsement to the named insured, location, and insured property involved. Do not attach an endorsement to an unrelated exposure.

Correct matching supports reliable coverage administration. Incorrect matching can create coverage gaps or unintended extensions.

Record the insured interest and exposure matched to the endorsement. Record any correction made before issuance.

**400.E** Verify that the endorsement request reflects the current risk condition. Suspend attachment when material facts remain unresolved.

Current risk information supports sound attachment decisions. Unresolved facts can affect eligibility and rating.

Record the facts reviewed for currency. Record the reason for any suspended request.

**400.F** Attach endorsements only after confirming that required underwriting information is available. Refer requests supported by incomplete or conflicting information.

Complete information supports consistent risk selection. Conflicting information may indicate an unrecognized hazard.

Record the information reviewed. Record the disposition of incomplete or conflicting information.

**400.G** Confirm that endorsement wording is appropriate for the requested coverage intent. Do not use an endorsement to cure an ineligible risk.

Proper wording preserves the carrier’s intended coverage position. Using an endorsement as a workaround defeats eligibility controls.

Record the coverage intent considered. Record the reason the selected endorsement addresses that intent.

**400.H** Apply endorsements consistently across comparable risks. Refer requests that require treatment outside normal underwriting practice.

Consistent handling supports equitable underwriting decisions. Exceptions require accountable review.

Record the comparable risk factors considered. Record the authority supporting any exception.

**400.I** Verify ownership, occupancy, and use before attaching property-related endorsements. Refer risks with uncertain insurable interest.

Ownership and use affect the scope of the exposure. Uncertain insurable interest can compromise the attachment decision.

Record the ownership, occupancy, and use reviewed. Record the resolution of any insurable interest concern.

**400.J** Confirm that location information supports the endorsement attachment. Do not attach location-specific coverage when the location data is incomplete.

Location data identifies the exposure receiving coverage. Incomplete location data can cause incorrect issuance.

Record the location information reviewed. Record any correction to location data.

**400.K** Review prior loss information when the requested endorsement could affect loss exposure. Refer adverse loss patterns that alter underwriting acceptability.

Loss history may reveal hazards not evident from the application. Adverse patterns may require a different underwriting response.

Record the loss information considered. Record the referral outcome when adverse history is identified.

**400.L** Confirm that risk protection features support the requested endorsement. Do not rely on unverified protective devices.

Protection features can affect hazard severity and claim outcomes. Unverified devices do not support a reliable underwriting decision.

Record the protection features reviewed. Record the source used to verify those features.

**400.M** Attach water-related endorsements only after evaluating water sources and drainage conditions. Refer risks with unresolved water intrusion concerns.

Water conditions can materially affect property damage potential. Unresolved conditions may require further underwriting review.

Record the water and drainage conditions reviewed. Record the disposition of identified concerns.

**400.N** Attach the drain backup sublimit only when the risk satisfies applicable underwriting requirements. Refer risks with repeated drainage or sewer concerns.

Drainage-related losses can indicate ongoing maintenance or location hazards. Referral supports appropriate review of recurring conditions.

Record the conditions supporting the drain backup sublimit. Record the referral result for adverse drainage history.

**400.O** Review plumbing condition before attaching endorsements affected by water discharge exposure. Refer risks with signs of deterioration or active leakage.

Plumbing condition influences the potential for sudden and repeated water damage. Deterioration may require underwriting action before attachment.

Record the plumbing condition reviewed. Record the action taken for any adverse condition.

**400.P** Confirm that roof condition supports endorsements affected by weather-related property damage. Refer risks with unresolved roof deterioration.

Roof condition affects the likelihood and severity of weather losses. Visible deterioration may change the underwriting decision.

Record the roof condition reviewed. Record the resolution of roof concerns.

**400.Q** Review electrical condition before attaching endorsements affected by fire exposure. Refer risks with uncorrected electrical hazards.

Electrical hazards can increase fire loss potential. Uncorrected conditions require accountable underwriting review.

Record the electrical condition reviewed. Record the disposition of identified hazards.

**400.R** Review heating condition before attaching endorsements affected by fire or freeze exposure. Refer risks with unsafe or inadequately maintained heating equipment.

Heating condition can affect fire, smoke, and freeze loss potential. Unsafe equipment may require correction before attachment.

Record the heating condition reviewed. Record any required corrective action.

**400.S** Confirm that the risk use remains within personal lines underwriting appetite. Refer risks with commercial, agricultural, or unusual use.

Nonstandard use can create exposures outside intended personal lines coverage. Referral prevents unintended acceptance of unsuitable risks.

Record the use reviewed. Record the referral decision for unusual use.

**400.T** Review vacancy, unoccupancy, and seasonal use before attaching endorsements affected by property supervision. Refer risks with uncertain occupancy status.

Occupancy affects maintenance, discovery of damage, and loss severity. Uncertain status prevents reliable underwriting evaluation.

Record the occupancy status reviewed. Record the disposition of any vacancy concern.

**400.U** Confirm that requested liability-related endorsements align with household and premises exposures. Refer risks with unusual activities or liability indicators.

Liability exposure can arise from activities not apparent from property details. Unusual indicators require further underwriting assessment.

Record the household and premises exposures reviewed. Record the referral result for unusual activities.

**400.V** Attach animal-related endorsements only when animal information supports eligibility. Refer animals with adverse history, restricted traits, or uncertain supervision.

Animal exposures can create significant liability concerns. Reliable information is necessary for consistent eligibility review.

Record the animal information reviewed. Record the disposition of adverse history or supervision concerns.

**400.W** Review recreational equipment exposures before attaching related endorsements. Refer equipment with unusual use, storage, or operator concerns.

Recreational equipment can introduce hazards beyond ordinary premises exposure. Unusual use may require specialized underwriting review.

Record the equipment exposure reviewed. Record the action taken for identified concerns.

**400.X** Confirm that accessory structures support the requested endorsement attachment. Refer structures with hazardous condition, unusual use, or uncertain ownership.

Accessory structures can create distinct property and liability exposures. Uncertain facts can affect eligibility and coverage intent.

Record the structure details reviewed. Record the resolution of ownership or condition concerns.

**400.Y** Review construction, renovation, and repair activity before attaching endorsements affected by building condition. Refer active work that changes the risk profile.

Construction activity can increase fire, theft, and liability exposure. Material changes require current underwriting evaluation.

Record the activity reviewed. Record the referral outcome for active work.

**400.Z** Confirm that the requested endorsement does not duplicate existing coverage. Correct duplicate attachment before issuance.

Duplicate coverage can create confusion during claim handling. Correction preserves clear coverage administration.

Record the coverage comparison performed. Record the correction of any duplicate attachment.

**400.AA** Attach endorsements that restrict coverage only after confirming that the restriction is appropriate for the risk. Refer requests that materially alter the expected coverage position.

Restrictions must align with the underwriting rationale. Material changes require accountable approval.

Record the reason for the restriction. Record the authority supporting the attachment.

**400.AB** Confirm that the endorsement effective date aligns with the underwriting transaction. Do not use an endorsement to retroactively address a known loss circumstance.

Effective attachment supports orderly coverage administration. Retroactive changes can create claim handling disputes.

Record the transaction facts supporting the effective date. Record the disposition of known loss circumstances.

**400.AC** Review pending claims before attaching endorsements that could affect related coverage. Refer requests involving active claim uncertainty.

Pending claims may involve facts relevant to the requested attachment. Active uncertainty requires coordinated underwriting review.

Record the claim information considered. Record the referral decision for pending claim concerns.

**400.AD** Confirm that prior underwriting restrictions have been reviewed before changing an endorsement attachment. Do not remove a restriction without documented support.

Prior restrictions may reflect unresolved hazards or prior loss experience. Documented review prevents inadvertent expansion of coverage.

Record the prior restriction reviewed. Record the support for any change.

**400.AE** Attach mold-related coverage only when the risk supports the requested treatment. Review the fungi, wet or dry rot, or bacteria aggregate limit for alignment with the risk.

Moisture and maintenance conditions can affect mold-related exposure. Clear alignment supports consistent claim handling.

Record the conditions reviewed for mold-related exposure. Record the basis for the fungi, wet or dry rot, or bacteria aggregate limit treatment.

**400.AF** Confirm that building ordinance exposure has been evaluated before attaching related coverage. Review the code upgrade percentage for alignment with the property characteristics.

Building changes after loss can increase repair obligations. Property characteristics affect the appropriateness of ordinance coverage.

Record the property characteristics reviewed. Record the basis for the code upgrade percentage treatment.

**400.AG** Review extended replacement cost requests for consistency with the dwelling exposure. Confirm that the additional amount percentage aligns with underwriting requirements.

Replacement cost extensions can materially affect loss severity. Alignment supports appropriate coverage administration.

Record the dwelling information reviewed. Record the basis for the additional amount percentage treatment.

**400.AH** Confirm that earthquake-related attachments reflect the risk location and construction characteristics. Review the quake deductible before completing the attachment.

Earthquake exposure depends on location, construction, and property condition. Deductible treatment must support the intended risk selection.

Record the location and construction characteristics reviewed. Record the quake deductible treatment in the underwriting file.

**400.AI** Attach personal property endorsements only when the described property and ownership information are credible. Refer unusual property concentrations or uncertain ownership.

Property concentration can increase theft and valuation exposure. Ownership uncertainty can affect claim administration.

Record the property description and ownership information reviewed. Record the disposition of unusual concentrations.

**400.AJ** Review valuation information before attaching endorsements affected by replacement cost exposure. Refer values that appear unsupported or inconsistent with the risk.

Reliable valuation supports appropriate underwriting and claim settlement expectations. Unsupported values can create material coverage concerns.

Record the valuation information reviewed. Record the resolution of valuation concerns.
