**400.AK** Confirm that special property characteristics have been evaluated before attaching related endorsements. Refer risks with features outside normal underwriting appetite.

Special characteristics may introduce unique maintenance or repair exposures. Referral supports appropriate technical review.

Record the property characteristics reviewed. Record the referral outcome for unusual features.

**400.AL** Review protective safeguards before attaching endorsements that rely on those safeguards. Do not attach coverage based on safeguards that are not maintained.

Safeguard reliability affects the expected severity of covered losses. Poor maintenance reduces the value of protection.

Record the safeguards reviewed. Record the maintenance information supporting the attachment.

**400.AM** Confirm that prior inspection findings have been resolved before attaching endorsements affected by those findings. Refer unresolved findings that may change risk acceptability.

Inspection findings can identify hazards requiring correction. Unresolved hazards may invalidate the basis for attachment.

Record the findings reviewed. Record the resolution or referral for each material finding.

**400.AN** Treat an inspection report as valid for 6 months. Obtain current inspection information when the report is no longer valid.

Inspection information must reasonably reflect present risk conditions. Stale information may not support an accurate attachment decision.

Record the inspection report reviewed and its validity status. Record the source of any updated inspection information.

**400.AO** Confirm that inspection observations match the risk information used for endorsement attachment. Refer material inconsistencies between inspection findings and submitted information.

Consistent information supports reliable underwriting conclusions. Material inconsistencies can indicate undisclosed hazards.

Record the inspection observations compared. Record the resolution of each material inconsistency.

**400.AP** Review photographs used to support endorsement attachment for relevance and clarity. Do not rely on images that do not reasonably identify the exposure.

Useful images can confirm property condition and risk features. Unclear images do not support a dependable underwriting decision.

Record the images reviewed. Record the reason any image was rejected.

**400.AQ** Confirm that any requested coverage expansion has a documented underwriting basis. Refer expansions that exceed normal authority.

Coverage expansions can materially alter expected loss exposure. Authority review preserves underwriting discipline.

Record the basis for the expansion. Record the approval or referral result.

**400.AR** Apply coverage limitations when risk conditions warrant restricted treatment. Refer requests to remove limitations without evidence of improved conditions.

Limitations may be necessary to address identified hazards. Improved conditions must be verified before restrictions are removed.

Record the conditions supporting the limitation. Record the evidence reviewed for any removal request.

**400.AS** Confirm that endorsement language does not create an unintended coverage grant. Refer unclear wording or conflicting attachment instructions.

Clear language supports consistent interpretation during claim handling. Conflicting instructions can create avoidable disputes.

Record the wording reviewed. Record the resolution of unclear or conflicting instructions.

**400.AT** Review producer-submitted instructions for completeness before attaching endorsements. Seek clarification when the instructions do not identify the requested coverage.

Incomplete instructions can result in incorrect attachment. Clarification protects against unintended coverage changes.

Record the instructions reviewed. Record the clarification obtained before issuance.

**400.AU** Confirm that applicant representations support the requested endorsement attachment. Refer material discrepancies between representations and available risk information.

Applicant representations are a key underwriting input. Material discrepancies may require further investigation.

Record the representations reviewed. Record the disposition of discrepancies.

**400.AV** Review cancellation, nonrenewal, and prior carrier action when relevant to the requested endorsement. Refer adverse prior carrier action that may indicate an unresolved hazard.

Prior carrier action can reveal underwriting concerns not otherwise apparent. Adverse action requires careful evaluation.

Record the prior carrier information reviewed. Record the referral result for adverse action.

**400.AW** Confirm that the risk has no unresolved condition that would make the endorsement inappropriate. Suspend issuance until material conditions are resolved.

Unresolved conditions can undermine the basis for attachment. Suspension prevents premature coverage changes.

Record the unresolved condition. Record the action required before issuance.

**400.AX** Attach endorsements through approved underwriting processing controls. Do not bypass required review or approval activity.

Processing controls support accurate and authorized issuance. Bypassing controls increases operational and coverage error risk.

Record the completed review activity. Record the authority for the attachment.

**400.AY** Confirm that endorsement changes are communicated through the approved servicing process. Refer requests received through unclear or unauthorized channels.

Controlled communication reduces the risk of unauthorized changes. Clear request channels support reliable audit activity.

Record the request channel used. Record the disposition of unclear or unauthorized requests.

**400.AZ** Review endorsement attachment after processing to confirm that the intended coverage change appears correctly. Correct any discrepancy promptly.

Post-processing review identifies errors before they affect claim handling. Prompt correction preserves accurate coverage records.

Record the confirmation review. Record the correction of any discrepancy.

**400.BA** Confirm that cancellation-related endorsement changes reflect the applicable underwriting decision. Refer requests that conflict with the current risk disposition.

Cancellation activity can affect the scope of coverage administration. Conflicting changes require review before processing.

Record the risk disposition reviewed. Record the resolution of conflicting requests.

**400.BB** Review renewal-related endorsements using current risk information. Do not automatically continue an attachment when current facts do not support it.

Renewal is an opportunity to reassess exposure changes. Automatic continuation can preserve unsuitable coverage.

Record the current information reviewed. Record the decision to continue, revise, or remove the attachment.

**400.BC** Confirm that endorsement removal is appropriate for the current risk and coverage intent. Refer removals that may leave an unresolved coverage concern.

Removal can alter protection expected by the insured or producer. Review ensures that the resulting coverage position is understood.

Record the reason for removal. Record the resolution of any resulting coverage concern.

**400.BD** Attach endorsements affecting deductibles only when the selected treatment is supported by underwriting requirements. Refer deductible requests outside normal authority.

Deductible treatment influences loss participation and risk selection. Authority review is necessary for unusual requests.

Record the deductible treatment selected. Record the authority supporting the attachment.

**400.BE** Confirm that endorsement attachment does not obscure a material underwriting concern. Refer risks where the requested attachment appears intended to avoid risk evaluation.

Endorsements must not replace sound risk selection. Concealed concerns can create unacceptable exposure.

Record the underwriting concern identified. Record the referral outcome.

**400.BF** Review all material risk changes before modifying endorsement attachments. Refer changes that alter occupancy, use, condition, or protection.

Material changes can affect eligibility and expected loss severity. Review ensures that attachment remains appropriate.

Record the material change reviewed. Record the disposition of the requested modification.

**400.BG** Confirm that the final endorsement set reflects the approved underwriting decision. Correct attachments that do not match the approved disposition.

The final endorsement set must accurately implement the underwriting decision. Mismatched attachments create operational and claim risk.

Record the approved disposition reviewed. Record any correction made to the final endorsement set.

**400.BH** Maintain a clear underwriting record for every endorsement attachment, revision, or removal. Include the facts, rationale, and authority supporting the action.

A clear record supports later review and consistent claim handling. Complete documentation demonstrates compliance with underwriting controls.

Record the action taken in the underwriting file. Record the supporting facts, rationale, and authority.

**400.BI** Refer any risk that presents an endorsement issue not addressed by established underwriting guidance. Do not issue the attachment until authorized direction is received.

Unaddressed issues may involve unintended coverage or risk selection concerns. Authorized direction preserves consistent carrier treatment.

Record the issue requiring referral. Record the authorized direction and final disposition.

## Rule 410 — Deductible Options

**410.A** Set the Section I all-other-perils deductible at no less than $500.

Use the deductible floor to preserve intended risk participation.

Document the selected deductible in the underwriting record.

**410.B** Apply deductible options only where the risk characteristics support the selection.

Deductibles must align with the carrier's expected loss participation.

Document the risk characteristics supporting the option.

**410.C** Verify that the deductible shown in the submission is clear and unambiguous.

Unclear deductible information creates rating and coverage administration error.

Document the verified deductible terms.

**410.D** Refer any risk with conflicting deductible information.

Conflicting information may indicate an unsupported coverage configuration.

Document the conflicting information and referral outcome.

**410.E** Use the deductible applicable to the coverage part being underwritten.

Coverage parts may have distinct deductible treatment.

Document the deductible assigned to each applicable coverage part.

**410.F** Do not infer a deductible that is not shown in the submission.

Assumptions can produce an unintended coverage result.

Document the request for clarification where needed.

**410.G** Confirm that the applicant has selected an available deductible option.

Selections outside available options require underwriting review.

Document the selected option and underwriting disposition.

**410.H** Refer any request to retain a deductible option that is no longer available.

Legacy selections may not fit current underwriting intent.

Document the prior selection and referral decision.

**410.I** Review deductible selections with the same care as other material underwriting terms.

The deductible affects both insured participation and claim handling.

Document the review in the file narrative.

**410.J** Do not alter a deductible solely to resolve an unrelated underwriting concern.

Deductible changes must reflect the risk and available options.

Document the reason for any deductible revision.

**410.K** Confirm that the deductible selection is consistent across all submission materials.

Inconsistent records can cause issuance error.

Document the materials reviewed and the resolved selection.

**410.L** Refer any risk where the requested deductible appears unsuitable for the exposure.

An unsuitable deductible may create adverse claim participation expectations.

Document the exposure concern and underwriting action.

**410.M** Apply the named storm deductible minimum where a named storm deductible is requested or required.

Named storm treatment must remain consistent with the applicable risk review.

Document the named storm deductible selection.

**410.N** Apply the wind deductible ceiling when reviewing a wind deductible request.

Wind deductible treatment must remain within available underwriting parameters.

Document the wind deductible reviewed.

**410.O** Confirm whether a deductible applies separately to a covered cause of loss.

Cause-of-loss treatment may affect claim adjustment expectations.

Document the applicable deductible treatment.

**410.P** Refer any risk requiring an exception to standard deductible handling.

Exceptions require underwriting authority and clear file support.

Document the exception request and approval.

**410.Q** Do not use a deductible option to offset unacceptable property conditions.

Deductible selection does not cure adverse physical risk characteristics.

Document the property condition and underwriting disposition.

**410.R** Review deductible selections for consistency with occupancy information.

Occupancy can affect the suitability of the insured's retained loss participation.

Document the occupancy considered.

**410.S** Review deductible selections for consistency with construction information.

Construction characteristics may affect expected claim severity.

Document the construction information reviewed.

**410.T** Review deductible selections for consistency with protection information.

Protection characteristics may affect the appropriateness of the requested option.

Document the protection information reviewed.

**410.U** Confirm that the deductible is displayed in the rating information.

The issued terms must match the terms evaluated by underwriting.

Document the rating record reviewed.

**410.V** Refer any risk where rating information does not match the requested deductible.

A rating mismatch can result in incorrect premium or coverage terms.

Document the mismatch and correction.

**410.W** Do not substitute a different deductible without an authorized selection.

Unauthorized substitution can create insured expectation issues.

Document the source of the authorized selection.

**410.X** Verify that any deductible change is supported by current risk information.

Changed conditions may affect the appropriateness of the revised option.

Document the information supporting the change.

**410.Y** Review deductible changes requested after underwriting review.

Late changes may alter the risk acceptance decision.

Document the requested change and final disposition.

**410.Z** Refer any deductible request that is contingent upon an unverified risk feature.

Unverified conditions cannot support a final underwriting decision.

Document the pending verification requirement.

**410.AA** Apply deductible treatment consistently for risks with similar characteristics.

Consistent handling supports underwriting discipline.

Document any material basis for different treatment.

**410.AB** Do not approve a deductible exception based solely on premium preference.

Deductible exceptions must be supported by underwriting considerations.

Document the underwriting basis for the decision.

**410.AC** Consider prior loss information when evaluating a deductible request.

Loss experience may indicate whether the requested retained participation is suitable.

Document the loss information considered.

**410.AD** Refer any risk with loss information that conflicts with the requested deductible.

The conflict may require a different underwriting approach.

Document the conflict and referral result.

**410.AE** Confirm that the deductible selection is suitable for the described property use.

Property use may affect the insured's ability to absorb a covered loss.

Document the property use reviewed.

**410.AF** Confirm that the deductible selection is suitable for the described occupancy.

Occupancy may influence claim frequency and insured expectations.

Document the occupancy rationale.

**410.AG** Review deductible requests involving vacant or unoccupied property.

Such property may present claim handling concerns not addressed by deductible selection.

Document the condition and underwriting decision.

**410.AH** Review deductible requests involving renovation or repair activity.

Property work can change the exposure during the underwriting review.

Document the activity considered.

**410.AI** Refer any risk where the requested deductible is inconsistent with the condition of the property.

Property condition may warrant additional underwriting evaluation.

Document the condition concern and referral outcome.

**410.AJ** Confirm that the deductible applies to the intended location.

Location errors can affect the issued coverage terms.

Document the location reviewed.

**410.AK** Refer any submission with multiple locations and unclear deductible application.

Each location must have clear deductible treatment.

Document the clarification received.

**410.AL** Do not combine deductible terms from separate coverage proposals.

Separate proposals may have different underwriting assumptions.

Document the proposal used for the final selection.

**410.AM** Confirm that deductible wording is consistent with the intended cause-of-loss treatment.

Wording must support predictable claim administration.

Document the wording reviewed.

**410.AN** Refer any risk with manuscript deductible language.

Manuscript language requires underwriting and operational review.

Document the manuscript language and approval.

**410.AO** Do not accept ambiguous deductible endorsements without clarification.

Ambiguity can lead to inconsistent claim outcomes.

Document the clarification and final handling.

**410.AP** Verify that the deductible selection is reflected in all issuance instructions.

Issuance instructions must preserve the underwriting decision.

Document the instructions provided.

**410.AQ** Refer any risk where issuance instructions omit the deductible selection.

An omitted selection can result in an incorrect policy record.

Document the omission and correction.

**410.AR** Review deductible selections when coverage is added or removed.

Coverage changes may alter applicable deductible treatment.

Document the coverage change and deductible review.

**410.AS** Review deductible selections when a location is added or removed.

Location changes may affect the deductible application.

Document the location change and final selection.

**410.AT** Confirm that deductible changes are effective only as authorized.

Unauthorized effective treatment can create administrative error.

Document the authorization supporting the change.

**410.AU** Refer any request to apply a deductible retroactively.

Retroactive treatment requires careful review of coverage and claim implications.

Document the request and disposition.

**410.AV** Do not use claim information alone to revise a deductible after loss.

Post-loss deductible changes can create coverage disputes.

Document any claim-related inquiry and response.

**410.AW** Maintain a clear record of deductible communications.

Clear communication supports accurate issuance and servicing.

Document material communications in the underwriting file.

**410.AX** Confirm that the applicant's requested deductible is captured before binding.

Binding with an unresolved deductible selection can create avoidable error.

Document the final selection before binding action.

**410.AY** Refer any bound risk with an unresolved deductible discrepancy.

The discrepancy must be resolved promptly to preserve accurate records.

Document the discrepancy and corrective action.

**410.AZ** Review deductible options when material exposure information changes.

Changed exposure information may affect the earlier underwriting decision.

Document the changed information and review outcome.

**410.BA** Do not treat a deductible as a substitute for required risk controls.

Risk controls address conditions that deductible selection does not resolve.

Document any required risk control action.

**410.BB** Refer any risk where the applicant declines a deductible option required by underwriting.

The requested terms may not meet the carrier's risk acceptance requirements.

Document the declined option and referral outcome.

**410.BC** Confirm that the deductible selection is consistent with any authorized exception.

Authorized exceptions must be applied exactly as approved.

Document the exception authority and applied terms.

**410.BD** Do not extend an authorized deductible exception beyond its stated scope.

Exceptions are limited to the risk circumstances reviewed.

Document the scope of the exception.

**410.BE** Refer any request to transfer a deductible exception to another risk.

Each risk requires its own underwriting evaluation.

Document the transfer request and decision.

**410.BF** Confirm that the deductible selection is understandable to servicing staff.

Clear terms support accurate policy maintenance and claim handling.

Document any clarification provided.

**410.BG** Escalate unresolved deductible questions before final underwriting action.

Unresolved questions can affect the carrier's intended coverage terms.

Document the question and resolved guidance.

**410.BH** Retain support for the deductible decision in the underwriting file.

The file must show how the final option was selected.

Document the basis for the final decision.

**410.BI** Complete deductible review before releasing the risk for issuance.

Final review prevents discrepancies between underwriting intent and issued terms.

Document completion of the deductible review.

## Rule 500 — Florida State Exceptions

**500.A**

Accept Florida risks only when the location, construction, occupancy, and protection features fit current state appetite. Refer any risk with incomplete or conflicting eligibility information.

Florida loss exposure can change materially with site conditions. Consistent screening protects portfolio quality.

Document the eligibility review in the underwriting file. Record the source of material risk information.

**500.B**

Verify the risk location before binding coverage. Refer any location that cannot be matched to reliable property information.

Location accuracy supports proper hazard evaluation and rating. Incorrect location data can impair claim handling.

Document the verified location source in the underwriting file. Retain any discrepancy resolution notes.

**500.C**

Require a roof inspection before binding when roof age is at or above 15 years. Refer any inspection that does not clearly identify roof condition and remaining serviceability.

Roof condition is a material Florida loss driver. Current inspection evidence supports an informed acceptance decision.

Document the inspection findings in the underwriting file. Record the underwriting disposition of adverse findings.

**500.D**

Decline a risk when roof age is at or above 20 years. Do not override this requirement through discretionary pricing.

Roof age beyond appetite creates elevated severity and frequency concerns. Pricing does not correct unacceptable physical condition.

Document the roof age source in the underwriting file. Record the declination reason using approved underwriting language.

**500.E**

Refer any roof with visible deterioration, missing materials, prior patching, or unresolved repair evidence. Do not bind while material roof condition concerns remain open.

Observable roof defects may indicate increased water intrusion potential. Unresolved condition issues can produce avoidable claim activity.

Document the observed condition concern in the underwriting file. Retain inspection evidence supporting the referral.

**500.F**

Refer any risk with roof information obtained solely from an unsupported applicant statement. Obtain reliable evidence before completing the acceptance review.

Applicant information may not establish actual roof condition. Independent support improves underwriting consistency.

Document the source used to validate roof information. Record any variance between reported and verified details.

**500.G**

Refer any risk with prior roof leakage, interior water staining, or moisture damage. Do not rely on cosmetic repairs as evidence that the exposure is resolved.

Prior water damage may indicate an active maintenance or construction issue. Hidden damage can produce recurrent losses.

Document the reported loss or damage history in the underwriting file. Record the basis for any approved exception.

**500.H**

Refer any risk with active construction, substantial renovation, or incomplete repair work. Bind only after confirming that the property meets occupancy and condition requirements.

Construction activity can alter hazard, valuation, and loss control conditions. Incomplete work may prevent accurate risk assessment.

Document the construction status in the underwriting file. Retain supporting property information and underwriting notes.

**500.I**

Verify that Coverage A reflects the carrier's accepted replacement cost basis. Refer any valuation with unsupported assumptions or material data gaps.

Accurate replacement cost supports adequate insurance and claim settlement. Unsupported valuation increases limit adequacy concerns.

Document the valuation source in the underwriting file. Record material assumptions used in the review.

**500.J**

Do not bind Coverage A below $200,000. Refer any request for a lower limit.

The Coverage A floor supports minimum risk quality standards. Limits below the floor may not support proper property protection.

Document the selected Coverage A limit in the underwriting file. Record the disposition of any lower limit request.

**500.K**

Do not write Coverage A above $900,000. Refer any submission requesting a higher limit.

The Coverage A ceiling defines the carrier's maximum accepted property exposure. Limits above the ceiling require placement outside this appetite.

Document the requested Coverage A limit in the underwriting file. Record the declination or referral disposition.

**500.L**

Bind Coverage A only within line underwriter authority of $600,000. Refer any risk exceeding that authority.

Authority controls maintain appropriate review of larger property exposures. Escalation supports consistent risk selection.

Document the applicable authority review in the underwriting file. Record the approving underwriter when referral is required.

**500.M**

A senior underwriter may bind Coverage A through $900,000. Refer any risk exceeding senior underwriter authority.

Higher property limits require increased underwriting oversight. Authority limits protect against unreviewed accumulation.

Document the senior underwriter approval in the underwriting file. Record the approved Coverage A limit.

**500.N**

Refer any dwelling with unusual architecture, custom construction, or specialized materials. Do not accept unsupported valuation for distinctive property features.

Unique construction can materially affect replacement cost and repair availability. Specialized materials may increase claim complexity.

Document the construction features in the underwriting file. Retain valuation support for the accepted limit.

**500.O**

Refer any risk with protective devices that are represented but cannot be verified. Do not apply related rating treatment without supporting evidence.

Protective devices affect loss prevention expectations. Unsupported representations can create rating and claim handling issues.

Document the verification source in the underwriting file. Record any unavailable or inoperative protective feature.

**500.P**

Verify that electrical, plumbing, heating, and cooling information is complete when material to risk acceptance. Refer any property with unresolved system condition concerns.

Core building systems affect water, fire, and equipment loss exposure. Incomplete system information impairs hazard assessment.

Document the system information reviewed in the underwriting file. Record the basis for resolving any condition concern.

**500.Q**

Refer any dwelling with evidence of repeated water loss, seepage, or maintenance-related damage. Do not accept the risk until the cause and corrective action are understood.

Repeated water events may indicate an unresolved physical condition. Prior repair activity does not establish a completed remedy.

Document prior water events in the underwriting file. Retain available repair or inspection evidence.

**500.R**

Refer any risk with prior fire loss, fire damage, or unresolved fire protection concerns. Confirm that material hazards have been corrected before binding.

Fire history may reveal ongoing construction, maintenance, or occupancy issues. Corrective action must address the actual cause of loss.

Document the fire history review in the underwriting file. Record evidence supporting any acceptance decision.

**500.S**

Refer any risk with prior liability loss involving premises conditions. Evaluate whether the condition remains present or may recur.

Premises liability history can identify maintenance and occupancy concerns. Recurring conditions may not be addressed by standard terms.

Document the prior liability loss information in the underwriting file. Record the underwriting evaluation of corrective action.

**500.T**

Refer any risk with vacant, seasonal, unoccupied, or irregularly occupied characteristics. Confirm that property care and loss prevention controls are adequate.

Reduced occupancy can delay discovery of water, fire, and weather damage. Property supervision affects the severity of developing losses.

Document the occupancy characteristics in the underwriting file. Record the basis for the acceptance decision.

**500.U**

Refer any risk used for rental, short-term lodging, or other income-producing activity. Do not classify income-producing occupancy as owner occupancy.

Occupancy classification affects hazard, liability, and eligibility review. Misclassification can invalidate underwriting assumptions.

Document the occupancy use in the underwriting file. Retain available evidence supporting the classification.

**500.V**

Refer any risk with business activity conducted from the premises. Confirm that the activity does not create an unaccepted liability or property hazard.

Business use may alter visitor traffic, equipment exposure, and premises operations. Accurate classification supports proper risk selection.

Document the business activity description in the underwriting file. Record the underwriting disposition.

**500.W**

Refer any risk with animals that present a material liability concern. Do not rely solely on an applicant's characterization of animal behavior.

Animal liability exposure depends on history, controls, and premises circumstances. Incomplete information can lead to adverse selection.

Document the animal information reviewed in the underwriting file. Record the reason for any referral or declination.

**500.X**

Refer any risk with a pool, spa, water feature, or similar premises attraction. Confirm that required safety controls are present and maintained.

Water-related premises features can produce severe liability exposure. Safety controls support loss prevention and defensible underwriting.

Document the safety control information in the underwriting file. Record any unresolved condition concern.

**500.Y**

Refer any risk with a trampoline, recreational structure, or similar elevated liability feature. Confirm that the feature is eligible before binding.

Recreational features can increase premises liability frequency and severity. Eligibility review must reflect actual site conditions.

Document the feature and its condition in the underwriting file. Record the underwriting disposition.

**500.Z**

Refer any risk with known sinkhole activity, ground movement, or structural distress. Do not accept unsupported assurances that the condition is stable.

Ground instability can affect property condition and future loss potential. Structural concerns require reliable evaluation.

Document the reported condition in the underwriting file. Retain available inspection or engineering information.

**500.AA**

Refer any risk with prior hurricane, wind, flood, or weather-related property loss. Confirm that repairs are complete and that the property remains acceptable.

Weather loss history may reveal unresolved vulnerability or deficient repairs. Loss recurrence can be driven by property condition.

Document the prior weather loss review in the underwriting file. Record the basis for accepting the repaired property.

**500.AB**

Verify exterior opening protection information when material to the risk review. Refer any property with conflicting or incomplete protection details.

Opening protection can affect wind-driven damage potential. Accurate information supports proper exposure evaluation.

Document the verified protection information in the underwriting file. Record the source of supporting evidence.

**500.AC**

Apply the maximum named storm deductible when required by the accepted risk profile. Refer any request that conflicts with approved deductible handling.

Named storm exposure requires consistent deductible application. Unauthorized deductible treatment can create unintended retention exposure.

Document the deductible selected in the underwriting file. Record the basis for any referral.

**500.AD**

Refer any risk with prior cancellation, nonrenewal, or declined coverage for underwriting reasons. Obtain sufficient information to understand the prior action.

Prior carrier action may identify material conditions requiring review. The prior disposition does not alone determine current eligibility.

Document the prior carrier action in the underwriting file. Record the explanation obtained for the action.

**500.AE**

Handle notice of nonrenewal through approved operational workflow. Refer any file where the reason for action is incomplete or unsupported.

Nonrenewal activity requires accurate file support and consistent handling. Clear records support internal review and regulatory response.

Document the reason for action in the underwriting file. Retain the approved workflow record.

**500.AF**

Refer any risk with material discrepancies among application data, inspections, loss information, or public property records. Do not bind until the discrepancy is resolved.

Conflicting information may indicate inaccurate classification or undisclosed condition concerns. Resolution supports reliable underwriting decisions.

Document each material discrepancy in the underwriting file. Record the evidence used to resolve it.

**500.AG**

Require corrected application information when a material error is identified before binding. Do not make an underwriting decision from known inaccurate data.

Accurate application data supports rating, eligibility, and claims administration. Uncorrected errors create avoidable processing issues.

Document the correction in the underwriting file. Retain the source supporting the revised information.

**500.AH**

Refer any risk where ownership cannot be confirmed. Do not bind coverage for an applicant without an insurable interest review.

Ownership and insurable interest affect eligibility and claim payment handling. Reliable verification prevents improper account setup.

Document the ownership information in the underwriting file. Record the source used for verification.

**500.AI**

Refer any risk held in trust, estate, entity, or other nonstandard ownership arrangement. Confirm that the named insured and interest structure are acceptable.

Nonstandard ownership can affect eligibility and claim settlement requirements. Accurate party identification supports proper account administration.

Document the ownership arrangement in the underwriting file. Record the underwriting determination.

**500.AJ**

Refer any risk with prior material misrepresentation, suspected fraud, or unverifiable loss information. Do not bind while the integrity concern remains unresolved.

Information reliability is fundamental to underwriting selection. Unresolved integrity concerns can affect all aspects of the risk review.

Document the concern in the underwriting file. Retain evidence supporting the referral.

**500.AK**

Refer any risk with an open claim, unrepaired loss, or pending damage assessment. Do not bind until the condition and exposure are understood.

Open loss conditions may obscure the property's actual state. Pending repairs can affect valuation and future loss potential.

Document the open claim or damage condition in the underwriting file. Record the disposition of the risk.

**500.AL**

Refer any property with visible deferred maintenance affecting roof, exterior, systems, or safety features. Require evidence that material conditions are corrected.

Deferred maintenance may increase the likelihood of property and liability losses. Condition issues can worsen rapidly in Florida weather.

Document the maintenance concern in the underwriting file. Retain available photographs or inspection findings.

**500.AM**

Refer any risk with fencing, gates, railings, stairs, or walkways in poor condition. Do not accept unresolved premises hazards.

Premises defects can create preventable liability exposure. Physical condition review supports loss prevention.

Document the observed hazard in the underwriting file. Record the corrective action or underwriting disposition.

**500.AN**

Refer any risk with prior code enforcement action or unresolved municipal compliance concern. Confirm that the property remains suitable for the requested coverage.

Compliance issues may indicate maintenance, occupancy, or construction concerns. Unresolved actions can affect loss potential and claim complexity.

Document the reported compliance concern in the underwriting file. Record the basis for resolution.

**500.AO**

Refer any risk with unusual access, restricted emergency response, or difficult property approach. Evaluate whether the condition affects protection or loss severity.

Access limitations can delay emergency services and repair response. Site constraints may increase claim severity.

Document the access condition in the underwriting file. Record the underwriting evaluation.

**500.AP**

Refer any risk with detached structures that are material to the account. Confirm the structures are accurately described and acceptable.

Detached structures can create unrecognized property and liability exposure. Accurate description supports proper account handling.

Document the detached structure information in the underwriting file. Record the accepted exposure basis.

**500.AQ**

Refer any risk with substantial personal property exposure that appears inconsistent with the dwelling characteristics. Confirm that the account information is complete and credible.

Inconsistent contents information may indicate inaccurate occupancy or valuation assumptions. Complete information supports appropriate underwriting review.

Document the information reviewed in the underwriting file. Record the basis for resolving the inconsistency.

**500.AR**

Apply credits only as shown in credits on the state exception pages of the rating manualcredits on the state exception pages of the rating manual (see Homeowners and Dwelling Rating Manual, P12 Part 12 — State Exception Pages). Do not apply unsupported state exception credits.

State exception credits require controlled application. Unauthorized credits can produce inaccurate premium and inconsistent treatment.

Document the credit support in the underwriting file. Record the source used for the credit decision.

**500.AS**

Refer any request for an exception from Florida eligibility requirements. Do not grant an exception without appropriate underwriting authority.

Exceptions can alter the carrier's intended exposure profile. Authority review ensures consistent treatment of nonstandard risks.

Document the exception request in the underwriting file. Record the approving authority and rationale.

**500.AT**

Refer any risk with adverse inspection findings that are not addressed by the applicant. Do not substitute verbal assurances for corrective evidence.

Inspection findings provide direct evidence of property condition. Reliable corrective evidence supports defensible acceptance decisions.

Document the adverse finding in the underwriting file. Retain available corrective evidence.

**500.AU**

Refer any risk where replacement cost information appears materially inconsistent with property characteristics. Obtain sufficient support before accepting the stated limit.

Inadequate replacement cost can create underinsurance and claim settlement concerns. Accurate valuation supports sound risk selection.

Document the valuation concern in the underwriting file. Record the final valuation basis.

**500.AV**

Refer any risk with prior litigation arising from ownership, occupancy, or maintenance of the premises. Evaluate the circumstances before making an underwriting decision.

Litigation history may identify recurring liability or management concerns. Context is necessary to distinguish isolated events from ongoing exposure.

Document the litigation information in the underwriting file. Record the underwriting disposition.

**500.AW**

Refer any risk with environmental, contamination, mold, or moisture concerns. Do not accept the risk until material property conditions are understood.

Environmental conditions can affect habitability, repair scope, and loss severity. Incomplete information may conceal significant damage.

Document the reported concern in the underwriting file. Retain available inspection or remediation information.

**500.AX**

Maintain a complete Florida underwriting file for every accepted, declined, or referred risk. Record the decision basis without using language intended for an insured.

File documentation supports audit, claim review, and consistent underwriting practice. This manual is internal carrier guidance and is not part of any policy contract.

Document the final underwriting action in the underwriting file. Retain material evidence supporting the action.

## Rule 510 — Texas State Exceptions

**510.1** Bind Coverage A within line underwriter authority only when the requested limit does not exceed $800,000.

This preserves the assigned approval structure for Texas risks.

Document the requested Coverage A limit and the binding authority used.

**510.2** Refer Coverage A requests exceeding line underwriter authority to a senior underwriter when the requested limit does not exceed $1,200,000.

This places elevated values with the appropriate approval level.

Document the referral, approval, and approved Coverage A limit.

**510.3** Decline or refer any request for Coverage A exceeding $1,200,000.

This maintains the carrier’s maximum Texas property exposure.

Document the disposition and the reason for any declined request.

**510.4** Refer any water backup limit exceeding $25,000 before binding.

This controls severity exposure arising from backup losses.

Document the requested limit, referral, and referral response.

**510.5** Obtain a roof inspection before binding when roof age is at or above 15 years.

This supports evaluation of roof condition and remaining serviceability.

Document the roof inspection source, findings, and underwriting disposition.

**510.6** Verify the Texas risk address before quoting or binding coverage.

This confirms that Texas exceptions apply to the risk.

Document the verified risk address in the underwriting record.

**510.7** Confirm the named insured has an insurable interest in the covered property.

This prevents issuance where the applicant lacks a valid property interest.

Document the source used to confirm insurable interest.

**510.8** Match the occupancy description to the actual use of the insured location.

This supports accurate eligibility and hazard evaluation.

Document the occupancy description and supporting source.

**510.9** Refer any risk with conflicting ownership or occupancy information.

This prevents binding on incomplete or unreliable risk facts.

Document the conflicting information and referral outcome.

**510.10** Verify that the property description supports the requested coverage.

This helps identify construction, use, or exposure concerns before binding.

Document the property description reviewed and any underwriting comments.

**510.11** Refer any risk with undisclosed commercial activity at the insured location.

This avoids accepting exposures outside personal lines appetite.

Document the commercial activity identified and the underwriting disposition.

**510.12** Confirm that incidental business activity does not materially increase property or liability exposure.

This distinguishes minor activity from unacceptable business use.

Document the activity described and the basis for the decision.

**510.13** Refer any risk with vacant, unoccupied, or seasonal occupancy concerns.

This allows review of maintenance, theft, water damage, and delayed loss discovery exposure.

Document the occupancy status and referral response.

**510.14** Verify that the applicant’s mailing address is complete and usable for carrier correspondence.

This supports timely notices and accurate account servicing.

Document the mailing address verified.

**510.15** Refer any risk where the mailing address and risk address create an unexplained ownership or occupancy concern.

This may indicate an undisclosed occupancy arrangement.

Document the explanation obtained and underwriting decision.

**510.16** Apply Texas rating credits only when supported by the required risk information, including credits on the state exception pages of the rating manualcredits on the state exception pages of the rating manual (see Homeowners and Dwelling Rating Manual, P12 Part 12 — State Exception Pages).

This ensures that credits are applied consistently and supported by the file.

Document the supporting risk information for each credit applied.

**510.17** Remove any credit that is unsupported, inconsistent, or no longer applicable.

This prevents inaccurate premium treatment for the Texas risk.

Document the credit removed and the reason for removal.

**510.18** Refer any request to retain a disputed credit.

This requires review where the available information does not support the rating treatment.

Document the disputed credit and referral outcome.

**510.19** Verify roof material, roof condition, and visible roof defects when the information is available.

This supports wind, hail, and water loss evaluation.

Document the roof information reviewed and any noted defects.

**510.20** Refer any risk with roof damage, active leakage, temporary repair, or unresolved roof condition concerns.

This avoids binding property coverage on a known deteriorated condition.

Document the condition identified and the referral decision.

**510.21** Require correction of material roof condition concerns before binding when underwriting determines correction is necessary.

This limits exposure to foreseeable water intrusion and wind damage.

Document the required correction and evidence of completion.

**510.22** Verify exterior condition, including signs of deferred maintenance, before accepting a risk with available inspection information.

This identifies property conditions that may increase loss frequency or severity.

Document the exterior conditions reviewed.

**510.23** Refer any risk with material deterioration, structural distress, or unsafe premises conditions.

This permits evaluation of hazards outside ordinary maintenance tolerance.

Document the condition identified and referral response.

**510.24** Do not bind a risk with an unresolved material property condition.

This prevents acceptance before the underlying hazard is addressed.

Document the unresolved condition and disposition.

**510.25** Verify the presence of prior water damage when disclosed by the applicant or shown by available information.

This assists evaluation of recurring water loss potential.

Document the prior water damage information reviewed.

**510.26** Refer any risk with repeated water damage, unresolved moisture, mold concern, or plumbing deterioration.

This may indicate an ongoing source of loss.

Document the condition, loss history, and referral outcome.

**510.27** Require repair of active plumbing leaks before binding.

This prevents acceptance with a known continuing water hazard.

Document the repair requirement and evidence of correction.

**510.28** Verify that the applicant has disclosed prior property losses accurately and completely.

This supports sound risk selection and loss history review.

Document the loss information reviewed and any discrepancy identified.

**510.29** Refer any risk with material discrepancies between disclosed losses and available loss information.

This may indicate incomplete underwriting information or an elevated loss exposure.

Document the discrepancy and referral disposition.

**510.30** Refer any risk with an unresolved prior loss or open claim condition.

This allows review of repair status and continuing exposure.

Document the open condition and available repair information.

**510.31** Verify the presence and condition of protective devices when credits or eligibility depend on them.

This ensures that protective features relied upon for underwriting are operational.

Document the device type, condition, and verification source.

**510.32** Remove protective device credit when the device cannot be verified.

This prevents rating treatment based on unsupported protection.

Document the unavailable verification and credit action taken.

**510.33** Refer any risk with disabled, impaired, or unreliable protective devices.

This permits review of the resulting fire, theft, or water exposure.

Document the device condition and referral response.

**510.34** Verify the condition of electrical service when available information identifies an electrical concern.

This supports evaluation of fire and equipment damage potential.

Document the electrical information reviewed and any required action.

**510.35** Refer any risk with unsafe wiring, overloaded service, exposed conductors, or unresolved electrical defects.

This prevents acceptance of a known fire hazard without review.

Document the electrical concern and underwriting disposition.

**510.36** Verify heating equipment condition when the risk information identifies a heating source or heating concern.

This supports evaluation of fire and freeze loss potential.

Document the heating equipment information reviewed.

**510.37** Refer any risk with unsafe heating equipment, unvented combustion concerns, or evidence of improper installation.

This requires review of a potentially material fire or injury hazard.

Document the condition identified and referral outcome.

**510.38** Verify that swimming pools, spas, trampolines, and similar recreational features are accurately disclosed.

This supports liability exposure evaluation.

Document the feature disclosed and applicable protective information.

**510.39** Refer any risk with recreational features that present an unresolved liability hazard.

This allows review of supervision, access control, and premises safety conditions.

Document the hazard identified and underwriting decision.

**510.40** Verify that animals at the insured location are accurately disclosed when required by the application.

This supports evaluation of animal liability exposure.

Document the animal information reviewed and any referral action.

**510.41** Refer any risk involving an animal with aggressive behavior, bite history, or restricted eligibility concern.

This requires review of potentially elevated liability exposure.

Document the disclosed animal history and referral response.

**510.42** Verify that outbuildings and detached structures are accurately described.

This supports proper evaluation of construction, use, and exposure.

Document the detached structure information reviewed.

**510.43** Refer any detached structure used for business, storage of hazardous materials, or occupancy not disclosed in the application.

This may create an exposure outside ordinary personal use.

Document the use identified and underwriting disposition.

**510.44** Verify that renovation, reconstruction, or major repair activity is disclosed before binding.

This identifies temporary property hazards and incomplete construction exposure.

Document the work described and current completion status.

**510.45** Refer any risk undergoing renovation or reconstruction that materially affects occupancy, protection, or property condition.

This allows review of changing exposure conditions.

Document the work activity and referral outcome.

**510.46** Verify that the insured location has reasonable access for emergency response.

This supports assessment of fire protection and loss mitigation conditions.

Document the access information reviewed.

**510.47** Refer any risk with restricted emergency access or unusual fire response concerns.

This may increase property loss severity following a covered event.

Document the access concern and underwriting disposition.

**510.48** Apply the maximum windstorm deductible in seacoast territories when required for the risk.

This maintains Texas coastal catastrophe exposure controls.

Document the deductible applied and the territorial basis.

**510.49** Refer any seacoast risk with unusual windstorm exposure, construction concerns, or unresolved roof condition.

This permits review of concentrated catastrophe exposure.

Document the exposure concern and referral response.

**510.50** Verify that windstorm-related risk characteristics are accurately reflected in the underwriting record.

This supports consistent handling of Texas weather exposure.

Document the characteristics reviewed and any corrective action.

**510.51** Refer any risk with prior windstorm damage that remains unrepaired or inadequately repaired.

This may indicate continuing vulnerability to future weather loss.

Document the damage history, repair status, and referral outcome.

**510.52** Verify that requested deductibles are available for the Texas risk and supported by underwriting requirements.

This prevents issuance of an unsupported deductible arrangement.

Document the deductible requested and applied.

**510.53** Refer any request for an exception to standard deductible treatment.

This requires approval before altering normal Texas underwriting controls.

Document the requested exception and approval decision.

**510.54** Complete underwriting review early enough to meet the accept or deny deadline.

This supports timely handling of the applicant’s request.

Document the review completion and final disposition.

**510.55** Refer any risk that cannot be evaluated from the information provided.

This prevents binding where material underwriting facts remain unknown.

Document the missing information and referral action.

**510.56** Obtain clarification when application information is incomplete, inconsistent, or unclear.

This supports accurate selection and rating of the Texas risk.

Document the clarification requested and information received.

**510.57** Decline any risk that remains ineligible after required clarification or referral review.

This preserves adherence to Texas underwriting appetite.

Document the ineligibility basis and final disposition.

**510.58** Retain all Texas exception support in the underwriting record before binding or declining the risk.

This provides an auditable basis for the underwriting action.

Document that required approvals, verifications, and exception support are complete.

## Rule 520 — California State Exceptions

**520.A** Bind California risks only within delegated underwriting authority. Refer any risk that exceeds the line underwriter authority ceiling of $1,000,000.

California catastrophe exposure requires controlled authority use. Authority referrals support consistent risk selection.

Document the authority review in the underwriting file. Record the referral outcome before issuing coverage.

**520.B** Do not issue Coverage A above $2,000,000. Refer any requested limit that exceeds this ceiling.

Higher property limits may create accumulation or reinsurance concerns. Central review protects portfolio capacity.

Document the requested Coverage A limit and the final limit issued. Retain any approval for an exception in the underwriting file.

**520.C** Require replacement cost settlement only when the insured-to-value review supports 100%. Refer any risk that does not meet the applicable valuation standard.

Adequate insurance values support proper loss settlement. Inadequate values may create valuation disputes and underwriting leakage.

Document the valuation source used for the review. Record the insured-to-value determination in the underwriting file.

**520.D** Verify the California risk address before binding coverage. Refer any submission with an unresolved address discrepancy.

Territory, wildfire, and catastrophe evaluation depend on an accurate address. Incomplete address data may produce an unreliable eligibility decision.

Document the verified risk address in the underwriting file. Retain supporting address validation when obtained.

**520.E** Evaluate the dwelling condition before accepting a California risk. Refer any risk with material deferred maintenance.

Poor condition may increase expected frequency and severity of loss. A clear condition review supports consistent selection.

Document observed maintenance concerns and the underwriting disposition. Retain inspection information when available.

**520.F** Refer any California dwelling with unrepaired roof damage. Do not bind until the condition is resolved or approved.

Roof damage may permit water intrusion and accelerate property deterioration. The condition may also indicate unaddressed maintenance concerns.

Document the reported roof condition and any repair evidence. Record the approval or declination decision.

**520.G** Refer any California risk with material brush, vegetation, or combustible debris near insured structures. Do not rely on applicant descriptions alone when other information conflicts.

Wildfire exposure may change with site conditions and property maintenance. Conflicting information requires underwriting judgment.

Document the vegetation review and supporting source. Record any required risk improvement communication.

**520.H** Require a complete exterior condition review for a California risk when available underwriting information is insufficient. Refer any risk that cannot be evaluated from reliable information.

California property conditions may change rapidly due to weather, fire, and maintenance activity. Complete information supports a defensible eligibility decision.

Document the reason for the review and the source used. Retain relevant inspection findings in the underwriting file.

**520.I** Refer any California risk with prior fire damage that has not been fully repaired. Do not issue coverage based solely on an applicant statement of completion.

Unrepaired fire damage may create continuing safety and property loss exposure. Repair verification supports proper risk classification.

Document the loss history review and repair status. Retain available repair evidence and approval notes.

**520.J** Review prior property losses before binding a California risk. Refer any loss pattern that indicates unresolved hazard or maintenance concerns.

Loss history may identify conditions not evident from the application. Repeated or related losses require closer underwriting review.

Document the loss information considered and the underwriting conclusion. Record any follow-up requested from the applicant.

**520.K** Refer any California risk with an open water damage claim. Do not bind until the source, scope, and repair status are understood.

Open water damage may involve hidden deterioration or continuing leakage. Unresolved claims may impair accurate risk assessment.

Document the claim status and the information reviewed. Retain repair or mitigation evidence when provided.

**520.L** Refer any California risk with an open liability claim. Do not make an eligibility decision until material facts are verified.

Open liability matters may affect hazard evaluation and future loss potential. Complete information supports consistent treatment.

Document the claim review and referral result. Record the basis for the final underwriting action.

**520.M** Review occupancy before binding a California dwelling risk. Refer any occupancy that differs from the submitted application.

Occupancy affects expected use, maintenance, and loss exposure. Misstated occupancy may invalidate the underwriting basis.

Document the occupancy information reviewed. Record any clarification obtained before issuance.

**520.N** Refer any California risk used for transient, short-term, or other nontraditional occupancy. Do not classify the risk until the use is confirmed.

Nontraditional occupancy may increase liability, theft, and property damage exposure. The use may also affect eligibility.

Document the reported use and the underwriting decision. Retain supporting information for the occupancy determination.

**520.O** Verify that the named insured has an insurable interest in the California dwelling. Refer any risk with unclear ownership or contractual responsibility.

Ownership uncertainty may complicate policy issuance and claim handling. Clear insurable interest supports proper underwriting intent.

Document the ownership information reviewed. Record any ownership clarification in the underwriting file.

**520.P** Refer any California risk with pending construction, major renovation, or incomplete repairs. Do not bind without an approved underwriting disposition.

Construction activity may alter fire, theft, liability, and water damage exposure. Incomplete work may also prevent reliable valuation.

Document the work description and current condition. Retain available permits, contracts, or inspection information.

**520.Q** Review electrical, plumbing, heating, and roofing information for material age or condition concerns. Refer any system that appears unsafe, deteriorated, or unsuitable.

Core building systems contribute directly to property loss potential. Material deficiencies may require repair or specialized review.

Document the system information considered. Record the underwriting action and any required corrective work.

**520.R** Refer any California risk with a known foundation, slope, retaining wall, or soil stability concern. Do not assume ordinary construction tolerances apply.

Ground movement and drainage conditions may cause substantial property damage. These exposures require individual evaluation.

Document the condition reported or observed. Retain engineering, inspection, or repair information when available.

**520.S** Evaluate drainage and water runoff conditions at the California risk location. Refer any property with evidence of recurring ponding, seepage, or erosion.

Drainage conditions may contribute to water intrusion and structural damage. Site-specific review supports appropriate risk selection.

Document the drainage information reviewed. Record any mitigation requirement or referral decision.

**520.T** Refer any California risk with a swimming pool, spa, pond, or similar water feature that presents an unresolved safety concern. Require satisfactory hazard controls before binding when applicable.

Water features may create significant bodily injury exposure. Safety controls support loss prevention and defensible underwriting.

Document the water feature review and controls observed. Record any required correction or referral outcome.

**520.U** Refer any California risk with a trampoline, climbing structure, or comparable recreational hazard. Do not rely on incomplete liability exposure information.

Recreational hazards may increase injury frequency and severity. Full disclosure supports proper eligibility review.

Document the hazard disclosed and the underwriting disposition. Retain any risk-control communication in the file.

**520.V** Review animal exposure for each California risk. Refer any animal history or condition that creates a material liability concern.

Animal behavior and prior incidents may affect bodily injury exposure. Clear review supports consistent underwriting action.

Document the animal information considered. Record the basis for acceptance, restriction, or declination.

**520.W** Refer any California risk with business activity conducted from the insured location. Do not treat business use as incidental without supporting information.

Business activity may change premises liability, property, and visitor exposure. The nature of operations must be understood before binding.

Document the business activity disclosed. Record the underwriting determination and any coverage limitations applied.

**520.X** Evaluate vacancy, unoccupancy, and seasonal use for California risks. Refer any risk with uncertain occupancy maintenance.

Reduced occupancy may delay detection of fire, water, or theft loss. Vacancy conditions may also affect property preservation.

Document the occupancy status and source of information. Record the final eligibility determination.

**520.Y** Refer any California risk with protective safeguard information that is incomplete, conflicting, or inoperative. Do not credit safeguards that cannot be verified.

Protective devices may affect fire and theft exposure. Unverified safeguards may result in inaccurate rating or selection.

Document the safeguard information reviewed. Record verification efforts and the underwriting outcome.

**520.Z** Apply credits on the state exception pages of the rating manualcredits on the state exception pages of the rating manual (see Homeowners and Dwelling Rating Manual, P12 Part 12 — State Exception Pages) only after confirming that the risk meets the applicable conditions. Refer any credit request supported by incomplete information.

State-specific credits require accurate risk characteristics. Unsupported credits may create rating error and audit exposure.

Document the basis for each credit applied. Retain the supporting underwriting information in the file.

**520.AA** Review wildfire-related information for every California risk requiring catastrophe evaluation. Refer any risk with unresolved wildfire hazard indicators.

Wildfire conditions may vary by location, access, vegetation, and surrounding development. A complete review supports controlled catastrophe exposure.

Document the wildfire information considered. Record the referral decision and any approval conditions.

**520.AB** Do not alter, waive, or negotiate the quake deductible without authorized underwriting direction. Refer any request for treatment outside approved handling.

Earthquake-related provisions require consistent administration. Unauthorized changes may create coverage and pricing error.

Document the request and the action taken. Retain authorized direction in the underwriting file.

**520.AC** Issue any notice of nonrenewal only through approved underwriting and service procedures. Refer any proposed action with incomplete support.

Nonrenewal activity requires accurate underwriting records and controlled handling. Complete support reduces dispute and compliance risk.

Document the underwriting basis for the action. Record required approvals and communications in the file.

**520.AD** Refer any California risk with prior cancellation, nonrenewal, or rescission information that is not fully explained. Do not assume the prior action is immaterial.

Prior coverage actions may identify unresolved eligibility or hazard concerns. Context is necessary for fair and consistent underwriting.

Document the prior action disclosed and the explanation obtained. Record the final underwriting disposition.

**520.AE** Verify material application answers before binding a California risk. Refer any answer that conflicts with available underwriting information.

Accurate application data is necessary for eligibility, rating, and claim handling. Unresolved conflicts may indicate material misrepresentation.

Document the information compared and the resolution reached. Retain supporting evidence when available.

**520.AF** Refer any California risk with an undisclosed prior loss discovered during underwriting review. Do not bind until the discrepancy is resolved.

Undisclosed loss information may affect hazard assessment and eligibility. Resolution supports an accurate underwriting record.

Document the discovered loss information and applicant response. Record the final underwriting decision.

**520.AG** Review prior insurance information for consistency with the submitted California application. Refer any unexplained lapse or discrepancy.

Prior insurance history may reveal occupancy, maintenance, or eligibility concerns. Clear information supports appropriate risk selection.

Document the prior insurance information reviewed. Record any explanation obtained and the resulting disposition.

**520.AH** Refer any California risk with an applicant or insured who declines material underwriting information. Do not complete binding with an incomplete file.

Material information is necessary for a reliable underwriting decision. Missing information may prevent proper classification of the risk.

Document the information requested and the response received. Record the reason for referral or declination.

**520.AI** Require photographs or inspection information when visible property condition cannot be reliably assessed. Refer any risk with inadequate evidence of condition.

Photographs and inspections may reveal hazards not described in the application. Reliable evidence supports consistent file decisions.

Document the source and date of the information reviewed. Retain relevant images or inspection findings.

**520.AJ** Refer any California risk with material discrepancies between photographs, inspection information, and application answers. Do not resolve discrepancies by assumption.

Conflicting information may indicate changed conditions or incomplete disclosure. Underwriting review must rely on the most reliable evidence.

Document each discrepancy and the resolution. Record the authority approving the final action.

**520.AK** Review replacement cost estimates for apparent omissions, unusual features, or unsupported characteristics. Refer any estimate that cannot be reconciled to the risk.

Reliable replacement cost estimates support appropriate Coverage A selection. Unsupported values may create settlement and adequacy concerns.

Document the estimate source and review outcome. Record any corrected property characteristics.

**520.AL** Refer any California dwelling with unusual construction, specialty materials, or nonstandard design features. Do not assign ordinary assumptions to a nonstandard structure.

Special construction may affect replacement cost, fire behavior, and repair availability. Individual review supports sound risk selection.

Document the construction characteristics identified. Retain valuation or inspection support when available.

**520.AM** Review attached and detached structures for material hazard conditions. Refer any structure with unsafe, deteriorated, or incomplete construction.

Accessory structures may increase property and liability exposure. Their condition may also indicate overall maintenance practices.

Document the structures reviewed and the condition observed. Record any required repair or underwriting restriction.

**520.AN** Refer any California risk with evidence of illegal dumping, abandoned materials, or hazardous storage. Do not bind until the hazard is resolved or approved.

Hazardous site conditions may increase fire, injury, and property damage exposure. They may also impair access for emergency response.

Document the condition identified and the source of information. Record the corrective action or referral outcome.

**520.AO** Review access conditions for California properties exposed to wildfire or emergency response limitations. Refer any risk with material access concerns.

Restricted access may affect evacuation, firefighting, inspection, and loss severity. Site access requires individual consideration.

Document the access information reviewed. Record the underwriting decision and supporting rationale.

**520.AP** Refer any California risk with material exposure from adjacent vacant, damaged, or poorly maintained property. Do not disregard neighboring conditions that affect the insured location.

Adjacent conditions may contribute to fire spread, vandalism, water intrusion, or liability exposure. External hazards may materially affect the selected risk.

Document the adjacent condition and available supporting information. Record the final underwriting action.

**520.AQ** Evaluate mitigation work presented in support of California eligibility or rating. Refer any mitigation claim that lacks reliable evidence of completion.

Mitigation may reduce hazard only when work is completed and maintained. Unsupported claims may produce inaccurate underwriting results.

Document the mitigation information reviewed. Retain available evidence of completion.

**520.AR** Refer any California risk with a material change after quote and before binding. Reevaluate eligibility before issuing coverage.

Changes in condition, occupancy, use, or loss information may affect the original underwriting decision. A current review preserves the integrity of the file.

Document the change reported or discovered. Record the revised underwriting decision.

**520.AS** Refer any California risk with a material change after issuance that affects eligibility. Do not defer review until a future transaction.

Prompt review supports timely risk management and accurate policy administration. Delayed action may increase unintended exposure.

Document the change and the date received. Record the action taken under approved procedures.

**520.AT** Maintain a complete California underwriting file for each referral, exception, and adverse decision. Record facts, sources, analysis, and authority action clearly.

California exceptions require a clear record of consistent underwriting treatment. Complete files support audit, complaint handling, and claim review.

Document compliance at the time of the underwriting action. Retain material correspondence and supporting evidence in the file.

## Rule 530 — New York State Exceptions

**530.A** Bind Coverage A when the requested limit does not exceed $750,000. Refer requests exceeding that authority.

Authority controls maintain consistent risk selection. Referral permits review of exposures outside routine handling.

Record the requested Coverage A limit and the disposition in the risk record. Identify the referral basis when referral is required.

**530.B** Refer any risk with 3 paid property claims. Do not clear the referral without underwriting direction.

Paid property losses may indicate recurring maintenance or occupancy concerns. Review is required before accepting continued exposure.

Record the loss details available to the carrier and the referral outcome. Retain underwriting direction in the risk record.

**530.C** Apply credits on the state exception pages of the rating manualcredits on the state exception pages of the rating manual (see Homeowners and Dwelling Rating Manual, P12 Part 12 — State Exception Pages) only after confirming eligibility. Remove any credit that lacks support.

Credits must reflect verified risk characteristics. Unsupported credits create rating and audit concerns.

Record the basis for each applied credit in the risk record. Identify the verified characteristic supporting the credit.

**530.D** Refer any risk with unresolved property damage. Do not bind while repairs remain uncertain.

Unresolved damage may affect condition, valuation, or future loss potential. Review determines whether the exposure remains acceptable.

Record the damaged area, repair status, and referral outcome. Note any underwriting conditions imposed.

**530.E** Refer any risk with evidence of vacancy. Do not treat intermittent occupancy as ordinary residence use without review.

Vacancy can increase severity from water, fire, and liability events. Review confirms the intended occupancy and protection measures.

Record the occupancy information and referral basis. Note the underwriting disposition.

**530.F** Refer any risk used for short-term lodging. Do not classify lodging activity as ordinary personal use.

Guest turnover can create liability, theft, and maintenance concerns. Review determines whether the exposure fits personal lines appetite.

Record the described lodging activity and referral outcome. Retain relevant underwriting notes.

**530.G** Refer any risk with business activity conducted at the premises. Do not bind until the activity is evaluated.

Business activity can change premises traffic, liability, and property exposure. Review confirms whether the activity is incidental and acceptable.

Record the business activity, premises use, and referral disposition. Note any restrictions required for acceptance.

**530.H** Refer any risk with commercial signage, customer traffic, or business inventory. Do not disregard visible business indicators.

Visible business use may conflict with the declared occupancy. Review protects against misclassification of the exposure.

Record the observed indicator and the referral basis. Document the underwriting decision.

**530.I** Refer any risk with a detached structure used for business purposes. Do not assume the structure is incidental to residence use.

Business use can alter construction, contents, and liability exposure. Review determines whether the structure remains eligible.

Record the structure use and referral outcome. Note any required exclusion or condition.

**530.J** Refer any risk with agricultural activity beyond personal hobby use. Do not bind until the activity is reviewed.

Agricultural activity may introduce equipment, animal, visitor, and outbuilding exposures. Review confirms fit within carrier appetite.

Record the activity, structures involved, and underwriting disposition. Retain supporting notes.

**530.K** Refer any risk with livestock kept at the premises. Do not accept animal exposure without review.

Livestock can create bodily injury and property damage potential. Review determines whether the hazard is acceptable.

Record the animal exposure and the referral outcome. Note any required risk control measures.

**530.L** Refer any risk with an animal having a known bite history. Do not bind pending evaluation.

Prior animal incidents may indicate elevated liability potential. Review is required to assess the hazard.

Record the reported history and the referral disposition. Retain underwriting direction.

**530.M** Refer any risk with a prohibited or unusual animal exposure. Do not rely on applicant descriptions alone when the exposure is unclear.

Animal type and handling arrangements can materially affect liability risk. Review confirms eligibility and required controls.

Record the animal information and the underwriting outcome. Note the basis for the decision.
