Document the reported loss circumstances and protective measures in place. Record the underwriting decision.

**570.Q** Refer risks with a history of liability loss involving an ongoing hazardous condition. Continuing hazards require evaluation before binding.

Document the loss circumstances and the corrective action reported. Record any special handling instruction.

**570.R** Refer risks with loss information that conflicts with the application. Conflicting loss information prevents reliable risk evaluation.

Document the discrepancy and the clarification obtained. Retain the source material supporting the final decision.

**570.S** Refer risks with material misrepresentation concerns. Reliable applicant information is necessary for sound underwriting.

Document the information at issue and the basis for concern. Record the referral disposition.

**570.T** Decline risks where material application information cannot be verified. Unverified material information prevents acceptable risk selection.

Document the information requested and the efforts made to obtain verification. Record the basis for decline.

**570.U** Refer risks with vacant or unoccupied conditions. Lack of regular occupancy can increase undetected loss exposure.

Document the occupancy status and the protections reported. Record the underwriting action.

**570.V** Refer risks with seasonal occupancy. Seasonal use can affect maintenance, supervision, and weather-related exposure.

Document the occupancy pattern and protective measures reported. Record the referral outcome.

**570.W** Refer risks used for business activity that may affect personal lines eligibility. Business activity can alter the nature of the insured exposure.

Document the business activity described and its relationship to the premises. Record the eligibility determination.

**570.X** Refer risks with commercial traffic, customer activity, or delivery activity at the premises. Such activity may create liability exposure outside normal personal use.

Document the activity described and the frequency reported. Record the underwriting decision.

**570.Y** Refer risks with short-term occupancy arrangements. Temporary occupancy can affect supervision and property protection.

Document the occupancy arrangement and the party responsible for maintenance. Record the referral outcome.

**570.Z** Refer risks with an undisclosed tenant or occupant. Undisclosed occupancy can affect eligibility and coverage suitability.

Document the occupancy facts obtained and the source of verification. Record any required underwriting action.

**570.AA** Refer risks with animals that present an elevated liability concern. Animal-related exposure requires review of ownership, handling, and prior incidents.

Document the animal information disclosed and any reported incident history. Record the underwriting disposition.

**570.AB** Refer risks with an unfenced or unsecured attractive hazard. Unsecured hazards can increase bodily injury exposure.

Document the condition identified and the protective measures reported. Record any corrective action required.

**570.AC** Refer risks with recreational equipment that may create elevated liability exposure. Equipment use and storage can materially affect loss potential.

Document the equipment described and the protective measures reported. Record the referral decision.

**570.AD** Refer risks with premises conditions that may create a slip, trip, or fall hazard. Unsafe premises conditions can increase liability loss potential.

Document the condition identified and the corrective action reported. Record the underwriting determination.

**570.AE** Refer risks with structural alterations that are incomplete or poorly documented. Ongoing alterations can increase property and liability exposure.

Document the work described, current condition, and responsible party. Record any binding restriction.

**570.AF** Refer risks with extensive renovation activity. Renovation can alter occupancy, protection, and property values.

Document the renovation details and the protective measures reported. Record the referral outcome.

**570.AG** Refer risks with construction activity that affects habitability or property protection. Construction conditions require evaluation before coverage is bound.

Document the work status and the safeguards described. Record the underwriting decision.

**570.AH** Refer risks with foundation movement, settlement, or structural cracking. These conditions can indicate significant property exposure.

Document the condition described and any professional evaluation provided. Retain supporting evidence in the file.

**570.AI** Refer risks with unrepaired storm damage. Unrepaired damage can increase the severity of later weather losses.

Document the reported damage and the repair status. Record the referral disposition.

**570.AJ** Refer risks located in an area with recurring surface-water concerns. Surface-water exposure may affect property suitability.

Document the reported condition and the source of the exposure information. Record the underwriting action.

**570.AK** Refer risks with drainage conditions that direct water toward the dwelling. Improper drainage can contribute to water damage.

Document the drainage condition and corrective measures reported. Record the underwriter decision.

**570.AL** Refer risks with a sump system that is reported as inoperable or unreliable. A deficient sump system can increase water loss exposure.

Document the sump condition and the repair information provided. Record any binding requirement.

**570.AM** Refer risks with prior sewer or drain loss that lacks a clear cause. Cause uncertainty prevents reliable evaluation of recurring exposure.

Document the loss details and any corrective action reported. Record the referral outcome.

**570.AN** Refer risks with sewage, waste, or drainage conditions affecting the premises. These conditions may create property and liability exposure.

Document the condition described and the remediation status. Record the underwriting determination.

**570.AO** Refer risks with mold, rot, or persistent moisture conditions. Persistent moisture can indicate maintenance or water-intrusion concerns.

Document the reported condition and the repair information supplied. Retain the final disposition.

**570.AP** Refer risks with prior freezing loss. Freeze losses may indicate heating, insulation, or occupancy concerns.

Document the loss cause and the corrective action reported. Record the underwriting decision.

**570.AQ** Refer risks with a history of repeated weather loss. Repeated weather loss requires review of mitigation and condition of the premises.

Document the reported losses and the protective measures taken. Record the referral outcome.

**570.AR** Refer risks with a history of vandalism or malicious damage. Such loss history may indicate supervision or security concerns.

Document the loss circumstances and protective measures reported. Record any special handling instruction.

**570.AS** Refer risks with protective device information that cannot be substantiated. Unverified protection information cannot support underwriting assumptions.

Document the device information submitted and the verification obtained. Record any change to the risk decision.

**570.AT** Refer risks with alarm, monitoring, or security representations that conflict with available information. Conflicting protection information requires resolution before binding.

Document the discrepancy and the clarification received. Retain evidence supporting the final decision.

**570.AU** Refer risks with fire protection information that cannot be verified. Reliable fire protection information is necessary for hazard evaluation.

Document the protection information reviewed and the verification source. Record the referral disposition.

**570.AV** Refer risks with access conditions that could delay emergency response. Restricted access can increase property loss severity.

Document the access condition and any mitigation reported. Record the underwriting action.

**570.AW** Refer risks with a condition that may impede inspection. Inspection limitations can prevent adequate evaluation of the exposure.

Document the condition preventing inspection and the information obtained by other means. Record the underwriter decision.

**570.AX** Order further review when inspection information identifies a material condition not disclosed on the application. Undisclosed material conditions require reconciliation before coverage action.

Document the inspection finding and the applicant explanation. Record the final underwriting disposition.

**570.AY** Refer risks when applicant statements conflict with inspection findings. Conflicting information must be resolved to support proper selection.

Document the conflicting statements and the clarification obtained. Retain the inspection material in the file.

**570.AZ** Refer risks with prior cancellation, nonrenewal, or coverage refusal information that raises eligibility concerns. Prior adverse action may identify underwriting issues requiring review.

Document the information disclosed and the explanation provided. Record the referral outcome.

**570.BA** Handle a notice of intent not to renew through approved Illinois underwriting controls. Nonrenewal activity requires consistent handling and a documented business basis.

Document the reason for the proposed action and the supporting underwriting facts. Retain evidence of required handling in the file.

**570.BB** Do not use protected-class information in an underwriting decision. Underwriting action must rest on risk-related facts.

Document the objective underwriting basis for the action taken. Exclude protected-class information from underwriting notes.

**570.BC** Refer any underwriting action that may appear inconsistent with similarly situated Illinois risks. Consistent treatment supports sound underwriting practice.

Document the risk characteristics supporting the proposed action. Record the referral decision.

**570.BD** Refer risks with information suggesting a consumer-reporting error. Consumer-reporting discrepancies require review before adverse action.

Document the disputed information and the resolution obtained. Record the final underwriting action.

**570.BE** Do not rely on unsupported adverse information. Unsupported information can lead to an inaccurate risk decision.

Document the source and verification of material adverse information. Record any information excluded from consideration.

**570.BF** Refer risks requiring an exception to established Illinois eligibility criteria. Eligibility exceptions require controlled underwriting approval.

Document the exception requested and the facts supporting consideration. Record the approval or decline decision.

**570.BG** Do not bind an exception without recorded underwriting approval. Unrecorded exceptions cannot be reliably administered.

Document the approval authority and the conditions of approval. Retain the approval evidence in the file.

**570.BH** Refer risks with requested terms that are inconsistent with the reported hazard. Terms must reflect the exposure accepted.

Document the requested terms and the hazard information reviewed. Record the final terms authorized.

**570.BI** Refer risks with coverage requests that conflict with occupancy or use information. Coverage suitability depends on accurate use of the premises.

Document the coverage request and the use information considered. Record the underwriting determination.

**570.BJ** Refer risks with replacement cost information that appears unreliable. Reliable valuation supports appropriate coverage selection.

Document the valuation information reviewed and the basis for concern. Record the valuation action taken.

**570.BK** Refer risks with property characteristics that are not adequately described. Incomplete property information prevents reliable hazard assessment.

Document the missing characteristics and the information obtained. Record the final disposition.

**570.BL** Refer risks with unusual construction features. Unusual construction can affect valuation, repairability, and loss severity.

Document the construction feature and the source of the information. Record the underwriting action.

**570.BM** Refer risks with detached structures that present a material hazard concern. Detached structures can affect property and liability exposure.

Document the structure information and its reported use. Record the referral outcome.

**570.BN** Refer risks with premises used to store hazardous materials. Hazardous materials may create fire, contamination, or liability exposure.

Document the materials described and the storage conditions reported. Record the underwriting decision.

**570.BO** Refer risks with fuel storage conditions that may increase hazard. Fuel storage requires review of location, maintenance, and protection.

Document the fuel storage information and the source reviewed. Record any required underwriting action.

**570.BP** Refer risks with prior claims that are open, disputed, or unresolved. Unresolved claims can affect risk evaluation and coverage handling.

Document the claim status and the information supporting it. Record the referral disposition.

**570.BQ** Refer risks with litigation related to the premises or occupancy. Litigation may indicate unresolved liability or property concerns.

Document the litigation information disclosed and the relevant risk facts. Record the underwriting decision.

**570.BR** Maintain a complete Illinois underwriting file for every action taken. Clear documentation supports consistent risk selection and regulatory review.

Document the material facts, underwriting rationale, and final disposition. Retain supporting information with the underwriting record.

## Rule 600 — Inspections

**600.A** Order an inspection when underwriting information is incomplete, inconsistent, or unreliable. Do not bind until the inspection issue is resolved or an authorized exception is recorded.

An inspection supports risk selection when file information does not establish the condition, occupancy, or exposure. It also protects against reliance on unsupported applicant statements.

Document the inspection trigger, the information requiring verification, and the binding disposition in the underwriting file.

**600.B** Require a roof survey before binding when roof age reaches 15 years. Obtain sufficient information to identify roof condition, materials, installation quality, and visible deterioration.

Roof condition materially affects water damage susceptibility and remaining useful life. A roof survey provides current evidence when age creates elevated uncertainty.

Document the reported roof age, survey request, survey findings, and underwriting action in the file.

**600.C** Order an inspection when exterior condition suggests deferred maintenance. Refer the risk when observed conditions indicate material deterioration or an unresolved hazard.

Deferred maintenance may indicate unreported loss exposure or inadequate property care. Visible deterioration can also affect valuation and eligibility.

Document the observed condition, inspection findings, referral rationale, and final disposition.

**600.D** Require inspection evidence when a dwelling appears vacant, unoccupied, or inadequately secured. Do not rely solely on application statements to clear the condition.

Vacancy and inadequate security increase the likelihood of theft, vandalism, water damage, and delayed loss discovery. Current inspection evidence establishes the actual risk condition.

Document the occupancy indicators, security observations, applicant explanation, and underwriting decision.

**600.E** Inspect risks with visible structural movement, foundation distress, or material cracking. Refer any risk with conditions that may affect stability or habitability.

Structural concerns can create severe property loss potential and may indicate progressive damage. Underwriting requires adequate evidence before accepting the exposure.

Document the location and appearance of the condition, inspection findings, and referral outcome.

**600.F** Require inspection when siding, trim, soffit, fascia, or exterior finishes show material damage. Obtain evidence sufficient to determine whether the condition is cosmetic or exposure creating.

Exterior deterioration can permit water intrusion, pest entry, and concealed damage. Surface conditions may also indicate broader maintenance concerns.

Document the damaged areas, apparent cause, inspection evidence, and corrective action if required.

**600.G** Inspect dwellings with signs of water intrusion, staining, rot, mold, or moisture damage. Refer the risk when the source, extent, or remediation status is not established.

Moisture conditions may indicate active leakage, concealed damage, or inadequate repairs. Unresolved moisture conditions can produce recurring loss activity.

Document the observed evidence, identified source, remediation information, and underwriting disposition.

**600.H** Require inspection evidence when electrical equipment appears obsolete, damaged, altered, or improperly installed. Refer any risk with exposed conductors, damaged panels, or unsafe electrical conditions.

Electrical defects create fire and shock hazards that may not be apparent from application information. Inspection findings support appropriate risk selection.

Document the electrical observations, inspector comments, corrective requirements, and final action.

**600.I** Inspect risks with heating equipment that appears damaged, improperly vented, or inadequately maintained. Refer any risk with visible fire, combustion, or carbon monoxide concerns.

Heating defects can create severe fire and life safety exposure. Current inspection evidence is required when condition cannot be confirmed through reliable records.

Document the equipment observed, condition concerns, inspection findings, and underwriting decision.

**600.J** Require inspection when solid fuel heating equipment is present or reported. Confirm installation condition, clearance concerns, venting, and evidence of improper use.

Solid fuel heating can increase fire frequency and severity when installation or maintenance is deficient. Inspection supports eligibility review and hazard control.

Document the equipment type, location, inspection findings, and any required corrective action.

**600.K** Inspect risks with evidence of plumbing leaks, damaged piping, corroded fixtures, or water supply concerns. Refer the risk when active leakage or widespread deterioration is observed.

Plumbing defects can cause sudden water loss and recurring interior damage. Inspection evidence helps distinguish isolated maintenance issues from broader exposure.

Document the plumbing condition, apparent source, inspection findings, and final disposition.

**600.L** Require inspection when a dwelling has visible drainage concerns, ponding, erosion, or water flow toward the structure. Refer any risk with unresolved conditions that may permit water entry.

Site drainage affects the likelihood of foundation, basement, and water intrusion losses. A current inspection establishes whether mitigation is present and effective.

Document the drainage observations, mitigation features, inspection evidence, and underwriting action.

**600.M** Inspect risks with retaining walls, steep slopes, unstable soil, or visible ground movement. Refer the risk when the condition may affect the dwelling or insured structures.

Ground movement and slope conditions can create substantial property damage exposure. Inspection findings are necessary when site stability cannot be otherwise confirmed.

Document the site condition, proximity to insured structures, inspection findings, and referral result.

**600.N** Require inspection when trees, limbs, or vegetation appear likely to damage the dwelling or other insured structures. Refer any risk with substantial overhang, decay, or visible tree instability.

Vegetation hazards can produce wind, impact, roof, and service line losses. Inspection permits evaluation of the condition and any required mitigation.

Document the vegetation concern, affected structure, inspection evidence, and corrective action status.

**600.O** Inspect risks with debris, stored materials, abandoned property, or poor housekeeping that may increase fire or liability exposure. Refer the risk when conditions materially impair access, maintenance, or hazard control.

Accumulation conditions can conceal defects and increase loss severity. They may also indicate inadequate care of the insured premises.

Document the observed condition, inspection findings, applicant response, and underwriting disposition.

**600.P** Require inspection when exterior stairs, handrails, walkways, decks, or porches appear damaged or unsafe. Refer any risk with conditions that create an apparent liability hazard.

Exterior access features can produce bodily injury claims when not properly maintained. Inspection evidence supports hazard identification and remediation.

Document the feature inspected, condition observed, corrective requirement, and completion evidence.

**600.Q** Inspect decks, balconies, elevated platforms, and attached structures with visible deterioration or questionable support. Do not accept unsupported statements that the condition is safe.

Elevated structures can create severe injury and property damage exposure when components fail. Current inspection evidence is necessary when deterioration is visible.

Document the structure condition, inspector findings, repair information, and underwriting action.

**600.R** Require inspection when swimming pools, spas, ponds, or similar water features are present. Confirm safeguards, access controls, condition, and apparent liability hazards.

Water features increase bodily injury exposure and require reliable information regarding control measures. Inspection identifies hazards not disclosed on the application.

Document the water feature, safeguards observed, inspection findings, and final disposition.

**600.S** Inspect risks with trampolines, playground equipment, recreational structures, or other apparent attractive hazards. Refer any risk with damaged equipment or inadequate controls.

Recreational hazards can create significant liability exposure. Inspection evidence supports evaluation of condition, supervision indicators, and access controls.

Document the equipment observed, condition concerns, safeguards, and underwriting decision.

**600.T** Require inspection when fencing, gates, or access barriers appear damaged, incomplete, or ineffective. Refer the risk when barrier condition affects a known hazard.

Barriers can be material to liability control and property protection. Inspection confirms whether the barrier performs its intended function.

Document the barrier location, condition, affected hazard, and required corrective action.

**600.U** Inspect risks with animals that present an apparent liability concern or where animal information is incomplete. Refer the risk when inspection findings conflict with disclosed information.

Animal exposure depends on actual premises conditions, containment, and observed behavior indicators. Inspection provides independent support for underwriting review.

Document the animal information, containment observations, inspection findings, and referral outcome.

**600.V** Require inspection when business activity, commercial storage, or unusual traffic is apparent at the premises. Do not bind until the actual use is evaluated.

Undisclosed business activity can materially alter property, liability, and occupancy exposure. Inspection evidence establishes whether the risk fits personal lines eligibility.

Document the observed activity, occupancy information, inspection findings, and underwriting disposition.

**600.W** Inspect risks with construction activity, renovation, demolition, or incomplete repairs. Refer the risk when work creates an unaddressed hazard or changes the exposure.

Construction conditions can increase fire, theft, water damage, and liability potential. Current evidence is required to evaluate the premises condition.

Document the work observed, stage of completion, inspection findings, and final action.

**600.X** Require inspection when insured structures appear materially different from application descriptions. Resolve discrepancies before binding or continuing coverage.

Accurate construction, occupancy, and condition information is necessary for proper underwriting. Inspection identifies material discrepancies requiring correction or referral.

Document the application discrepancy, inspection evidence, corrected information, and disposition.

**600.Y** Inspect risks with apparent additions, converted areas, detached structures, or unreported improvements. Refer any risk when the use, construction, or condition is unclear.

Unreported improvements can affect valuation, eligibility, and loss exposure. Inspection establishes whether the property information is complete.

Document the structure or improvement observed, actual use, inspection findings, and underwriting action.

**600.Z** Require inspection evidence when a dwelling is seasonal, intermittently occupied, or otherwise not regularly monitored. Confirm maintenance, security, and loss prevention conditions.

Limited monitoring can delay discovery of water, fire, theft, and weather damage. Inspection provides current evidence of premises care.

Document the occupancy pattern, security observations, inspection findings, and final disposition.

**600.AA** Inspect risks with prior damage that appears unrepaired, improperly repaired, or inconsistent with reported loss history. Refer the risk when repair quality cannot be established.

Prior damage can indicate continuing exposure or a likelihood of repeat loss. Inspection evidence supports evaluation of repair completion and condition.

Document the damage observed, repair evidence, inspection findings, and underwriting decision.

**600.AB** Require inspection when claim information indicates a recurring property condition. Evaluate whether the underlying cause has been corrected.

Recurring conditions may produce repeated loss activity if the cause remains unresolved. Inspection confirms whether prior mitigation remains effective.

Document the claim-related condition, mitigation evidence, inspection findings, and final action.

**600.AC** Inspect risks with visible fire damage, smoke staining, charring, or heat effects. Refer the risk unless the cause, repair status, and current condition are adequately established.

Fire-related evidence may indicate unrepaired damage or unsafe building systems. Inspection is necessary to identify remaining hazards.

Document the observed evidence, repair information, inspection findings, and referral outcome.

**600.AD** Require inspection when the dwelling has visible signs of pest, rodent, or insect activity that may affect structure or wiring. Refer the risk when damage appears material or active.

Pest activity can contribute to structural deterioration, wiring damage, and sanitation concerns. Inspection supports evaluation of the extent and control status.

Document the observed activity, affected areas, treatment information, and underwriting disposition.

**600.AE** Inspect risks with damaged windows, doors, shutters, screens, or exterior openings. Confirm whether the condition affects security, weather resistance, or maintenance.

Compromised openings can increase theft, wind, water intrusion, and property damage exposure. Inspection distinguishes minor wear from material hazard.

Document the opening condition, affected area, inspection evidence, and required corrective action.

**600.AF** Require inspection when roofing accessories, gutters, downspouts, or flashing appear damaged or poorly maintained. Refer any risk with evidence of active water entry.

Roof drainage components affect the likelihood of water intrusion and exterior deterioration. Inspection determines whether the condition requires remediation.

Document the component observed, condition findings, repair evidence, and underwriting action.

**600.AG** Inspect risks with detached garages, sheds, workshops, or similar structures that appear deteriorated or unusually used. Evaluate condition, use, access, and hazard potential.

Detached structures can create property and liability exposure beyond the main dwelling. Inspection identifies material conditions not reflected in the application.

Document the structure observed, use, condition, inspection findings, and final disposition.

**600.AH** Require inspection when fuel storage, tanks, cylinders, or related equipment is present or apparent. Refer the risk when installation, condition, or separation concerns are observed.

Fuel-related equipment can increase fire, explosion, and environmental exposure. Inspection provides evidence of visible hazard controls and maintenance.

Document the equipment type, location, condition observations, and underwriting decision.

**600.AI** Inspect risks with generators, solar equipment, wind equipment, or other exterior utility installations. Refer any risk with damaged, altered, or apparently unsafe equipment.

Utility installations can create fire, electrical, wind, and maintenance exposure. Inspection establishes whether the equipment appears suitable for the premises.

Document the equipment observed, installation condition, inspection findings, and referral result.

**600.AJ** Require inspection when utility service lines, meters, poles, or connections appear damaged or improvised. Do not accept the risk until the condition is resolved or referred.

Utility defects can create fire, electrical, and service interruption exposure. Current inspection evidence is necessary when visible conditions suggest unsafe installation.

Document the utility concern, affected equipment, inspection findings, and final action.

**600.AK** Inspect risks with apparent code enforcement activity, posted notices, or government warnings. Refer the risk when the notice affects safety, occupancy, or property condition.

Official notices may indicate unresolved hazards or prohibited use. Inspection clarifies the current status and underwriting impact.

Document the notice observed, applicant explanation, inspection evidence, and referral disposition.

**600.AL** Require inspection when access to the dwelling is limited, obstructed, or unsafe. Do not complete underwriting based on incomplete exterior evidence.

Limited access can prevent confirmation of material risk conditions. Inspection must provide sufficient evidence for a reliable underwriting decision.

Document the access limitation, inspection scope, missing information, and final disposition.

**600.AM** Inspect risks when aerial imagery, third-party data, or underwriting reports conflict with applicant information. Resolve material discrepancies through reliable inspection evidence.

Conflicting information can affect eligibility, valuation, and hazard assessment. Inspection provides current confirmation of the actual premises condition.

Document the conflicting information, inspection findings, corrected data, and underwriting action.

**600.AN** Require inspection when a prior inspection contains unresolved recommendations. Refer the risk when completion evidence is unavailable or inadequate.

Unresolved recommendations may represent continuing property or liability hazards. Underwriting requires confirmation that material conditions have been addressed.

Document the prior recommendation, completion evidence, current inspection findings, and final disposition.

**600.AO** Inspect risks when inspection photographs are unclear, outdated, incomplete, or inconsistent with reported conditions. Obtain usable evidence before relying on the inspection.

Clear evidence is necessary to support underwriting conclusions and file review. Inadequate photographs can conceal material hazards.

Document the photograph deficiency, replacement evidence, inspection findings, and underwriting decision.

**600.AP** Require inspection evidence from a reliable source with sufficient authority and relevant expertise. Do not rely on altered, incomplete, or unverifiable inspection material.

Reliable evidence supports consistent underwriting and defensible file decisions. Verification reduces the risk of accepting inaccurate condition information.

Document the inspection source, verification performed, evidence reviewed, and final action.

**600.AQ** Review inspection findings promptly and determine whether the risk remains acceptable. Refer any material hazard that cannot be resolved within underwriting authority.

Timely review prevents binding or continuing coverage without a supported risk decision. Prompt action also supports effective hazard correction.

Document the review date, material findings, authority used, and disposition.

**600.AR** Issue corrective requirements only when the inspection identifies a material condition affecting acceptability. State the required outcome clearly and avoid speculative repair direction.

Corrective requirements must address actual underwriting concerns and permit objective verification. Clear requirements support consistent follow-up.

Document the condition requiring correction, required outcome, due status, and verification method.

**600.AS** Confirm completion of required corrective action through reliable evidence. Do not close the requirement based solely on an unsupported verbal statement.

Completion evidence confirms that the underwriting concern has been addressed. Reliable verification protects the file from unresolved hazard exposure.

Document the evidence received, reviewer determination, remaining concerns, and closure action.

**600.AT** Refer any risk when inspection findings reveal a hazard outside normal underwriting authority. Do not waive material conditions without authorized approval.

Referral ensures consistent treatment of unusual or severe exposures. Authorized review is required when standard acceptance criteria do not resolve the risk.

Document the referred condition, supporting inspection evidence, approval authority, and final disposition.

**600.AU** Decline or cancel consideration when inspection access, requested evidence, or material information is withheld. Apply the action only as permitted by applicable underwriting procedures.

Failure to provide inspection evidence prevents reliable risk evaluation. Underwriting cannot support acceptance when material conditions remain unknown.

Document the information requested, efforts to obtain it, response received, and action taken.

**600.AV** Protect inspection records, photographs, reports, and related communications in the underwriting file. Restrict access and use to legitimate underwriting purposes.

Inspection material may contain sensitive property and occupancy information. Proper file handling supports privacy, consistency, and audit readiness.

Document the inspection material retained, source, review status, and any access limitation applied.

**600.AW** Use inspection findings as underwriting evidence and not as a substitute for professional repair advice. Refer technical questions requiring specialized evaluation.

Underwriting staff must evaluate risk acceptability rather than direct construction methods. Specialized issues require qualified assessment beyond ordinary underwriting review.

Document the technical issue, specialist evidence requested, inspection findings, and underwriting disposition.

**600.AX** Close the inspection process only after all material findings are resolved, referred, or documented as accepted within authority. Maintain a clear file record supporting the final decision.

A complete inspection record demonstrates that material conditions received appropriate underwriting review. Clear closure documentation supports later servicing, claims review, and audit activity.

Document the final inspection status, outstanding conditions if any, authority applied, and final underwriting action.

## Rule 610 — Documentation Standards

**610.A** Record underwriting facts in the case record when received. Identify the source of each fact.

Clear sourcing supports review of the underwriting basis. It prevents unsupported assumptions from entering the record.

Document the source, receipt method, and reviewer action in the case record. Preserve the related record material.

**610.B** Enter material risk characteristics using carrier-approved terminology. Do not substitute informal descriptions for established risk terms.

Consistent terminology supports reliable risk selection. It also improves later file review.

Document any clarification needed to align source information with carrier terminology. State the final underwriting interpretation.

**610.C** Record the date information is received when timing affects underwriting action. Distinguish received information from later verification.

Timing can affect whether information remains suitable for use. Clear timing also supports audit review.

Document the receipt date and the verification date in the case record. Explain any material gap between them.

**610.D** Identify the person or source providing material risk information. Record the source without characterizing its reliability unless the record supports that characterization.

Source identification allows later review of the information basis. It also helps resolve conflicting information.

Document the source name or source type and the information provided. Record any source limitations known to underwriting.

**610.E** Record material discrepancies when sources provide conflicting risk information. Resolve the discrepancy before completing underwriting action when the conflict affects eligibility, classification, or terms.

Unresolved conflicts can produce unsupported underwriting results. Prompt resolution protects record integrity.

Document the conflicting information, the resolution, and the basis for the resolution. Record the reviewer responsible for the action.

**610.F** Record the underwriting decision in clear operational language. State whether the risk is acceptable, declined, referred, restricted, or subject to condition.

A clear decision record supports consistent downstream handling. It also prevents later uncertainty about underwriting intent.

Document the decision status and the basis for that status. Record any required follow-up action.

**610.G** Record the reason for any referral. Describe the risk characteristic requiring elevated review.

Referral rationale allows the reviewing authority to evaluate the matter efficiently. It also shows that the referral was purposeful.

Document the referral trigger and the information provided for review. Record the resulting direction in the case record.

**610.H** Record all material conditions imposed on an acceptable risk. State the condition in language that can be acted upon by underwriting staff.

Conditions must be clear to support proper follow-up. Ambiguous conditions can create avoidable handling errors.

Document the condition, its purpose, and the party responsible for follow-up. Record completion or failure to complete the condition.

**610.I** Record material eligibility findings before issuing an underwriting decision. Do not rely on implied eligibility.

Eligibility findings support the carrier's risk selection standards. They also allow later confirmation that the risk was handled properly.

Document the facts supporting the eligibility finding. Record any exception authority used.

**610.J** Record material occupancy information. Describe occupancy based on verified risk facts rather than assumptions.

Occupancy affects exposure assessment and underwriting treatment. Unsupported occupancy descriptions can misstate the risk.

Document the occupancy information and its source. Record any unresolved occupancy concern for follow-up.

**610.K** Record material ownership information. Identify ownership circumstances that affect underwriting eligibility or insurable interest review.

Ownership information may alter the risk relationship and underwriting approach. Clear recording supports accurate handling.

Document the ownership facts relied upon and their source. Record any limitation or concern identified.

**610.L** Record material construction characteristics. Use the carrier's approved risk descriptions where available.

Construction characteristics influence hazard evaluation and risk classification. Consistent descriptions support reliable underwriting.

Document the characteristic reviewed and the source supporting it. Record any discrepancy requiring additional review.

**610.M** Record material protection information. Distinguish verified protection features from features reported without verification.

Protection information can affect risk assessment and required underwriting action. The record must show the confidence level of the information used.

Document the protection feature, its source, and its verification status. Record any required follow-up.

**610.N** Record material loss information considered in underwriting. Describe the loss information without minimizing or exaggerating the reported facts.

Loss information may indicate recurring exposure or maintenance concerns. Neutral descriptions support fair underwriting judgment.

Document the loss information reviewed and its source. Record the underwriting significance assigned to the information.

**610.O** Record material maintenance concerns identified during underwriting. State the observed concern and its effect on risk acceptability.

Maintenance concerns can indicate elevated hazard or a need for corrective action. Clear records support consistent treatment.

Document the concern, the source of the information, and the required action. Record the final disposition of the concern.

**610.P** Record material hazard information promptly after it is identified. Refer the risk when the hazard requires authority beyond the handler's limit.

Prompt recording supports timely risk control action. It also prevents material hazards from being overlooked.

Document the hazard, referral action, and resulting direction. Record any restriction applied to the risk.

**610.Q** Record material changes reported after initial review. Reassess the underwriting decision when the change affects the risk basis.

Risk conditions can change after information is initially evaluated. The record must show whether the change altered underwriting treatment.

Document the reported change and its source. Record the reassessment outcome and any resulting action.

**610.R** Record all underwriting assumptions that materially affect the decision. Do not leave material assumptions unstated.

Stated assumptions permit later validation of the underwriting basis. Hidden assumptions can undermine decision quality.

Document the assumption and the facts supporting it. Record any action needed to confirm or replace the assumption.

**610.S** Record exceptions to normal underwriting practice. Obtain appropriate authority before treating an exception as approved.

Exception handling requires a clear record of authority and rationale. This protects against unauthorized risk acceptance.

Document the exception, the reason requested, and the approval authority. Record any limitation attached to the approval.

**610.T** Record adverse information even when the risk remains acceptable. Do not omit adverse information because a favorable decision is reached.

Complete records show the actual underwriting analysis. They also support future review if risk conditions change.

Document the adverse information and the reason it did not alter the decision. Record any monitoring or follow-up action.

**610.U** Record favorable information when it materially offsets an adverse risk characteristic. State the relationship between the favorable and adverse information.

Balanced documentation supports sound underwriting judgment. It prevents the record from presenting an incomplete risk picture.

Document the favorable information and its source. Record the underwriting effect assigned to it.

**610.V** Record communications that provide material underwriting direction. Capture the direction accurately and without interpretation beyond the source.

Material direction may control the final handling of the risk. Accurate capture reduces the risk of later disagreement.

Document the communication source, content, and receipt date. Record the action taken in response.

**610.W** Record requests for additional risk information. State what information is needed and why it is material.

Clear requests promote efficient completion of underwriting review. They also show that unresolved concerns were actively managed.

Document the requested information and the request status. Record the response received or the lack of response.

**610.X** Record receipt of additional risk information. Evaluate whether the information resolves the stated underwriting concern.

Additional information must be tied to the reason it was requested. This prevents unsupported closure of open issues.

Document the information received and the resulting evaluation. Record any remaining concern.

**610.Y** Record material information that cannot be verified. Do not present unverified information as established fact.

Verification status affects the reliability of the underwriting basis. Clear distinction supports proper judgment.

Document the information, the attempted verification, and the outcome. Record the underwriting treatment applied because verification was unavailable.

**610.Z** Record the basis for accepting information from a nontraditional source. Refer the matter when source reliability cannot be reasonably assessed.

Some sources require added scrutiny before their information can support underwriting action. The record must show why the source was used.

Document the source, the information relied upon, and the basis for reliance. Record any referral direction obtained.

**610.AA** Record the inspection status when inspection information is relevant to underwriting. Treat an inspection report as valid for 18 from its completion.

Inspection information may no longer represent the current risk condition. Clear status recording supports proper use of inspection findings.

Document the inspection completion date and the underwriting action based on the report. Record any reason the report was not used.

**610.AB** Record material inspection findings in the case record. Identify findings that affect eligibility, conditions, restrictions, or referral.

Inspection findings provide direct risk information that may require underwriting action. The record must show how material findings were handled.

Document the finding, its underwriting effect, and the action required. Record completion status for required corrective action.

**610.AC** Record the source of inspection information when it is not provided through normal carrier handling. Identify any limitation affecting the information's reliability.

Inspection information from alternate sources may require additional review. Source limitations can affect the weight given to the findings.

Document the source and the limitation identified. Record the underwriting determination regarding use of the information.

**610.AD** Record material photographs or visual evidence reviewed during underwriting. Describe the observed condition without drawing unsupported conclusions.

Visual evidence can support or challenge reported risk information. Neutral descriptions preserve the integrity of the record.

Document the observed condition and the source of the visual evidence. Record the underwriting action taken.

**610.AE** Record material property condition concerns. State whether the concern is observed, reported, or inferred from available information.

Property condition affects hazard assessment and risk quality. The record must distinguish fact from inference.

Document the condition concern and its basis. Record any required verification or corrective action.

**610.AF** Record material prior underwriting decisions when they affect current handling. Do not treat prior handling as authority without confirming its relevance.

Prior decisions may provide useful context but may not reflect current risk facts. Independent review protects against unsupported repetition.

Document the prior decision and the current relevance assessment. Record the basis for following or departing from prior handling.

**610.AG** Record the authority level used for a material underwriting action. Refer the matter when required authority is not available.

Authority records support accountability and proper escalation. They also show that limits were observed.

Document the authority relied upon and the resulting action. Record the referral outcome when escalation occurred.

**610.AH** Record material risk-control recommendations affecting underwriting treatment. Distinguish recommended action from required action.

Risk-control recommendations may inform but do not always determine underwriting disposition. Clear distinction prevents improper enforcement.

Document the recommendation and its underwriting significance. Record any requirement imposed as a separate underwriting action.

**610.AI** Record completion evidence for required corrective action. Do not close the requirement based solely on an unsupported representation.

Completion evidence supports confirmation that the risk condition was addressed. It also prevents premature removal of restrictions.

Document the evidence reviewed and the completion determination. Record any remaining limitation.

**610.AJ** Record the basis for declining a risk. State the material underwriting reason without using vague or conclusory language.

A clear decline basis supports consistent risk selection. It also permits later review of the decision.

Document the decline reason and the facts supporting it. Record the authority responsible for the decision.

**610.AK** Record the basis for withdrawing an adverse underwriting action when new information changes the risk assessment. Explain how the new information changed the decision.

Revised decisions require a traceable underwriting rationale. The record must show that the change was deliberate and supported.

Document the prior action, new information, and revised determination. Record the authority for the revised handling.

**610.AL** Record material risk characteristics that remain unresolved. Do not finalize underwriting treatment while an unresolved characteristic requires further review.

Unresolved characteristics can materially affect eligibility or terms. Clear recording ensures the issue remains visible.

Document the unresolved characteristic and the action required. Record the current handling status.

**610.AM** Record material limitations on available information. State what information is unavailable and why the limitation affects review.

Information limitations may narrow the basis for underwriting judgment. The record must show how the limitation was addressed.

Document the unavailable information and the reason it could not be obtained. Record the resulting underwriting treatment.

**610.AN** Record material statements made by an applicant or representative. Attribute the statement to its source and avoid converting it into verified fact.

Reported statements can be relevant without being independently confirmed. Attribution preserves the proper evidentiary status.

Document the statement, source, and verification status. Record the underwriting use of the statement.

**610.AO** Record material external information used in underwriting. Identify the source and the risk characteristic supported by that information.

External information may supplement information supplied through ordinary handling. Clear identification supports later validation.

Document the external source and the information relied upon. Record any limitation noted during review.

**610.AP** Record the reason for any underwriting hold. State the pending matter preventing final action.

A hold must be traceable to a specific underwriting need. Clear records prevent risks from remaining inactive without purpose.

Document the hold reason and the information or action required. Record the release decision when the hold is removed.

**610.AQ** Record material contact attempts related to unresolved underwriting issues. Describe the purpose of the contact attempt.

Contact activity demonstrates active handling of open matters. It also supports appropriate next action when information is unavailable.

Document the contact method, recipient, and result. Record the resulting underwriting disposition.

**610.AR** Record material communication failures that affect risk review. Escalate the matter when the failure prevents proper underwriting action.

Communication failures can leave material questions unresolved. The record must show that the limitation was recognized.

Document the communication failure and its underwriting impact. Record the escalation or alternate action taken.

**610.AS** Record material data-entry corrections. Preserve the reason for the correction when the corrected item affects underwriting treatment.

Corrections can alter the apparent basis for a decision. A clear correction history supports auditability.

Document the corrected item, prior entry, and reason for correction. Record the reviewer making the correction.

**610.AT** Record material system limitations affecting underwriting review. Do not rely on unavailable system information as though it had been reviewed.

System limitations can create gaps in the underwriting basis. Clear documentation supports appropriate alternative handling.

Document the limitation and the information affected. Record the alternative source or action used.

**610.AU** Record material underwriting notes in a professional and factual manner. Do not include speculation, commentary, or language unrelated to risk evaluation.

Professional notes support consistent carrier handling. They also reduce ambiguity during later review.

Document only information relevant to the underwriting decision or required follow-up. Remove or correct immaterial commentary when identified.

**610.AV** Record material actions promptly after completion. Do not rely on memory to recreate underwriting activity.

Timely records are more accurate and more useful to later reviewers. Delayed entry can obscure the actual decision basis.

Document the action and the time of entry in the case record. Explain any material delay in recording.

**610.AW** Record the final underwriting rationale when a risk presents unusual characteristics. Connect the rationale to the actual risk facts reviewed.

Unusual risks require a clear explanation of the underwriting judgment. The record must show why the disposition is appropriate.

Document the unusual characteristic and the rationale for the final decision. Record the authority involved when applicable.

**610.AX** Record material restrictions applied to an acceptable risk. State the operational effect of the restriction.

Restrictions must be understood by all handling parties. Vague restrictions can result in inconsistent administration.

Document the restriction, its underwriting purpose, and the authority for it. Record any later change to the restriction.

**610.AY** Record material endorsements or coverage-related underwriting direction only as underwriting instructions. Do not interpret coverage terms in the case record.

Underwriting records must preserve underwriting intent without replacing formal coverage administration. This supports appropriate separation of functions.

Document the underwriting direction and the risk reason supporting it. Record the responsible underwriting authority.

**610.AZ** Record material cancellation or nonrenewal underwriting rationale. State the risk facts supporting the action.

Adverse servicing action requires a complete and supportable record. Clear rationale assists later review and handling.

Document the action basis, source information, and authority. Record any required notice handling direction without interpreting notice requirements.

**610.BA** Record material quality-review findings affecting underwriting work. Correct identified deficiencies when the finding is supported.

Quality review helps maintain consistent application of carrier standards. The case record must show how material findings were addressed.

Document the quality finding and corrective action. Record the final resolution status.

**610.BB** Record any material departure from documented underwriting direction. Obtain appropriate authority before proceeding with the departure.

Departures may create unsupported risk treatment if not reviewed. Clear escalation protects underwriting discipline.

Document the direction, the departure, and the reason for it. Record the approval authority and final disposition.

## Rule 700 — Renewal Underwriting

**700.A** Review every renewal file for changes that affect eligibility, exposure, valuation, occupancy, or loss potential. Refer material changes that require underwriting judgment.

Renewal underwriting preserves alignment between the insured risk and carrier appetite.

Document the review outcome and the basis for any referral in the renewal file.

**700.B** Confirm that the named insured, mailing address, risk address, and contact information remain suitable for renewal processing. Resolve conflicting information before releasing renewal terms.

Accurate party and location information supports valid underwriting decisions and reliable correspondence.

Document verified changes and the source used to resolve discrepancies.

**700.C** Review the current policy record for unresolved underwriting conditions. Do not renew without addressing conditions that remain material to the risk.

Unresolved conditions may indicate continuing exposure outside carrier standards.

Document the condition status, required action, and underwriting disposition.

**700.D** Review loss activity reported during the current policy term. Refer any renewal risk with 2 paid property claims for underwriting review.

Loss activity may indicate adverse loss potential or a need to revise terms.

Document the loss review, referral action, and final underwriting decision.

**700.E** Evaluate reported losses for cause, severity, recurrence, and corrective action. Refer losses that suggest an unresolved hazard or unacceptable loss pattern.

Loss characteristics may be more significant than loss coding alone.

Document the loss facts considered and any corrective action verified.

**700.F** Confirm whether prior loss repairs have been completed where repair status affects risk acceptability. Refer incomplete repairs that leave material exposure.

Incomplete repairs can increase the likelihood or extent of future loss.

Document repair evidence, outstanding work, and underwriting action.

**700.G** Review changes in occupancy, use, or household activity that may alter the insured exposure. Refer a risk when reported use falls outside filed eligibility or carrier appetite.

Occupancy and use changes can materially affect hazard, liability, and property loss potential.

Document the reported use and the basis for the eligibility determination.

**700.H** Verify that the dwelling remains owner occupied when owner occupancy is required for the coverage offered. Refer any occupancy that is unclear, vacant, tenant occupied, or business related.

Occupancy status affects eligibility, pricing, and available coverage.

Document the occupancy verification and the source of information.

**700.I** Refer a renewal risk with vacancy, unoccupancy, or restricted access that may impair maintenance or loss prevention. Apply underwriting action consistent with the observed exposure.

Properties without regular oversight may present elevated property and liability hazards.

Document the condition, reason for referral, and disposition.

**700.J** Review the file for changes in construction, renovation, addition, or demolition. Refer changes that affect replacement cost, protection class, or hazard.

Physical alterations may change the insured value and the nature of the exposure.

Document the reported alteration and any valuation or eligibility action.

**700.K** Confirm that replacement cost information remains reasonable for the insured property. Refer values that appear unsupported, outdated, or inconsistent with known characteristics.

Accurate valuation supports appropriate limits and reduces settlement concerns.

Document the valuation review and any revised information obtained.

**700.L** Review protective device information when credits, eligibility, or loss control requirements depend on the device. Remove unsupported credits and refer unavailable or impaired required protection.

Protection features must be present and functional when relied upon for underwriting.

Document verification, credit action, and any required follow-up.

**700.M** Review premises maintenance information for evidence of deferred upkeep or hazardous conditions. Refer conditions that may create material property or liability exposure.

Poor maintenance can indicate increased loss frequency or severity.

Document the observed condition, information source, and underwriting action.

**700.N** Refer any risk with unrepaired roof damage, exterior deterioration, or water intrusion concerns. Obtain sufficient information to determine whether the exposure remains acceptable.

Building envelope conditions may lead to recurring weather or water losses.

Document the condition review and the basis for the decision.

**700.O** Review plumbing, heating, electrical, and mechanical system information when available. Refer systems that appear unsafe, obsolete, impaired, or inadequately maintained.

Core building systems can create significant fire, water, and liability exposure.

Document the system information reviewed and any underwriting requirement.

**700.P** Refer a renewal risk with evidence of repeated water damage, leakage, seepage, or moisture conditions. Require satisfactory mitigation before accepting continued exposure where warranted.

Water-related conditions may signal unresolved maintenance or system failure.

Document the loss history, mitigation information, and final disposition.

**700.Q** Review prior inspection findings before renewal action. Do not disregard material findings merely because the policy is renewing.

Inspection findings provide direct evidence of current risk conditions.

Document the inspection findings considered and the action taken.

**700.R** An inspection report remains valid for 12 months. Obtain updated information when the report is no longer valid or when conditions may have changed.

Current inspection information supports reliable renewal underwriting.

Document the inspection date, report status, and any updated evidence obtained.

**700.S** Refer any renewal risk when inspection information conflicts with the application, policy record, claim record, or other reliable evidence. Resolve the conflict before finalizing terms.

Conflicting information may indicate inaccurate risk classification or undisclosed hazards.

Document the conflicting information and the resolution reached.

**700.T** Review property photographs when photographs are available and relevant to the renewal decision. Refer images that show material deterioration, unusual use, or unaddressed hazards.

Photographs may reveal conditions not apparent from policy data alone.

Document the image review and any resulting underwriting action.

**700.U** Evaluate whether the insured has complied with prior loss control requirements. Refer noncompliance that remains material to continued acceptability.

Loss control requirements are intended to reduce identified exposure.

Document the requirement, compliance status, and disposition.

**700.V** Review prior underwriting exceptions at renewal. Do not continue an exception without confirming that the original basis remains valid.

Exceptions require ongoing oversight because the risk may remain outside normal standards.

Document the exception review and approval for any continued variance.

**700.W** Refer a renewal risk when material information remains unavailable after reasonable follow-up. Do not assume favorable facts where the file is incomplete.

Incomplete information prevents reliable assessment of exposure.

Document the missing information, follow-up efforts, and underwriting disposition.

**700.X** Review cancellation, nonrenewal, and rescission history when disclosed or present in carrier records. Refer history that may affect eligibility, moral hazard, or underwriting confidence.

Prior adverse policy action may indicate unresolved concerns requiring review.

Document the history considered and the basis for the decision.

**700.Y** Evaluate payment history when it is available for renewal review. Refer patterns that require underwriting attention under carrier practice.

Payment behavior may affect servicing expectations and renewal handling.

Document the payment information reviewed and any action taken.

**700.Z** Review changes in ownership interest, trust status, estate status, or legal responsibility for the insured property. Refer changes that affect insurable interest or policy issuance.

Legal interest must remain clear for continued coverage.

Document the reported change and the evidence supporting the renewal action.

**700.AA** Confirm that all named insureds remain properly shown in the policy record. Refer additions or removals that create uncertainty regarding ownership, occupancy, or insurable interest.

Accurate named insured information supports proper policy administration.

Document the requested change and the basis for acceptance or referral.

**700.AB** Review mortgagee, lienholder, and additional interest information for material changes. Correct known errors before renewal issuance.

Interested party information affects notices, payments, and policy servicing.

Document the information reviewed and any correction completed.

**700.AC** Refer any renewal risk involving foreclosure, receivership, probate, or other impaired ownership circumstances. Obtain sufficient information to confirm continued eligibility.

Impaired ownership circumstances may affect occupancy, maintenance, and insurable interest.

Document the reported circumstance and the underwriting determination.

**700.AD** Review exposure arising from business activity conducted at the insured location. Refer activity that may create commercial, professional, manufacturing, retail, or public-access exposure.

Business activity can create hazards not contemplated by personal lines coverage.

Document the activity described and the basis for the eligibility decision.

**700.AE** Refer a renewal risk with short-term rental, hosted rental, room rental, or similar occupancy activity. Determine whether the activity is eligible before offering renewal.

Rental activity may alter property, liability, and loss frequency exposure.

Document the activity, occupancy facts, and underwriting disposition.

**700.AF** Review animal exposure when animal information is reported or identified. Refer animals, breeds, prior incidents, or conditions that may exceed carrier appetite.

Animal liability exposure may change during the policy term.

Document the animal information reviewed and any required action.

**700.AG** Review recreational property, watercraft, recreational vehicle, or similar incidental exposure when associated with the insured premises. Refer exposure that requires separate eligibility review.

Incidental exposures may create liability conditions beyond the basic premises risk.

Document the exposure identified and the action taken.

**700.AH** Refer a renewal risk with trampoline, pool, diving feature, playground equipment, or similar attractive nuisance exposure when the condition affects acceptability. Confirm required safeguards when applicable.

Attractive nuisance exposures may increase bodily injury potential.

Document the feature, safeguards reviewed, and underwriting decision.

**700.AI** Review fencing, gates, handrails, stairs, walkways, and similar premises features when condition information is available. Refer material defects that may create liability exposure.

Premises defects may contribute to preventable injury claims.

Document the condition and any corrective action required.

**700.AJ** Refer any renewal risk with fire protection concerns, restricted emergency access, or impaired protective services. Evaluate whether the exposure remains within carrier appetite.

Protection limitations may increase expected property loss severity.

Document the protection concern and the basis for the disposition.

**700.AK** Review geographic hazard information available in the underwriting file. Refer a risk when known hazard conditions require special underwriting consideration.

Location characteristics may affect catastrophe, weather, wildfire, or water exposure.

Document the hazard information considered and the action taken.

**700.AL** Evaluate prior catastrophe losses for evidence of unresolved damage, inadequate repairs, or repeated exposure concerns. Refer material concerns before renewing.

Catastrophe history may affect property condition and future loss potential.

Document the catastrophe review and the disposition.

**700.AM** Review changes in surrounding conditions that may affect access, protection, drainage, or property exposure. Refer material changes that alter the risk profile.

External conditions can affect the insured property even when the structure is unchanged.

Document the changed condition and the information source.

**700.AN** Refer a renewal risk with sinkhole, subsidence, earth movement, or foundation concerns. Obtain sufficient evidence to determine whether continued coverage is acceptable.

Ground stability conditions may produce severe property damage and recurring exposure.

Document the concern, supporting information, and underwriting action.

**700.AO** Review drainage, grading, water flow, and prior flooding information when available. Refer conditions that indicate material water accumulation exposure.

Site drainage conditions may contribute to water damage or liability loss.

Document the conditions reviewed and any mitigation information received.

**700.AP** Refer a renewal risk with known mold, contamination, smoke residue, or environmental condition affecting the insured property. Determine whether remediation is complete and satisfactory.

Environmental conditions may indicate ongoing property damage or habitability concerns.

Document the condition, remediation status, and disposition.

**700.AQ** Review fraud indicators, material misrepresentation concerns, and inconsistencies in risk information. Refer suspected fraud or intentional concealment without advising the insured of internal review criteria.

Reliable underwriting depends on truthful and supportable risk information.

Document the concern, supporting facts, and referral handling.

**700.AR** Handle suspicious claim patterns with care and restrict file comments to objective facts. Refer concerns that require special investigation or underwriting review.

Objective documentation supports appropriate handling and preserves file integrity.

Document verified facts and the referral outcome.

**700.AS** Review underwriting correspondence returned as undeliverable. Resolve address concerns before issuing renewal communications.

Undeliverable mail may indicate inaccurate contact information or occupancy changes.

Document the returned correspondence and steps taken to obtain current information.

**700.AT** Issue a notice of intent not to renew only after completing the required underwriting review and approval process. Use factual, supportable reasons consistent with the file record.

Adverse renewal action requires clear internal support and controlled handling.

Document the reason, approval, and mailing record in the renewal file.

**700.AU** Use the general cancellation notice only when cancellation action is authorized and supported by the underwriting file. Do not use cancellation correspondence as a substitute for renewal review.

Cancellation handling requires a distinct basis and controlled communication.

Document the cancellation basis, authorization, and correspondence action.

**700.AV** Review renewal offers for consistency with the final underwriting decision. Correct terms that do not reflect approved eligibility, coverage, or rating action.

Renewal documents must match the underwriting record.

Document the verification and any correction made before release.

**700.AW** Refer any request to broaden coverage when current risk information is insufficient to support the request. Obtain required underwriting information before approving expanded protection.

Broader coverage may increase exposure beyond the original underwriting basis.

Document the request, information obtained, and decision.

**700.AX** Review requests to reduce coverage when the change may affect valuation, lender interest, or underwriting acceptability. Confirm that the requested change is understood and permissible.

Coverage reductions may create servicing or protection concerns.

Document the request, explanation provided, and final action.

**700.AY** Review deductible changes for consistency with carrier underwriting standards and current risk conditions. Refer changes that appear intended to address known or imminent loss.

Deductible changes should not be used to manage a loss already in progress.

Document the request, timing, and underwriting disposition.

**700.AZ** Refer a renewal risk when an insured reports a circumstance that may lead to a claim. Do not treat a known loss circumstance as routine renewal information.

Known loss circumstances require careful handling and timely referral.

Document the reported circumstance and action taken.

**700.BA** Review open claim information before finalizing a renewal decision. Refer open claims that prevent reliable assessment of the continuing exposure.

Open claims may involve unresolved damage, liability, or loss causation issues.

Document the open claim status and underwriting decision.

**700.BB** Review subrogation, recovery, salvage, and litigation information when it affects the loss picture. Refer material developments that alter the assessment of prior loss activity.

Loss outcomes may change as recovery or litigation develops.

Document the information considered and any revised underwriting action.

**700.BC** Confirm that renewal processing reflects any approved underwriting restrictions or conditions. Do not release terms that omit required limitations or endorsements.

Approved controls must appear accurately in the renewal transaction.

Document the transaction review and confirmation of required terms.

**700.BD** Refer a renewal risk when system data, third-party information, or insured statements cannot be reconciled. Obtain clarification before accepting unresolved contradictions.

Reliable decisions require a supportable record.

Document the sources reviewed, clarification requested, and disposition.

**700.BE** Review consumer report information only for permissible underwriting purposes and in accordance with carrier procedures. Refer adverse information requiring review or action.

Consumer information must be handled consistently and with appropriate controls.

Document the information used and the resulting underwriting action.

**700.BF** Protect nonpublic personal information during renewal review. Limit file content to information necessary to support underwriting action.

Controlled information handling reduces privacy and operational risk.

Document necessary underwriting facts without recording unnecessary sensitive detail.

**700.BG** Apply renewal decisions consistently to similarly situated risks. Refer circumstances that require an exception or individualized judgment.

Consistent treatment supports sound underwriting practice and controlled risk selection.

Document the applicable facts and the basis for any exception.

**700.BH** Obtain required underwriting approval before releasing a renewal that is outside assigned authority. Do not bind an exception without recorded approval.

Authority controls ensure appropriate oversight of elevated risk decisions.

Document the approver, approval basis, and renewal action.

**700.BI** Maintain a clear renewal file that supports the final disposition. Record material facts, sources, contacts, and decisions contemporaneously.

A complete file allows later review of underwriting judgment and compliance.

Document the completed renewal review and confirm that the file supports the action taken.

## Rule 800 — Cancellation and Nonrenewal Procedures

**800.A** Confirm that the proposed action is cancellation, nonrenewal, withdrawal, or binding suspension. Apply the procedure that matches the action.
  
Use the correct action type to preserve required rights and carrier controls. Do not substitute a nonrenewal process for a cancellation process.
  
Document the action type, the proposed effective event, and the responsible unit in the file.
  
**800.B** Verify the policy status before issuing any adverse action. Confirm that the policy is active and that no prior action controls the outcome.
  
Accurate status review prevents conflicting notices and unsupported termination activity. Policy records may change after an initial referral.
  
Document the status review and retain the policy activity record in the file.
  
**800.C** Confirm that the underwriting basis supports the proposed action. Refer uncertain grounds to underwriting leadership before notice preparation.
  
An adverse action must rest on a valid carrier reason. Unsupported action creates avoidable complaint and regulatory exposure.
  
Document the underwriting basis and the reviewer’s disposition in the file.
  
**800.D** Use verified policy, billing, inspection, claim, and applicant information when evaluating an adverse action. Do not rely on unconfirmed verbal reports.
  
Verified information supports consistent decisions and clear notice content. Unverified information may be incomplete or inaccurate.
  
Document the source of the information and the date of verification in the file.
  
**800.E** Issue a premium default notice that provides 10 days before cancellation for nonpayment of premium. State the amount due and the payment method available to cure the default.
  
Premium default action requires clear communication of the unpaid obligation. The notice must allow the applicable cure opportunity.
  
Document the billing status, notice content, delivery method, and planned cancellation effective event in the file.
  
**800.F** Use the notice of cancellation for other reasons when cancellation is not based on premium default. State the specific underwriting or policy reason supporting the action.
  
A clear reason permits proper review and reduces inconsistent communications. Generic language does not establish the carrier basis for action.
  
Document the reason used, supporting evidence, and notice issuance record in the file.
