**800.G** Issue notice of intent not to renew at least 45 days before the expiration event. Use the approved nonrenewal reason language.
  
Timely nonrenewal notice permits the customer to seek replacement coverage. Approved language promotes consistent carrier communications.
  
Document the expiration event, notice issue event, delivery method, and reason code in the file.
  
**800.H** Provide the renewal deductible notice when a renewal deductible change requires notice. Use the approved communication for the affected policy.
  
Deductible changes may affect the customer’s financial responsibility after a loss. Clear notice supports informed renewal decisions.
  
Document the deductible change, notice content, and delivery record in the file.
  
**800.I** Suspend new binding when forecast landfall is within 72 hours. Refer affected submissions for catastrophe review.
  
Binding controls reduce unmanaged accumulation during an approaching event. Referral permits authorized review of exceptional circumstances.
  
Document the forecast source, suspension status, referral activity, and underwriting disposition in the file.
  
**800.J** Apply binding suspension controls to new business, coverage increases, and location additions within the affected area. Do not process changes that circumvent the suspension.
  
Catastrophe controls apply to exposure changes as well as new submissions. Inconsistent application may create unintended concentration.
  
Document the requested transaction, affected location, and suspension decision in the file.
  
**800.K** Confirm the affected territory before applying catastrophe-related binding controls. Escalate uncertain location information for underwriting review.
  
Territory accuracy is necessary to apply suspension controls consistently. Location ambiguity can produce improper acceptance or rejection.
  
Document the location information reviewed and the territorial determination in the file.
  
**800.L** Release a binding suspension only after authorized underwriting confirmation. Do not rely on informal reports that conditions have improved.
  
Suspension release requires consistent carrier direction. Premature release may expose the carrier to unmanaged catastrophe risk.
  
Document the authorization, release event, and resumed processing status in the file.
  
**800.M** Stop automated adverse action processing when material policy information is under review. Resolve the pending information before issuing notice.
  
Automation cannot replace underwriting judgment where facts are incomplete. A temporary hold prevents inaccurate communications.
  
Document the hold reason, pending information, and resolution in the file.
  
**800.N** Review pending cancellation activity before accepting a policy change. Do not allow a routine endorsement to override an authorized cancellation action.
  
Policy servicing activity may conflict with adverse action processing. Coordinated handling protects the intended carrier decision.
  
Document the requested change, cancellation status, and servicing disposition in the file.
  
**800.O** Review pending nonrenewal activity before offering renewal terms. Block renewal output until the nonrenewal decision is resolved.
  
Renewal output may create confusion when an adverse action is pending. Controls prevent inconsistent carrier communications.
  
Document the renewal block, review outcome, and final processing direction in the file.
  
**800.P** Use the carrier-approved reason description in every cancellation and nonrenewal notice. Do not alter approved wording without underwriting authorization.
  
Approved language supports consistency and avoids unsupported statements. Modified wording may change the meaning of the action.
  
Document the reason description used and any authorized exception in the file.
  
**800.Q** State the applicable policy identifier and insured name on every adverse action notice. Verify that the notice addresses the correct named insured.
  
Accurate identification supports delivery and reduces misdirected notices. Identification errors may impair the intended action.
  
Document the identifying information reviewed before notice issuance.
  
**800.R** Send adverse action notices through an approved delivery channel. Use the delivery channel required for the policy jurisdiction and customer preference where permitted.
  
Proper delivery supports evidence that notice was issued and directed correctly. Unapproved delivery methods may not satisfy carrier requirements.
  
Document the delivery channel, mailing information, and transmission record in the file.
  
**800.S** Validate the mailing address before issuing an adverse action notice. Use the most current address available in carrier records.
  
A current address improves the likelihood of effective notice delivery. Returned mail may require further handling.
  
Document the address source, validation activity, and address used for notice delivery.
  
**800.T** Review returned adverse action mail promptly. Refer returned mail for address research and follow applicable carrier handling.
  
Returned mail may indicate that the customer did not receive the notice. Timely review supports appropriate corrective action.
  
Document the return event, research performed, and resulting handling in the file.
  
**800.U** Do not rescind an issued cancellation or nonrenewal notice without authorized approval. Confirm that the approval identifies the affected policy and action.
  
Rescission changes the customer’s coverage expectation and carrier position. Authorization protects against unauthorized reinstatement or continuation.
  
Document the approval, rescission communication, and revised policy status in the file.
  
**800.V** Confirm that any replacement notice supersedes prior notice content before issuance. Avoid sending conflicting adverse action communications.
  
Replacement notices must provide a clear carrier position. Conflicting notices can create uncertainty about coverage status.
  
Document the prior notice, replacement reason, and replacement delivery record in the file.
  
**800.W** Refer disputed cancellation or nonrenewal reasons to underwriting leadership. Do not resolve a material dispute through routine service handling.
  
Material disputes may involve underwriting judgment, complaint exposure, or legal concerns. Leadership review promotes consistent outcomes.
  
Document the dispute, referral, and authorized resolution in the file.
  
**800.X** Refer allegations of inaccurate underwriting information before proceeding with adverse action. Pause notice activity when the allegation could affect the decision.
  
Correcting material information may change the carrier action. Proceeding without review may produce an unsupported notice.
  
Document the allegation, information reviewed, and decision after review in the file.
  
**800.Y** Confirm that premium payment activity has posted before cancelling for premium default. Review pending payment records and authorized payment arrangements.
  
Recent payment activity may cure the stated default. Accurate payment review prevents improper cancellation.
  
Document the billing review, payment status, and cancellation disposition in the file.
  
**800.Z** Do not treat a partial payment as full cure unless billing confirms that the default is resolved. Refer unclear payment status to billing support.
  
Partial payment handling depends on the remaining obligation and carrier billing rules. Assumptions may result in incorrect termination activity.
  
Document the payment amount, billing confirmation, and resulting policy status in the file.
  
**800.AA** Confirm that a reinstatement request does not conflict with an active nonrenewal decision. Refer conflicting requests to underwriting leadership.
  
Reinstatement and nonrenewal actions require coordinated handling. Separate processing may produce inconsistent policy status.
  
Document the request, existing action status, and authorized disposition in the file.
  
**800.AB** Evaluate reinstatement requests using the facts available at the time of review. Do not promise reinstatement before authorized approval.
  
Reinstatement is an underwriting decision and may require review of changed risk conditions. Premature commitments can create customer reliance.
  
Document the request, reviewed facts, and final underwriting decision in the file.
  
**800.AC** Verify that no loss, claim report, or material change is pending before reinstatement approval. Refer material developments for underwriting review.
  
New loss information or risk changes may affect reinstatement eligibility. Complete review supports sound carrier decisions.
  
Document the loss inquiry, claim status, and underwriting review in the file.
  
**800.AD** Apply the adverse action process consistently across similarly situated risks. Refer apparent exceptions for approval before notice issuance.
  
Consistent treatment supports fair underwriting administration. Unapproved exceptions may create operational and complaint concerns.
  
Document the comparison basis and any approved exception in the file.
  
**800.AE** Do not use adverse action procedures to resolve service dissatisfaction. Refer service concerns to the appropriate servicing function.
  
Cancellation and nonrenewal must rest on valid carrier grounds. Service issues require separate handling.
  
Document the service concern, referral activity, and underwriting determination in the file.
  
**800.AF** Do not issue adverse action solely because a customer asked questions about coverage or billing. Evaluate the policy using applicable underwriting facts.
  
Customer inquiry is not an underwriting reason. Decisions must remain tied to valid risk or payment information.
  
Document the underwriting facts relied upon and the final action determination in the file.
  
**800.AG** Review claim information for relevance before using it as an adverse action basis. Do not rely on claim activity that does not support the stated reason.
  
Claim records may contain incomplete or unrelated information. Relevance review supports accurate notice language.
  
Document the claim information reviewed and its connection to the action reason.
  
**800.AH** Confirm that inspection findings are complete before relying on them for adverse action. Refer unclear or disputed findings for verification.
  
Inspection information may change after correction or clarification. Verified findings support defensible underwriting action.
  
Document the inspection record, verification activity, and findings used in the decision.
  
**800.AI** Allow correction evidence to be reviewed before acting on a remediable condition when carrier practice permits. Refer the evidence to the assigned underwriter.
  
Correction evidence may affect whether the stated condition remains present. Review promotes accurate underwriting results.
  
Document the evidence received, reviewer disposition, and resulting action decision in the file.
  
**800.AJ** Confirm that the named insured has authority for any request to withdraw, cancel, or alter an adverse action. Verify authority when the request comes from another party.
  
Authority review protects against unauthorized policy changes. Third-party communications may not reflect the named insured’s intent.
  
Document the requesting party, authority evidence, and action taken in the file.
  
**800.AK** Coordinate with a producer or representative without delegating carrier decision authority. Issue the carrier notice through approved processing.
  
Producers and representatives may provide information but do not control carrier action. Carrier-issued notice preserves procedural consistency.
  
Document the communication received and the carrier action taken.
  
**800.AL** Do not disclose adverse action details to an unauthorized party. Confirm authorization before discussing policy status or notice content.
  
Policy information must be handled according to carrier privacy controls. Unauthorized disclosure may create privacy exposure.
  
Document the authorization reviewed and the information disclosed, if any.
  
**800.AM** Preserve the notice image and all related correspondence in the policy file. Ensure that the stored record is readable and complete.
  
Complete records support later review of carrier handling. Missing or unreadable records impair complaint response and audit work.
  
Document the storage location and record quality review in the file.
  
**800.AN** Preserve system-generated evidence of notice issuance. Retain available mailing, transmission, and processing records.
  
System evidence supports the carrier’s account of notice handling. It may be needed for complaint, audit, or dispute review.
  
Document the system record identifier and the issuance evidence retained.
  
**800.AO** Record the person or unit that approved the adverse action. Do not leave approval fields incomplete.
  
Approval identification establishes accountability for the carrier decision. Incomplete records prevent effective review.
  
Document the approver, approval event, and scope of the authorization in the file.
  
**800.AP** Use escalation when an adverse action involves unusual facts or uncertain authority. Do not create local handling outside approved controls.
  
Unusual situations may require broader underwriting or management judgment. Escalation reduces inconsistent practice.
  
Document the issue, escalation recipient, and authorized direction in the file.
  
**800.AQ** Review jurisdictional requirements before issuing an adverse action. Refer jurisdictional uncertainty to designated compliance support.
  
Requirements may vary by policy location and action type. Jurisdictional review helps prevent improper notice handling.
  
Document the jurisdiction reviewed and the compliance direction received.
  
**800.AR** Do not issue notice until required review is complete. Hold the action when required information or approval is missing.
  
Incomplete review can result in inaccurate notice or unsupported action. A documented hold preserves control of the process.
  
Document the hold reason, pending requirement, and release authorization in the file.
  
**800.AS** Verify the proposed effective event before finalizing notice content. Ensure that all system records reflect the same action event.
  
Conflicting effective events create uncertainty regarding coverage status. Alignment supports accurate policy administration.
  
Document the effective event reviewed and the system records confirmed.
  
**800.AT** Correct clerical errors in adverse action records promptly. Refer any correction that could change the carrier decision.
  
Clerical correction differs from a change in underwriting judgment. Material changes require authorized review.
  
Document the error, correction made, and approval for any material change.
  
**800.AU** Do not backdate an adverse action without authorized underwriting and compliance approval. Refer any request for retroactive handling.
  
Backdating may affect coverage, claims, and customer rights. Controlled review is required before any retroactive action.
  
Document the request, supporting facts, and authorized disposition in the file.
  
**800.AV** Review open claim activity before finalizing cancellation or nonrenewal. Coordinate with claims when the proposed action may affect claim handling.
  
Policy action and claim handling require consistent carrier records. Coordination prevents inaccurate communications about coverage status.
  
Document the claim review, coordination activity, and final action direction.
  
**800.AW** Do not alter claim records to support an underwriting action. Use claim information as recorded and refer discrepancies for claims review.
  
Claims records must remain accurate and independently maintained. Altered records compromise carrier controls.
  
Document the discrepancy, claims referral, and underwriting handling in the file.
  
**800.AX** Confirm that a policy transfer does not bypass an active adverse action. Refer transfer requests involving pending cancellation or nonrenewal.
  
A transfer may change administrative records without resolving the underlying underwriting issue. Referral preserves the intended carrier control.
  
Document the transfer request, action status, and authorized disposition.
  
**800.AY** Review ownership, occupancy, and location changes before withdrawing an adverse action. Do not assume that a changed record resolves the stated concern.
  
Changed risk information may require a new underwriting assessment. Assumptions may leave material risk conditions unreviewed.
  
Document the changed information, underwriting review, and resulting decision.
  
**800.AZ** Apply underwriting authority limits before approving an exception to adverse action procedures. Refer matters beyond assigned authority.
  
Authority limits maintain consistent risk acceptance and governance. Unauthorized exceptions may create unapproved exposure.
  
Document the assigned authority, exception request, and approval record in the file.
  
**800.BA** Use clear and neutral language in all adverse action communications. Do not include opinions, speculation, or unnecessary commentary.
  
Neutral communication reduces misunderstanding and complaint risk. Notice content must reflect the approved carrier reason.
  
Document the communication reviewed and the approved language used.
  
**800.BB** Direct customer questions about adverse action to an authorized servicing channel. Do not provide coverage opinions outside assigned authority.
  
Authorized handling supports accurate and consistent customer responses. Informal explanations may conflict with the official notice.
  
Document the inquiry, referral, and response provided in the file.
  
**800.BC** Refer threatened complaints, legal demands, or regulatory inquiries involving adverse action immediately. Preserve all related records without alteration.
  
Escalated matters may require coordinated carrier response. Record preservation supports accurate review and response.
  
Document the inquiry, referral event, and records preserved.
  
**800.BD** Do not delete, overwrite, or obscure adverse action records. Preserve the original record and add corrective documentation when needed.
  
Original records establish the history of carrier handling. Record alteration may impair audit or dispute review.
  
Document the corrective entry and the reason for the correction.
  
**800.BE** Review system exceptions that prevent notice generation or delivery. Resolve the exception before allowing the action to proceed.
  
System exceptions may interrupt required processing or create incomplete records. Resolution supports reliable notice administration.
  
Document the exception, corrective action, and confirmation of completed processing.
  
**800.BF** Monitor queued adverse actions for duplicate processing. Stop duplicate notices and confirm the controlling action record.
  
Duplicate notices may confuse customers and create inconsistent policy status. Queue review supports orderly processing.
  
Document the duplicate review, stopped transaction, and controlling record.
  
**800.BG** Confirm that policy records reflect the final cancellation or nonrenewal disposition. Reconcile discrepancies between underwriting, billing, and policy systems.
  
Aligned records support accurate customer service and claim handling. Unreconciled systems may show conflicting coverage status.
  
Document the systems reviewed, discrepancies found, and reconciliation completed.
  
**800.BH** Perform quality review of adverse action files as assigned. Refer recurring errors or control failures to underwriting management.
  
Quality review identifies process weaknesses before they become broader operational issues. Recurring errors require management attention.
  
Document the quality review result, identified issue, and referral activity.
  
**800.BI** Close the adverse action file only after required notice, approval, and record retention activity is complete. Maintain the file for authorized audit and review.
  
Complete closure confirms that the carrier action is supported by a usable record. Premature closure may leave required work unresolved.
  
Document the closure review, completed requirements, and final file status.

## Rule 900 — Appendix: Referral Matrix

**900.A** Refer any reported loss exceeding $25,000 to the designated claims authority. Do not approve settlement activity outside delegated authority.

This control preserves required claim oversight. It also prevents unauthorized loss disposition.

Document the referral in the claim file. Attach the available loss evaluation.

**900.B** Refer any new business risk with Coverage A above $1,500,000 to senior underwriting. Do not issue binding confirmation before authority is recorded.

This control maintains adherence to underwriting authority. It also directs higher-value exposures for review.

Document the requested Coverage A amount in the file. Record the senior underwriting disposition.

**900.C** Obtain loss history covering 3 years before completing an eligibility decision. Refer incomplete loss history for underwriting direction.

This control supports consistent loss evaluation. It also identifies adverse experience requiring review.

Document the loss history source in the file. Record any unavailable information and the disposition.

**900.D** Refer any risk with roof age at or beyond 25 years for declination processing. Do not bind the risk unless an authorized exception is recorded.

This control maintains property condition standards. It also prevents acceptance of risks outside appetite.

Document the roof age support in the file. Record the declination or authorized exception.

**900.E** Refer any request for water backup coverage above $25,000 to underwriting authority. Do not quote the requested limit as available pending review.

This control maintains control of water-related exposure. It also ensures consistent limit selection.

Document the requested water backup limit in the file. Record the referral response.

**900.F** Refer any risk with material discrepancies between the application and inspection information. Do not resolve material discrepancies without underwriting direction.

This control protects rating and eligibility integrity. It also prevents reliance on unsupported risk information.

Document the discrepancy in the file. Attach the supporting inspection material.

**900.G** Refer any risk with unrepaired property damage. Do not bind coverage until repair status is acceptable or an authorized exception is recorded.

This control limits exposure to known conditions. It also supports accurate property assessment.

Document the observed damage in the file. Record repair evidence or the authorized exception.

**900.H** Refer any risk with evidence of vacancy. Do not continue standard underwriting handling until occupancy is confirmed.

This control identifies elevated property exposure. It also prevents incorrect occupancy classification.

Document the occupancy evidence in the file. Record the underwriting disposition.

**900.I** Refer any risk with evidence of unoccupied premises. Do not rely on applicant statements alone where other information conflicts.

This control supports dependable occupancy review. It also identifies conditions requiring specialized handling.

Document all occupancy information in the file. Record the source of conflicting information.

**900.J** Refer any risk with indications of business activity at the premises. Do not bind personal lines coverage until the activity is reviewed.

This control prevents acceptance of commercial exposure under personal lines authority. It also supports proper classification.

Document the business activity indicators in the file. Record the underwriting decision.

**900.K** Refer any risk with rental activity not clearly described in the submission. Do not assume owner occupancy when rental indicators are present.

This control supports correct occupancy classification. It also identifies liability and property exposure requiring review.

Document the rental information in the file. Attach available lease or listing evidence.

**900.L** Refer any risk with short-term rental exposure. Do not bind coverage before underwriting confirms eligibility.

This control addresses changing occupancy and liability conditions. It also supports consistent treatment of rental exposure.

Document the rental platform or listing information in the file. Record the underwriting response.

**900.M** Refer any risk with an applicant-provided valuation that conflicts with replacement cost information. Do not alter valuation assumptions without support.

This control protects adequacy of insurance to value. It also reduces underwriting error.

Document the valuation conflict in the file. Attach the supporting replacement cost information.

**900.N** Refer any risk with signs of deferred maintenance. Do not rely on favorable exterior appearance where maintenance concerns remain.

This control identifies property condition concerns. It also supports sound eligibility decisions.

Document the maintenance concerns in the file. Attach photographs when available.

**900.O** Refer any risk with structural movement, cracking, or settlement indicators. Do not bind the risk pending underwriting review.

This control identifies potential severity exposure. It also avoids acceptance without adequate assessment.

Document the observed indicators in the file. Attach inspection information when available.

**900.P** Refer any risk with a swimming pool, spa, or similar water feature lacking acceptable safeguards. Do not issue coverage confirmation before review.

This control manages premises liability exposure. It also confirms appropriate risk controls.

Document the water feature details in the file. Record the safeguard information reviewed.

**900.Q** Refer any risk with an animal exposure that is unclear or inconsistent. Do not assume eligibility based on incomplete animal information.

This control supports liability exposure evaluation. It also prevents unsupported underwriting decisions.

Document the animal information in the file. Record the source of the information.

**900.R** Refer any risk with a prior cancellation, nonrenewal, or declined application. Do not proceed without understanding the stated reason.

This control identifies adverse underwriting history. It also supports consistent eligibility review.

Document the prior action and stated reason in the file. Record the underwriting disposition.

**900.S** Refer any risk with material omissions in the application. Do not complete binding activity until the missing information is obtained or authority directs otherwise.

This control preserves application quality. It also prevents decisions based on incomplete facts.

Document the missing information in the file. Record the follow-up activity.

**900.T** Refer any risk with conflicting applicant identity information. Do not issue coverage until identity concerns are resolved.

This control supports accurate account handling. It also reduces potential misrepresentation exposure.

Document the conflicting information in the file. Record the resolution or referral outcome.

**900.U** Refer any risk where prior insurance information cannot be verified. Do not treat unverified coverage history as confirmed.

This control supports dependable eligibility decisions. It also identifies possible lapse or cancellation concerns.

Document the verification effort in the file. Record the information received.

**900.V** Refer any risk with a lapse in prior coverage that is not adequately explained. Do not waive the concern without underwriting authority.

This control identifies possible adverse loss exposure. It also supports consistent treatment of coverage gaps.

Document the stated reason for the lapse in the file. Record the authority decision.

**900.W** Refer any risk with a loss cause that remains unclear. Do not classify an unclear loss without supporting information.

This control supports accurate loss analysis. It also prevents incorrect application of underwriting criteria.

Document the available loss details in the file. Record the reason the cause remains unclear.

**900.X** Refer any risk with repeated water-related damage indicators. Do not approve expanded water coverage without underwriting review.

This control addresses recurring property loss exposure. It also supports consistent water risk selection.

Document the water damage information in the file. Attach available repair or mitigation evidence.

**900.Y** Refer any risk with prior fire, smoke, or heat damage. Do not assume repairs eliminate underwriting concern.

This control identifies potential recurrence and condition exposure. It also requires review of completed remediation.

Document the loss details in the file. Attach repair or restoration information when available.

**900.Z** Refer any risk with theft, vandalism, or malicious damage history. Do not proceed without reviewing the reported circumstances.

This control identifies security and location concerns. It also supports consistent loss assessment.

Document the reported circumstances in the file. Record any security information obtained.

**900.AA** Refer any risk with unrepaired electrical, plumbing, heating, or cooling concerns. Do not bind coverage based solely on applicant assurances.

This control identifies potential property damage exposure. It also requires reliable condition support.

Document the system concern in the file. Attach available repair evidence.

**900.AB** Refer any risk with protective devices that are represented but cannot be supported. Do not apply related underwriting treatment without verification.

This control protects rating and eligibility accuracy. It also prevents reliance on unsupported safeguards.

Document the device information in the file. Record the verification source.

**900.AC** Refer any risk with an alarm, monitoring, or detection feature that appears inactive. Do not assume the feature provides protection.

This control addresses possible property and liability exposure. It also supports accurate risk classification.

Document the feature status in the file. Record the supporting information.

**900.AD** Refer any risk with an adverse inspection recommendation. Do not close the underwriting review until the recommendation is addressed.

This control ensures inspection concerns receive appropriate attention. It also supports defensible file handling.

Document the inspection recommendation in the file. Record the action taken.

**900.AE** Refer any risk where requested coverage differs materially from the submitted exposure information. Do not revise coverage without confirming the underlying facts.

This control preserves accurate coverage placement. It also reduces the risk of mismatched protection.

Document the requested coverage change in the file. Record the confirmed exposure information.

**900.AF** Refer any risk with an unusual construction feature. Do not classify the feature without reliable supporting information.

This control identifies property characteristics outside routine handling. It also supports proper risk evaluation.

Document the construction feature in the file. Attach available photographs or inspection information.

**900.AG** Refer any risk with a detached structure used for purposes not clearly described. Do not assume ordinary private use.

This control identifies possible occupancy and liability concerns. It also supports correct property classification.

Document the structure use in the file. Record the underwriting disposition.

**900.AH** Refer any risk with land, water, or access conditions that may affect emergency response. Do not disregard access concerns in property evaluation.

This control identifies potential loss severity concerns. It also supports appropriate risk selection.

Document the access condition in the file. Attach available location information.

**900.AI** Refer any risk with evidence of prior mitigation work that cannot be verified. Do not give underwriting credit for unsupported mitigation.

This control maintains consistent treatment of protective improvements. It also prevents reliance on incomplete information.

Document the mitigation representation in the file. Record the verification result.

**900.AJ** Refer any risk with a requested exception to an underwriting requirement. Do not grant an exception without recorded authority.

This control preserves delegated authority standards. It also supports consistent exception handling.

Document the requested exception in the file. Record the approving authority and rationale.

**900.AK** Refer any risk with a material change identified after binding activity. Do not leave the file unchanged when the change affects eligibility or rating.

This control supports timely correction of underwriting records. It also prevents continuation of inaccurate terms.

Document the material change in the file. Record the corrective action.

**900.AL** Refer any risk where available information does not support a clear eligibility decision. Do not bind the risk based on assumption or incomplete interpretation.

This control provides an escalation path for uncertain risks. It also supports disciplined underwriting judgment.

Document the information reviewed in the file. Record the referral outcome.
